# QA-PG-001 — QAPI Program Establishment & Governance
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PG-001 |
| Title | QAPI Program Establishment & Governance |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PG — Program Governance |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement (QAPI) program as a formalized, data-driven, and governing body–directed system for continuous quality monitoring, performance improvement, and patient safety assurance at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The QAPI program is the central mechanism through which the agency identifies opportunities for improvement, implements evidence-based interventions, monitors outcomes, and sustains gains across all operational and clinical domains. This policy ensures the agency satisfies the requirements set forth in 42 CFR § 484.65 — Condition of Participation: Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement, which mandates that a home health agency must develop, implement, evaluate, and maintain an effective, ongoing, agency-wide, data-driven QAPI program.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The QAPI Coordinator (designated program lead)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All members of the QAPI Committee
The Compliance Officer
All clinical, administrative, and support staff whose work contributes to or is measured by the QAPI program
All contracted and per diem staff providing services on behalf of the agency
Exclusion: This policy does not govern the detailed methodology of individual Performance Improvement Projects (PIPs), which is addressed in QA-PI-001, nor adverse event investigation procedures, which are addressed in QA-AE-001 and QA-AE-002. This policy establishes the governance framework within which those activities operate.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall establish, maintain, and continuously operate an agency-wide QAPI program that reflects the complexity of the agency's organization and services, as required by 42 CFR § 484.65(a).
4.2 The QAPI program shall be governed by the Governing Body, which retains ultimate accountability for the program's effectiveness, resource allocation, and strategic direction. The Governing Body shall not delegate this ultimate accountability.
4.3 The Governing Body shall designate a qualified QAPI Coordinator with defined authority, dedicated time, and sufficient resources to administer the QAPI program on a day-to-day basis.
4.4 The QAPI program shall be data-driven, utilizing measurable indicators derived from clinical outcomes, patient safety events, operational performance, patient experience, regulatory compliance, and financial data to identify priorities and evaluate improvement interventions.
4.5 The QAPI program shall integrate and coordinate with the agency's compliance program (CO-CP-001), risk management program (RM-ER-001), infection prevention and control program (CL-SD-016), and all clinical and operational domains to ensure a unified quality and safety framework.
4.6 The QAPI program shall maintain a written QAPI plan, approved annually by the Governing Body per policy QA-PG-002, that defines the program's goals, measurable indicators, performance improvement project requirements, committee structure, reporting schedule, and accountability framework.
4.7 All agency staff — clinical, administrative, and contracted — are responsible for participating in quality improvement activities within their scope of practice and role, and for reporting quality concerns, adverse events, and near-misses through established channels.
4.8 The QAPI program shall comply with all applicable CMS Conditions of Participation, state regulatory requirements, and accreditation standards. The agency shall not operate any service line or clinical program outside the scope of QAPI monitoring.
4.9 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid. Superseded versions must not be used for any operational or compliance purpose. Any revision to this policy requires Governing Body approval and re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| QAPI | Quality Assessment and Performance Improvement — the structured, data-driven program required by 42 CFR § 484.65 for ongoing quality monitoring, performance improvement, and patient safety assurance. |
| QAPI Coordinator | The individual designated by the Governing Body with operational authority and accountability for administering the agency's QAPI program on a day-to-day basis. Must be a licensed healthcare professional or individual with demonstrated quality management expertise. |
| QAPI Committee | The multidisciplinary committee responsible for reviewing quality data, directing performance improvement projects, monitoring corrective actions, and reporting to the Governing Body. |
| Quality Indicator | A quantifiable measure used to assess agency performance in a defined area of clinical care, operational efficiency, patient safety, or patient experience. |
| Performance Improvement Project (PIP) | A structured, time-limited initiative designed to achieve measurable improvement in a specific area identified through data analysis, per policy QA-PI-001. |
| Adverse Event | An undesirable clinical outcome or occurrence that results in, or has the potential to result in, harm to a patient, per policy QA-AE-001. |
| Near-Miss | An event or circumstance that had the potential to cause harm but did not reach the patient due to timely intervention or chance. |
| Benchmark | A reference point or standard against which agency performance is measured, typically derived from national data sets (e.g., CMS Home Health Compare, HHCAHPS national averages). |
| Root Cause Analysis (RCA) | A systematic investigative process used to identify the fundamental cause(s) of an adverse event or systemic failure, per policy QA-AE-002. |
| Corrective Action Plan (CAP) | A structured, time-bound plan developed to address the root cause(s) of identified deficiencies and prevent recurrence, per policy QA-AE-003. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 QAPI Program Establishment

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Governing Body | Formally establish the QAPI program through a resolution or directive documented in Governing Body minutes. The establishing action must identify: (a) the program's scope (agency-wide); (b) the designated QAPI Coordinator; (c) authorization for the QAPI Committee; (d) the initial resource allocation including dedicated QAPI Coordinator time. | Prior to initial Medicare certification and maintained continuously thereafter. |
| 6.1.2 | Governing Body | Designate a qualified QAPI Coordinator. The QAPI Coordinator must be: (a) a licensed healthcare professional (RN, PT, OT, SLP, MSW) or an individual with documented quality management certification or equivalent experience; (b) granted authority to access all agency data systems, clinical records, and operational reports necessary to perform QAPI functions; (c) allocated a minimum of [agency-defined] hours per week dedicated to QAPI activities. Document designation in Governing Body minutes. | Prior to agency operation; within 30 calendar days of any vacancy. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Develop and maintain a written QAPI Program Description document that includes: (a) program mission and objectives; (b) organizational placement and reporting relationships; (c) committee structure and membership; (d) data sources and quality indicator definitions; (e) PIP methodology; (f) adverse event and patient safety reporting process; (g) integration points with compliance, risk management, and infection control programs; (h) staff responsibilities for quality participation. | Completed within 60 calendar days of program establishment; updated annually or upon any structural change. |
| 6.1.4 | Administrator | Ensure QAPI program is resourced with: (a) data analysis tools and/or software access; (b) staff time for data collection, chart review, and committee participation; (c) training resources for quality improvement methodology; (d) budget allocation for program operations. | Annually during budget process; verified at each QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.2 QAPI Committee Structure and Function

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish and maintain the QAPI Committee with the following minimum composition: (a) QAPI Coordinator (Chair); (b) Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager; (c) Administrator or designee; (d) at least one representative from each active clinical discipline (nursing, therapy, aide services); (e) Compliance Officer or designee (standing invitation). Additional members may include intake, scheduling, billing, and HR representatives as needed for specific agenda items. | Committee established within 30 calendar days of program establishment; membership roster updated within 7 calendar days of any change. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Convene QAPI Committee meetings no fewer than monthly. Meeting schedule for the upcoming quarter must be established and distributed to all members at least 14 calendar days before the start of the quarter. | Monthly; schedule distributed quarterly. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Prepare and distribute the meeting agenda to all QAPI Committee members no fewer than 5 business days before each meeting. The agenda must include standing items: (a) review of prior meeting minutes and action items; (b) quality indicator dashboard review; (c) active PIP status updates; (d) adverse event and incident summary; (e) infection surveillance summary; (f) patient satisfaction data; (g) regulatory/survey update; (h) new business. | 5 business days before each meeting. |
| 6.2.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Record formal minutes for each QAPI Committee meeting. Minutes must document: (a) date, time, and attendees; (b) each agenda item discussed with key findings; (c) all decisions and directives issued; (d) assigned responsible parties and deadlines for each action item; (e) follow-up items carried forward from prior meetings. | Draft minutes completed within 7 calendar days of the meeting; distributed to all committee members and filed in QAPI records. |
| 6.2.5 | QAPI Committee | Review and act upon the quality indicator dashboard at each meeting. For any indicator that falls below the defined performance threshold for 2 consecutive reporting periods: (a) determine whether a root cause analysis is required; (b) assign a responsible party for investigation; (c) establish a deadline for findings and recommended action; (d) document the committee's analysis and decision in meeting minutes. | At each monthly meeting. |
| 6.2.6 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain a QAPI Action Item Tracker that documents all open and closed action items, including: assigned party, deadline, current status, completion date, and outcome. Present the tracker at each committee meeting. | Updated continuously; presented monthly. |

### 6.3 Governing Body QAPI Oversight

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Prepare and submit a written QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body no fewer than 7 calendar days before each quarterly Governing Body meeting. The report must include: (a) quality indicator trends with comparison to prior quarter and national benchmarks; (b) status of all active PIPs including progress against measurable goals; (c) adverse event summary with root cause analysis findings; (d) patient satisfaction data and HHCAHPS trends; (e) Star Rating / Home Health Compare trends; (f) infection surveillance summary; (g) open corrective action plans with status; (h) recommendations for Governing Body action. | 7 calendar days before each quarterly Governing Body meeting. |
| 6.3.2 | Governing Body | Review, discuss, and document its response to the quarterly QAPI Performance Report per policy GV-GB-001, Section 6.2.4. If any quality indicator falls below threshold for 2 consecutive quarters, the Governing Body shall direct corrective action, assign accountability, and establish a resolution deadline. | At each quarterly meeting; corrective directives within 14 calendar days if thresholds are breached. |
| 6.3.3 | Governing Body | Review and approve the annual QAPI plan per policy QA-PG-002 at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year. | Annually at Q1 meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Report any patient safety event classified as a sentinel event or any adverse event resulting in patient death, hospitalization, or permanent harm to the Governing Body within 72 hours of agency awareness. Interim report may be verbal with written follow-up within 7 calendar days. | Within 72 hours of agency awareness; written follow-up within 7 calendar days. |

### 6.4 QAPI Program Integration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | Establish a defined coordination protocol between the QAPI program and the Compliance program (CO-CP-001) to ensure: (a) compliance audit findings that indicate quality or patient safety implications are referred to the QAPI Committee; (b) QAPI findings that indicate potential regulatory non-compliance are referred to the Compliance Officer; (c) duplicative investigation is avoided through joint review when appropriate. | Protocol established within 60 days of program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator / Risk Manager | Establish a defined coordination protocol between the QAPI program and the Risk Management program (RM-ER-001) to ensure: (a) incident reports are routed to the QAPI Committee for trending and pattern analysis; (b) risk assessment findings inform QAPI priority-setting; (c) corrective action plans developed under either program are tracked centrally. | Protocol established within 60 days of program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator / Director of Nursing | Ensure the QAPI program encompasses all clinical service lines including: (a) skilled nursing; (b) physical therapy; (c) occupational therapy; (d) speech-language pathology; (e) medical social work; (f) home health aide services; (g) any specialty programs (telehealth, infusion, wound care). No clinical program shall operate outside QAPI monitoring. | Ongoing; verified annually during QAPI plan review. |
| 6.4.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain a QAPI Data Source Inventory documenting all data inputs to the program including: source system, data type, frequency of collection, responsible party, and quality indicator(s) derived. Data sources must include at minimum: (a) OASIS outcome data; (b) claims/billing data; (c) incident/adverse event reports; (d) patient satisfaction surveys/HHCAHPS; (e) infection surveillance data; (f) staff competency records; (g) Home Health Compare / Star Rating data; (h) documentation audit results; (i) compliance audit findings. | Inventory established within 90 days of program establishment; updated annually or when data sources change. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QAPI Committee fails to meet for 2 consecutive months | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and Compliance Officer in writing. | Administrator directs immediate scheduling of a meeting. If pattern persists for a third month, Compliance Officer documents the deficiency and initiates corrective action per QA-AE-003. The deficiency is reported to the Governing Body at the next quarterly meeting. | Make-up meeting within 14 calendar days of missed month. |
| QAPI Coordinator position vacant for more than 30 calendar days | Administrator escalates to Governing Body. | Governing Body designates an interim QAPI Coordinator within 14 calendar days of vacancy. Permanent appointment within 90 calendar days. Interim designee must meet minimum qualifications in Section 6.1.2. | Interim: 14 days. Permanent: 90 days. |
| Quality indicator falls below defined threshold for 3 consecutive reporting periods without documented corrective action | QAPI Coordinator escalates to Governing Body via special written report. | Governing Body directs immediate development of a corrective action plan per QA-AE-003 with a designated lead, measurable goals, and resolution deadline not to exceed 90 calendar days. Governing Body receives monthly status updates until resolved. | CAP initiated within 14 calendar days; resolved within 90 days. |
| CMS survey identifies QAPI-related deficiency | Administrator convenes QAPI Committee within 48 hours of survey exit. | QAPI Committee develops Plan of Correction within CMS-required timeframe (typically 10 calendar days). Governing Body receives immediate notification and weekly status updates until resolution confirmed. | Committee convened within 48 hours; POC per CMS deadline. |
| Staff non-participation in QAPI activities (failure to report events, submit data, or attend required meetings) | Department supervisor documents non-participation and reports to QAPI Coordinator. | QAPI Coordinator notifies the employee's supervisor. Repeated non-participation (3 or more instances within a 12-month period) is addressed through progressive discipline per HR-ER-002. | Notification within 5 business days of identified non-participation. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| QAPI program is formally established and documented. | Review of Governing Body minutes; QAPI Program Description document. | Current establishing documentation and Program Description on file at all times. |
| QAPI Coordinator is designated with documented qualifications and authority. | Review of designation documentation; personnel file; Governing Body minutes. | Current designation on file; no vacancy exceeds 30 days without interim designee. |
| QAPI Committee meets at least monthly. | Review of meeting minutes with dates and attendance. | 12 or more meetings per calendar year with documented attendance. |
| QAPI Committee meeting minutes are complete and timely. | Review of minutes for required content per Section 6.2.4. | All required elements documented; drafts completed within 7 calendar days. |
| Quarterly QAPI Performance Reports are submitted to the Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body agendas and minutes; QAPI report files. | Reports submitted 7 days before each quarterly meeting; Governing Body discussion documented. |
| Quality indicators are monitored and acted upon. | Review of QAPI dashboard; meeting minutes documenting analysis and action for adverse trends. | All defined indicators monitored monthly; corrective action documented for any indicator below threshold for 2 consecutive periods. |
| QAPI program integrates with compliance and risk management. | Review of coordination protocols; evidence of cross-referral in meeting minutes. | Written protocols on file; cross-referral documented at least quarterly. |
| All service lines are included in QAPI monitoring. | Review of QAPI Data Source Inventory; QAPI plan scope. | No clinical service line operates outside QAPI monitoring. |
| Policy acknowledgments are current. | Review of signed acknowledgment forms. | 100% acknowledgment within 14 calendar days of effective date or new assignment. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under SOM Appendix B will specifically verify:
Evidence that a QAPI program exists and functions. Surveyors will request the QAPI plan, QAPI Program Description, and committee meeting minutes. An agency that cannot produce these documents will face an immediate Condition-level inquiry under 42 CFR § 484.65.
Evidence that the QAPI program is agency-wide. Surveyors will verify that all service lines — not just nursing — are monitored by the QAPI program. A program limited to a single discipline will be cited as non-compliant.
Evidence that the QAPI program is data-driven. Surveyors will look for measurable indicators, dashboards, trending data, and documented analysis. Programs that rely solely on anecdotal discussion without data will be cited.
Evidence that the Governing Body exercises oversight. Surveyors will cross-reference QAPI Committee reports with Governing Body minutes to verify that the Governing Body receives, reviews, discusses, and acts upon quality data. Passive receipt of reports without documented discussion or action is a common deficiency.
Evidence that performance improvement projects exist. Surveyors will request documentation of active and completed PIPs including methodology, data, and results. An agency with no active PIPs will be cited.
Evidence that adverse events are identified, reported, and acted upon. Surveyors will review the adverse event reporting process, investigation records, and corrective action documentation.
Evidence that the QAPI program leads to measurable improvement. Surveyors will look for documented improvement trends, not just identification of problems. A program that identifies issues but shows no improvement over time will be cited for ineffectiveness.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| QAPI program exists on paper but QAPI Committee does not meet regularly or meeting minutes lack substance. | Condition-level deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.65. Surveyors will treat undocumented meetings as not having occurred. | Enforce monthly meeting schedule; use standardized agenda; require minutes within 7 calendar days. |
| QAPI program monitors only nursing — therapy, aide, and other disciplines are excluded. | Surveyor will cite failure to maintain an agency-wide program. | Include all disciplines in QAPI Committee composition and data monitoring. |
| Quality indicators are tracked but no action is taken when performance deteriorates. | Surveyor will cite passive monitoring without improvement action. | Require documented corrective action for any indicator below threshold for 2 consecutive periods. |
| Governing Body receives QAPI reports but minutes show no discussion or directives. | Surveyor will cite failure of Governing Body oversight of QAPI. | Document specific Governing Body discussion, questions, and directives in meeting minutes per GV-GB-001. |
| No active Performance Improvement Projects. | Surveyor will cite failure to implement PIPs as required by 42 CFR § 484.65(c). | Maintain at least 2 active PIPs at all times per QA-PI-001. |
| QAPI Coordinator has no dedicated time or authority; role is nominal. | Surveyor will determine QAPI program is not effectively implemented. | Governing Body must allocate defined hours and documented authority per Section 6.1.2. |

### 9.2 CMS Guidance
CMS State Operations Manual, Appendix B — Guidance to Surveyors: Provides interpretive guidelines for survey of 42 CFR § 484.65 compliance; defines surveyor expectations for QAPI program evidence, data use, and governing body involvement.
CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual: OASIS outcome data serves as a primary data source for the QAPI program.
CMS Home Health Quality Reporting Program (HH QRP): Defines quality measures reported publicly on Home Health Compare; QAPI program must monitor these measures.
### 9.3 OIG Guidance
OIG Compliance Program Guidance for Home Health Agencies: Establishes expectation that quality monitoring is integrated with the compliance program and that the governing body receives regular quality performance reports.

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All QAPI Committee members shall receive orientation to this policy and the QAPI program structure within 14 calendar days of appointment to the committee. Orientation shall be conducted by the QAPI Coordinator and must cover: (a) QAPI program purpose and regulatory basis; (b) committee member responsibilities; (c) quality indicator definitions and dashboard interpretation; (d) PIP methodology overview; (e) adverse event reporting and escalation; (f) documentation expectations; (g) CMS survey expectations for QAPI evidence.
10.2 All agency staff (clinical, administrative, and contracted) shall receive QAPI awareness training during initial orientation per HR-TA-005 and annually thereafter per HR-TD-001. Training must cover: (a) the purpose and structure of the QAPI program; (b) staff responsibility for reporting quality concerns, adverse events, and near-misses; (c) how to report through established channels; (d) the non-punitive culture of quality reporting; (e) how QAPI data is used to improve care.
10.3 All personnel within scope of this policy shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
10.4 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a training tracking log documenting all QAPI-related training including: attendee name, training topic, date, trainer, and completion status. Training completion rates shall be reported to the Governing Body as part of the quarterly QAPI Performance Report.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.

## 2. Purpose
This policy mandates the development, content requirements, approval process, and annual review of a written QAPI plan for Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The QAPI plan is the agency's actionable roadmap for quality improvement — translating the governance structure established in QA-PG-001 into defined goals, measurable indicators, performance improvement priorities, committee responsibilities, and reporting timelines for each calendar year. This policy ensures the agency satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65(a) and (e), which require the governing body to ensure the QAPI program has a written plan with defined goals and measurable outcomes, and that the governing body approves the plan and ensures adequate resources for its execution.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.
The Agency Administrator
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All QAPI Committee members
The Compliance Officer
All department heads and supervisors responsible for achieving QAPI plan goals
Exclusion: This policy defines QAPI plan content, approval, and review requirements. It does not govern the execution of individual PIPs (QA-PI-001) or specific quality indicator monitoring procedures (QA-PI-002), though the QAPI plan must define priorities for both.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a written QAPI plan that is reviewed, updated, and approved by the Governing Body annually, at the first quarterly meeting of each calendar year.
4.2 The QAPI plan shall define the agency's quality improvement priorities for the plan year based on data analysis of the prior year's performance, regulatory requirements, patient population characteristics, and identified areas of risk.
4.3 The QAPI plan shall include measurable goals with defined performance thresholds, responsible parties, monitoring frequency, and expected outcomes for each priority area.
4.4 The QAPI plan shall identify a minimum of two (2) active Performance Improvement Projects (PIPs) at all times, proportionate to the scope and complexity of the agency's services, as required by 42 CFR § 484.65(d).
4.5 The QAPI plan shall not be treated as a static document. The QAPI Coordinator shall update the plan as needed throughout the year in response to: (a) new adverse event trends; (b) significant regulatory changes; (c) CMS survey findings; (d) Governing Body directives. All mid-year modifications must be documented, approved by the QAPI Committee, and reported to the Governing Body at the next quarterly meeting.
4.6 The QAPI plan year shall align with the calendar year (January 1 through December 31). The plan for the upcoming year must be finalized and submitted to the Governing Body for approval no later than December 15 of the preceding year.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| QAPI Plan | The written annual document that defines the agency's quality improvement priorities, measurable goals, indicator thresholds, PIP selections, committee responsibilities, data sources, monitoring schedule, and reporting timeline for the plan year. |
| Plan Year | The 12-month period covered by the QAPI plan, aligned with the calendar year (January 1 – December 31). |
| Quality Priority Area | A specific domain of agency performance selected for focused monitoring and improvement based on data analysis, risk assessment, or regulatory requirement. |
| Performance Threshold | The minimum acceptable level of performance for a quality indicator, below which corrective action is triggered. Thresholds may be based on national benchmarks, prior agency performance, or regulatory standards. |
| Mid-Year Plan Modification | Any change to the approved QAPI plan content made after Governing Body approval, including addition or modification of priorities, goals, indicators, or PIPs. |
| Annual QAPI Evaluation | The formal year-end assessment of the QAPI plan's effectiveness, including achievement of goals, PIP outcomes, and identified lessons learned, which informs the subsequent plan year. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Annual QAPI Plan Development

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Initiate the annual QAPI plan development process by conducting a comprehensive year-end quality assessment that includes: (a) review of all quality indicator trends for the current plan year; (b) analysis of PIP outcomes and sustainability; (c) review of adverse event and incident data; (d) review of patient satisfaction and HHCAHPS results; (e) review of Star Rating trends and Home Health Compare data; (f) review of infection surveillance data; (g) review of compliance audit findings and CMS survey results; (h) review of staff competency evaluation results; (i) feedback from QAPI Committee members and department heads; (j) assessment of new or changing regulatory requirements. | Begin no later than October 1 of each year; year-end assessment completed by November 30. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Based on the year-end assessment, identify and prioritize quality improvement focus areas for the upcoming plan year. Selection criteria must include: (a) areas where performance is below national benchmarks or internal thresholds; (b) areas with adverse event patterns; (c) areas where regulatory changes require focused attention; (d) areas identified by the Governing Body or Administrator as strategic priorities; (e) areas where prior PIPs require sustainment monitoring. Present proposed priorities to the QAPI Committee for discussion and input. | Draft priorities presented to QAPI Committee by November meeting. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Develop the draft QAPI plan for the upcoming year. The plan must contain all elements specified in Section 6.2 (Required Plan Content). | Draft completed by December 1. |
| 6.1.4 | QAPI Committee | Review the draft QAPI plan at the December meeting. Provide feedback, request modifications, and approve the final draft for submission to the Governing Body. | December QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.1.5 | QAPI Coordinator | Submit the final QAPI plan to the Governing Body no later than December 15, along with a written summary of the year-end assessment findings and the rationale for selected priorities. | Submitted by December 15. |
| 6.1.6 | Governing Body | Review and approve the QAPI plan at the first quarterly meeting of the plan year. Approval must be documented in Governing Body meeting minutes and must include: (a) confirmation that the plan reflects the agency's scope and complexity; (b) confirmation that adequate resources are allocated; (c) any modifications directed by the Governing Body. | Q1 Governing Body meeting (per GV-GB-001 §6.2.4.1). |

### 6.2 Required QAPI Plan Content
The QAPI plan must include all of the following elements:

| Section | Required Content | Detail |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Plan Header | Plan year, version, approval date, approved by, QAPI Coordinator name, effective date. | Aligned with policy header standards. |
| 2. Program Mission & Goals | Statement of the QAPI program's mission and the specific quality improvement goals for the plan year. | Goals must be measurable, time-bound, and linked to identified priority areas. Minimum 3 goals. |
| 3. Quality Priority Areas | List of selected priority areas with rationale for each selection based on data analysis. | Minimum 3 priority areas; each linked to data from year-end assessment. |
| 4. Quality Indicators & Thresholds | For each priority area: specific quality indicator(s), data source, measurement frequency, performance threshold (target), and national benchmark (if available). | Indicators must be measurable and tracked no less frequently than quarterly. |
| 5. Performance Improvement Projects | List of active PIPs with: title, clinical/operational area, measurable goal, methodology, responsible lead, start date, and expected completion date. | Minimum 2 active PIPs at all times. Each PIP must have a responsible lead and measurable goal. |
| 6. Patient Safety Initiatives | Specific patient safety monitoring priorities and any active safety improvement projects. | Must include at minimum: fall prevention, medication safety, infection prevention. |
| 7. Data Collection & Analysis Plan | Description of data sources, collection methods, analysis frequency, and responsible parties for each indicator. | Must reference QAPI Data Source Inventory (QA-PG-001 §6.4.4). |
| 8. Committee Structure & Schedule | QAPI Committee composition, meeting frequency, and reporting schedule to the Governing Body. | Cross-reference QA-PG-003. |
| 9. Staff Education Plan | Planned QAPI-related education and training activities for the plan year. | Must include annual staff QAPI awareness training and any PIP-specific training. |
| 10. Resource Allocation | Description of resources allocated to the QAPI program including staff time, tools, and budget. | Must demonstrate Governing Body commitment per 42 CFR § 484.65(e). |
| 11. Reporting Timeline | Calendar of all required QAPI reports to the Governing Body and internal reporting milestones. | Quarterly Governing Body reports; monthly committee reporting. |
| 12. Prior Year Evaluation Summary | Summary of prior year plan performance including goals achieved, goals not met, PIP outcomes, and lessons learned. | Informs current year priorities. Included in plan submitted to Governing Body. |

### 6.3 Mid-Year Plan Modifications

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Identify the need for a mid-year plan modification based on: (a) new adverse event trends; (b) significant decline in quality indicator performance; (c) CMS survey findings; (d) new regulatory requirements; (e) Governing Body directive. Document the rationale for modification. | As events occur. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the proposed modification at the next QAPI Committee meeting (or at a special meeting if urgent). Document approval in committee minutes. | At next committee meeting; special meeting if urgent. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Update the QAPI plan document, noting the modification date, rationale, and committee approval. Maintain version control per EN-LC-001. | Within 7 calendar days of committee approval. |
| 6.3.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Report the modification to the Governing Body at the next quarterly meeting, including the rationale, expected impact, and any resource implications. | At next quarterly Governing Body meeting. |

### 6.4 Annual QAPI Plan Evaluation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Conduct the formal year-end evaluation of the current QAPI plan. The evaluation must assess: (a) achievement status for each goal (Met / Partially Met / Not Met); (b) outcome of each PIP (Completed / Sustained / Ongoing / Discontinued with rationale); (c) quality indicator trend analysis (improved / stable / declined); (d) patient safety event trends; (e) effectiveness of education initiatives; (f) adequacy of resources; (g) barriers encountered; (h) lessons learned; (i) recommendations for the next plan year. | Completed by November 30 of each year. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the year-end evaluation report at the December committee meeting. | December QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Include the evaluation summary in the QAPI plan submitted to the Governing Body per Section 6.1.5, and present the full evaluation to the Governing Body at the Q1 meeting alongside the new plan for approval. | Submitted with new plan by December 15; presented at Q1 meeting. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| QAPI plan not submitted to Governing Body by December 15 deadline. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator immediately. | Administrator escalates to Governing Body Chair. A special meeting may be convened. Plan must be submitted before Q1 meeting under all circumstances. | Plan submitted before Q1 meeting; root cause addressed per QA-AE-003. |
| Governing Body declines to approve QAPI plan at Q1 meeting. | Governing Body documents specific deficiencies and directs modifications. | QAPI Coordinator revises plan per Governing Body directives and resubmits within 30 calendar days. Interim operations continue under the prior year's plan pending approval. | Resubmitted within 30 calendar days. |
| QAPI plan has fewer than 2 active PIPs at any point during the plan year. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to QAPI Committee at next meeting. | QAPI Committee identifies and initiates a new PIP within 30 calendar days. | New PIP initiated within 30 days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Year-end quality assessment | Written assessment per Section 6.1.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Completed by November 30 annually. |
| Draft QAPI plan | Draft plan per Section 6.2 content requirements. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Completed by December 1 annually. |
| QAPI Committee plan approval | Committee meeting minutes documenting review and approval of final draft. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | December committee meeting. |
| Governing Body plan approval | Governing Body meeting minutes documenting review, discussion, and approval. | Governing Body Chair | Agency governance file; QAPI records. | Q1 Governing Body meeting. |
| Approved QAPI plan | Final approved plan document with all required elements per Section 6.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; copy to Administrator. | Distributed within 7 days of Governing Body approval. |
| Mid-year modifications | Updated plan with documented rationale, committee approval, and Governing Body notification. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Within 7 days of committee approval. |
| Year-end plan evaluation | Written evaluation report per Section 6.4.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Completed by November 30 annually. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all personnel within scope. | Each individual (completion); QAPI Coordinator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new assignment. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Written QAPI plan exists and is current. | Review of QAPI plan document; verify plan year and content completeness. | Current plan on file covering the active plan year with all required content per Section 6.2. |
| QAPI plan is approved annually by the Governing Body. | Review of Governing Body minutes for Q1 meeting. | Annual approval documented at Q1 meeting; no gap between plan years. |
| QAPI plan contains all required elements. | Audit of plan document against Section 6.2 checklist. | 100% of required elements present. |
| Minimum 2 active PIPs at all times. | Review of plan PIP section and QAPI Committee minutes. | 2 or more PIPs active at any point in the plan year. |
| Year-end evaluation is completed and informs next plan. | Review of evaluation report and its integration into subsequent plan. | Evaluation completed by November 30; summary included in next plan. |
| Mid-year modifications are documented and reported. | Review of plan version history and Governing Body minutes. | All modifications documented with rationale, committee approval, and Governing Body notification. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors will specifically verify:
A written QAPI plan exists for the current period. Surveyors will request the plan and verify it covers the current year with defined goals and indicators.
The plan was approved by the Governing Body. Surveyors will cross-reference the plan with Governing Body minutes to verify documented approval.
The plan is data-driven. Surveyors will verify that priority selection was based on data analysis, not arbitrary selection.
Goals are measurable. Surveyors will look for specific, quantifiable targets — not vague aspirational statements.
The plan is operationalized. Surveyors will compare plan goals with actual QAPI Committee activities and meeting minutes to verify the plan drives action.
The plan is reviewed and evaluated. Surveyors will look for evidence that the prior year's plan was evaluated and that results informed the current plan.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| QAPI plan contains vague goals without measurable thresholds. | Surveyor will cite inability to measure quality improvement. | Require specific numeric thresholds and measurement methods for every goal. |
| QAPI plan is a copy-paste of prior year with no data-driven updates. | Surveyor will cite failure to use data to inform priorities. | Mandate year-end assessment and document rationale for each priority based on data. |
| QAPI plan exists but QAPI Committee meetings do not reflect plan execution. | Surveyor will determine plan is not operationalized. | Require monthly committee agenda to reference plan goals and indicators. |
| No evidence of Governing Body approval. | Surveyor will cite failure of governing body oversight. | Document approval in Governing Body minutes per GV-GB-001. |
| Plan has not been updated after significant change (survey finding, sentinel event). | Surveyor may cite failure to respond to identified risks. | Enforce mid-year modification process per Section 6.3. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 The QAPI Coordinator and all QAPI Committee members shall be trained on QAPI plan development methodology, content requirements, and evaluation processes within 30 calendar days of appointment.
10.2 All department heads and supervisors responsible for achieving QAPI plan goals shall be briefed on the approved plan content, their specific responsibilities, and performance thresholds within 14 calendar days of Governing Body approval.
10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version is valid. Superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body.

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the composition, authority, operating procedures, documentation standards, and escalation requirements for the QAPI Committee at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The QAPI Committee is the operational body responsible for translating the QAPI program governance (QA-PG-001) and annual plan (QA-PG-002) into executed quality improvement activities. This policy ensures the committee functions as a structured, accountable, multidisciplinary forum for data review, decision-making, and quality improvement action consistent with the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator (Committee Chair)
All QAPI Committee members (standing and ad hoc)
The Agency Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Compliance Officer (standing invitee)
All department heads and supervisors who participate in or present to the QAPI Committee
Exclusion: This policy governs committee structure and operations. The content of the QAPI plan is governed by QA-PG-002. Individual PIP execution is governed by QA-PI-001. Adverse event investigation methodology is governed by QA-AE-002.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a standing multidisciplinary QAPI Committee that serves as the primary operational body for quality assessment and performance improvement activities.
4.2 The QAPI Committee shall be chaired by the QAPI Coordinator. In the absence of the QAPI Coordinator, the Director of Nursing shall serve as acting chair.
4.3 The QAPI Committee shall meet no fewer than 12 times per calendar year (monthly). Special meetings shall be convened when urgent quality or safety matters arise.
4.4 Committee membership shall include representation from all active clinical disciplines, administration, and compliance to ensure agency-wide perspective in quality monitoring.
4.5 All committee decisions, directives, and action items shall be documented in formal meeting minutes with assigned responsible parties, deadlines, and follow-up requirements.
4.6 The QAPI Committee shall serve as the primary conduit for escalating quality and safety concerns to the Governing Body through the quarterly QAPI Performance Report prepared by the QAPI Coordinator.
4.7 Committee attendance shall be monitored. Members who miss 3 or more meetings within a 12-month period without documented cause shall be replaced.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Standing Member | An individual appointed to the QAPI Committee on an ongoing basis with recurring meeting attendance obligations. |
| Ad Hoc Member | An individual invited to attend specific QAPI Committee meetings to provide subject matter expertise on a particular topic. |
| Standing Invitation | An open invitation to attend all meetings without voting obligation; applicable to the Compliance Officer and Administrator. |
| Quorum | For the QAPI Committee: the QAPI Coordinator (or acting chair) plus a minimum of 50% of standing members, with at least one clinical discipline representative present. |
| Action Item | A specific task assigned to a named individual with a defined deadline, arising from committee discussion or decision. |
| Escalation | The formal referral of a quality or safety concern from the QAPI Committee to the Governing Body, Administrator, or Compliance Officer when the matter exceeds committee authority or requires immediate executive attention. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Committee Composition and Appointment

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish the QAPI Committee with the following minimum standing membership: (a) QAPI Coordinator (Chair); (b) Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager; (c) at least one registered nurse representative; (d) at least one therapy representative (PT, OT, or SLP); (e) at least one representative from home health aide services or supervision; (f) Intake/Scheduling representative; (g) Administrator or administrative designee. Standing invitations: Compliance Officer, CFO/Revenue Cycle Director (or designee). Additional members may be added based on agency scope. | Within 30 calendar days of program establishment. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Prepare and maintain a formal Committee Membership Roster documenting: member name, title/role, discipline, standing or ad hoc status, appointment date, and contact information. Roster must be available for CMS survey review. | Updated within 7 calendar days of any change; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Orient new committee members within 14 calendar days of appointment. Orientation must cover: (a) committee purpose and authority; (b) meeting schedule and attendance expectations; (c) current QAPI plan and active priorities; (d) quality indicator dashboard review; (e) active PIPs; (f) documentation expectations; (g) confidentiality and non-retaliation principles. | Within 14 calendar days of appointment. |
| 6.1.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Monitor committee attendance. If a standing member misses 3 or more meetings within a rolling 12-month period without documented cause (illness, leave, agency-approved conflict), notify the member's supervisor and the Administrator. Recommend replacement if pattern continues. | Monitored monthly; action within 14 calendar days of third missed meeting. |

### 6.2 Meeting Scheduling and Preparation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish the annual meeting schedule (12 regular monthly meetings) and distribute to all standing members and standing invitees by December 15 of the preceding year. Meetings shall be scheduled at a consistent day and time to maximize attendance. | Annual schedule distributed by December 15. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Prepare and distribute the meeting agenda to all committee members no fewer than 5 business days before each meeting. The agenda must include standing items: (a) Call to order and attendance; (b) Approval of prior meeting minutes; (c) Action item tracker review (open items); (d) Quality indicator dashboard review; (e) Active PIP status updates; (f) Adverse event / incident summary (current month); (g) Infection surveillance update; (h) Patient satisfaction data; (i) Regulatory / survey update; (j) Education / training update; (k) New business; (l) Next meeting date / adjournment. | 5 business days before each meeting. |
| 6.2.3 | All presenters (DON, Compliance Officer, Department Heads) | Prepare data reports, dashboards, or summaries for assigned agenda items and submit to the QAPI Coordinator at least 3 business days before the meeting for compilation into the meeting packet. | 3 business days before each meeting. |
| 6.2.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Compile the meeting packet including the agenda, prior meeting minutes, action item tracker, quality indicator dashboard, and all presenter submissions. Distribute to all members at least 2 business days before the meeting. | 2 business days before each meeting. |

### 6.3 Meeting Conduct and Documentation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator (Chair) | Call the meeting to order and determine quorum. If quorum is not achieved, the meeting may proceed for discussion purposes only; no official directives or decisions may be adopted. A meeting without quorum must be noted in the minutes, and a make-up meeting must be scheduled within 14 calendar days. | At the start of each meeting. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Facilitate the meeting per the published agenda. Ensure each standing item receives adequate discussion time. Ensure data is presented, analyzed, and discussed — not merely read aloud. Direct the committee to identify specific actions for any adverse trend or identified concern. | During each meeting. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator (or designated recorder) | Record formal minutes for each meeting. Minutes must document: (a) date, time, location, and duration; (b) members present and absent (with quorum determination); (c) each agenda item discussed with summary of key data points and discussion; (d) all decisions made; (e) all action items assigned including responsible party, specific task, and deadline; (f) items requiring escalation to the Governing Body; (g) next meeting date. | Draft minutes completed within 7 calendar days of the meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Distribute draft minutes to all committee members for review within 7 calendar days. Minutes are formally approved at the next regular meeting. | Distributed within 7 days; approved at next meeting. |
| 6.3.5 | QAPI Coordinator | Update the QAPI Action Item Tracker immediately following each meeting. Track all open items, newly assigned items, items completed since last meeting, and overdue items. Flag any item overdue by more than 30 calendar days for escalation at the next meeting. | Immediately following each meeting. |

### 6.4 Special Meetings

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Convene a special QAPI Committee meeting when any of the following occur: (a) sentinel event or serious adverse event; (b) CMS survey findings requiring immediate QAPI response; (c) quality indicator showing acute deterioration (defined as ≥ 20% decline from prior period); (d) Governing Body directive for immediate QAPI action; (e) public health emergency affecting care quality or patient safety. | Within 48 hours of triggering event for sentinel events and survey findings; within 5 business days for other triggers. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Provide notice of special meeting to all standing members at least 24 hours in advance (or as soon as practicable for patient safety emergencies). Agenda must specify the triggering event and required discussion. | Minimum 24 hours notice. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Document special meeting minutes using the same standards as regular meetings per Section 6.3.3. | Within 7 calendar days of meeting. |

### 6.5 Escalation to Governing Body

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Escalate the following to the Governing Body immediately (outside the quarterly report cycle): (a) sentinel events or patient deaths with agency-contributing factors; (b) quality indicators below threshold for 3 consecutive periods without improvement despite corrective action; (c) patterns suggesting systemic quality failure across multiple indicators; (d) CMS survey Condition-level deficiency; (e) any matter the committee determines exceeds its authority or resource capacity. | Within 72 hours of committee determination. |
| 6.5.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include in each quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body: (a) committee meeting summary (dates, attendance, key decisions); (b) all quality concerns and actions taken; (c) items requiring Governing Body direction. | Per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1 schedule. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Committee fails to meet for 2 consecutive months. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and Compliance Officer in writing. | Administrator directs immediate scheduling. If 3 consecutive months missed, reported to Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.5. | Make-up meeting within 14 calendar days. |
| Standing member attendance below 75% (misses 3+ meetings per year). | QAPI Coordinator notifies member's supervisor and Administrator. | Supervisor addresses with employee. If not corrected, member is replaced within 30 calendar days. | Replacement within 30 days if not corrected. |
| Committee lacks clinical discipline representation at a meeting. | QAPI Coordinator ensures at least one clinician is present for clinical agenda items. | If clinical items cannot be addressed, defer to next meeting and document. Clinical items must not go unaddressed for more than 2 consecutive meetings. | Addressed at next meeting. |
| Action item overdue by more than 30 calendar days. | QAPI Coordinator flags at monthly meeting and escalates to assigned party's supervisor. | If overdue by 60 calendar days, escalated to Administrator. If overdue by 90 calendar days, escalated to Governing Body in quarterly report. | 30/60/90 day escalation tiers. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Committee Membership Roster | Current roster per Section 6.1.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated within 7 days of change. |
| Annual meeting schedule | Meeting dates and times for the calendar year. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; distributed to all members. | Distributed by December 15 of preceding year. |
| Meeting agendas | Agenda for each regular and special meeting. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | 5 business days before each meeting; retained 7 years. |
| Meeting packets | Compiled data reports and materials for each meeting. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | 2 business days before each meeting; retained 7 years. |
| Meeting minutes | Formal minutes per Section 6.3.3. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Draft within 7 days; approved at next meeting; retained 7 years. |
| QAPI Action Item Tracker | Central log per Section 6.3.5. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated continuously; presented monthly. |
| Attendance log | Record of member attendance for each meeting. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At each meeting; retained 7 years. |
| New member orientation documentation | Record of orientation completion per Section 6.1.3. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; member's personnel file. | Within 14 days of appointment. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed acknowledgment forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator (collection). | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new assignment. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Committee established with required composition. | Review of membership roster against Section 6.1.1 requirements. | All required disciplines represented; roster current. |
| Committee meets at least monthly. | Review of meeting minutes with dates. | 12+ meetings per calendar year. |
| Meeting minutes are complete and timely. | Audit of minutes against Section 6.3.3 requirements. | All elements present; drafts within 7 days. |
| Action items are tracked and resolved. | Review of Action Item Tracker; aged item analysis. | No item overdue > 60 days without documented escalation. |
| Attendance meets minimum standards. | Review of attendance logs. | Average attendance ≥ 75% of standing members per meeting. |
| Quorum achieved at all decision-making meetings. | Review of quorum determination in minutes. | Quorum documented at all meetings where decisions were adopted. |
| New members oriented within 14 days. | Review of orientation documentation. | 100% compliance. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors will verify: (1) a multidisciplinary committee exists with regular meetings; (2) meeting minutes demonstrate substantive data review and decision-making, not perfunctory check-the-box documentation; (3) action items are assigned, tracked, and closed; (4) committee activities align with the QAPI plan; (5) escalation to the Governing Body is documented when warranted.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minutes reflect "reviewed data" without documenting specific findings, analysis, or actions. | Surveyor cites lack of evidence that committee is functioning effectively. | Train recorder to document specific data points, committee discussion, and resulting decisions. |
| Committee membership is nursing-only; therapy and other disciplines absent. | Surveyor cites failure of agency-wide QAPI per 42 CFR § 484.65(a). | Enforce multidisciplinary composition per Section 6.1.1. |
| Action items assigned but never tracked or followed up. | Surveyor determines quality improvement is not sustained. | Require Action Item Tracker review as standing agenda item; escalate overdue items per Section 6.6. |
| Meetings occur but do not reference the QAPI plan goals. | Surveyor determines plan is not operationalized. | Align monthly agenda to plan priorities; reference plan indicators in dashboard. |

# QA-PI-001 — Performance Improvement Project Management
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-001 |
| Title | Performance Improvement Project Management |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the requirements for selecting, designing, implementing, monitoring, and documenting Performance Improvement Projects (PIPs) at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. PIPs are the primary mechanism through which the agency translates quality data analysis into targeted, measurable improvement in specific areas of clinical care, operational performance, or patient safety. This policy ensures the agency satisfies 42 CFR § 484.65(c) and (d), which require the agency to conduct performance improvement activities focused on high-priority areas, implement and track PIPs proportionate to the agency's scope and complexity, and demonstrate measurable improvement over time.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
All QAPI Committee members
All PIP leads and team members
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
All department heads whose areas are the subject of a PIP
All staff participating in PIP data collection, intervention, or implementation activities
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a minimum of two (2) active PIPs at all times, proportionate to the scope and complexity of the agency's services, as required by 42 CFR § 484.65(d).
4.2 At least one active PIP shall address a clinical outcome or patient safety topic. The second PIP may address clinical, operational, compliance, or patient experience areas.
4.3 PIPs shall be selected based on data analysis identifying areas of underperformance, adverse trends, high-risk processes, or areas with the greatest potential for patient impact. PIP selection shall not be arbitrary.
4.4 Each PIP shall follow a structured quality improvement methodology (Plan-Do-Study-Act [PDSA], or equivalent evidence-based methodology approved by the QAPI Committee) and shall include: (a) a defined problem statement based on data; (b) measurable goals with numeric targets; (c) root cause analysis or contributing factor analysis; (d) specific, implementable interventions; (e) defined data collection plan; (f) monitoring schedule; (g) evaluation criteria; (h) sustainment plan.
4.5 Each PIP shall have a designated PIP Lead who is accountable for project execution, data collection, reporting, and achievement of measurable goals. The PIP Lead shall report progress to the QAPI Committee monthly.
4.6 PIP results shall be documented and reported to the Governing Body through the quarterly QAPI Performance Report. Completed PIPs shall include a final evaluation with demonstrated outcomes and sustainment plan.
4.7 PIPs that fail to demonstrate measurable progress within the defined evaluation timeframe shall be subject to reassessment by the QAPI Committee, including consideration of methodology revision, resource reallocation, or project redesign.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Performance Improvement Project (PIP) | A structured, time-limited quality improvement initiative with a defined problem statement, measurable goals, evidence-based interventions, and monitoring plan. |
| PIP Lead | The individual assigned accountability for the design, execution, monitoring, and reporting of a specific PIP. |
| PDSA Cycle | Plan-Do-Study-Act — a four-stage iterative quality improvement methodology. |
| Baseline | The starting performance level against which improvement is measured, established from data collected prior to intervention implementation. |
| Target | The specific, measurable performance level the PIP aims to achieve within the defined timeframe. |
| Sustainment | The phase following active PIP intervention during which the agency monitors whether improvement gains are maintained over time (minimum 3 months post-intervention). |
| PIP Charter | The formal project document that defines the PIP's scope, problem statement, goals, team, methodology, timeline, and evaluation plan. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 PIP Selection

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Identify potential PIP topics through systematic review of: (a) quality indicator trends showing underperformance vs. threshold or national benchmark; (b) adverse event patterns and root cause analysis findings; (c) patient satisfaction data and HHCAHPS results; (d) Star Rating and Home Health Compare data trends; (e) compliance audit findings; (f) CMS survey findings or Plan of Correction requirements; (g) infection surveillance trends; (h) staff competency evaluation trends; (i) clinical outcome data (hospitalization rates, emergency department use, wound healing, functional improvement); (j) Governing Body directives. | Ongoing; formal review during QAPI plan development cycle per QA-PG-002. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Present candidate PIP topics to the QAPI Committee with supporting data analysis. For each candidate, provide: (a) data supporting the need; (b) estimated scope and impact; (c) feasibility assessment; (d) alignment with QAPI plan priorities. | At QAPI Committee meeting during annual planning or when new need identified. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Committee | Select PIPs based on: (a) magnitude of the quality gap; (b) impact on patient safety and outcomes; (c) regulatory requirement or survey finding; (d) feasibility of improvement within available resources; (e) alignment with QAPI plan priorities. Document selection rationale in committee minutes. | At QAPI Committee meeting; annually during planning and as needs arise. |
| 6.1.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Assign a PIP Lead for each selected project. The PIP Lead must have clinical or operational expertise in the project area, available time for project management, and authority to implement interventions within the defined scope. Document assignment in committee minutes. | At time of PIP selection. |

### 6.2 PIP Design and Charter

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | PIP Lead | Develop a PIP Charter document for approval by the QAPI Committee. The charter must include: (a) Problem Statement — a concise, data-supported description of the issue; (b) Baseline Data — current performance level with data source, time period, and sample size; (c) Measurable Goal/Target — specific numeric or percentage target (e.g., "Reduce 30-day hospitalization rate from 22% to 15% within 6 months"); (d) Root Cause / Contributing Factor Analysis — documented analysis of why the current performance gap exists; (e) Interventions — specific, actionable changes to be implemented; (f) Team Members — named individuals with defined roles; (g) Data Collection Plan — data source, collection method, frequency, and responsible collector; (h) Timeline — start date, PDSA cycle milestones, evaluation dates, and expected completion date; (i) Evaluation Criteria — how success will be measured; (j) Sustainment Plan — how gains will be maintained post-intervention; (k) Resource Requirements — staff time, tools, training, budget. | Draft charter completed within 30 calendar days of PIP selection. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the PIP Charter. Committee may require modifications before approval. Approval documented in committee minutes. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following charter submission. |

### 6.3 PIP Implementation and Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | PIP Lead | Implement interventions as defined in the approved charter. Document all implementation activities including: date of implementation, staff involved, processes changed, education delivered, and any barriers encountered. | Per charter timeline. |
| 6.3.2 | PIP Lead | Collect data per the charter's data collection plan. Maintain a PIP data tracking document that records: data point, date collected, value, and variance from target. | Per charter frequency (minimum monthly). |
| 6.3.3 | PIP Lead | Present a monthly PIP status report to the QAPI Committee including: (a) current data vs. baseline and target; (b) interventions implemented since last report; (c) barriers encountered; (d) PDSA cycle status (which cycle, what was learned); (e) recommended adjustments. | At each monthly QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.3.4 | QAPI Committee | Review PIP status. If data shows no measurable progress after 2 PDSA cycles or 3 months of intervention (whichever comes first), the committee shall: (a) analyze contributing factors; (b) consider methodology revision; (c) consider additional interventions or resource allocation; (d) document the committee's analysis and decision. | At each monthly review; reassessment trigger at 3 months or 2 PDSA cycles. |
| 6.3.5 | QAPI Coordinator | Include PIP status in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. | Quarterly. |

### 6.4 PIP Completion and Evaluation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | PIP Lead | Upon achieving the measurable goal, prepare a PIP Completion Report that includes: (a) final data compared to baseline and target; (b) summary of all interventions implemented and PDSA cycles completed; (c) analysis of what worked and what did not; (d) lessons learned; (e) sustainment plan with monitoring schedule (minimum 3-month sustainment monitoring period); (f) recommendation for formal closure or transition to ongoing monitoring. | Within 30 calendar days of goal achievement. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Committee | Review the PIP Completion Report. If the committee confirms the goal was achieved and the sustainment plan is adequate, approve formal closure. If sustainment monitoring reveals regression, the committee may reopen the PIP or initiate a new PIP. Document in committee minutes. | At QAPI Committee meeting following report submission. |
| 6.4.3 | PIP Lead | During the 3-month sustainment monitoring period, collect data per the sustainment plan schedule and report results to the QAPI Committee. If performance declines below the achieved target by more than 10%, notify the QAPI Coordinator immediately for committee review. | Monthly during 3-month sustainment period. |
| 6.4.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Archive the completed PIP file including: charter, all monthly reports, data tracking document, completion report, and sustainment data. Completed PIPs serve as evidence for CMS survey review. | Within 14 calendar days of formal closure. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Fewer than 2 active PIPs at any time. | QAPI Coordinator notifies QAPI Committee. | Committee selects and charters a new PIP per Section 6.1. | New PIP initiated within 30 calendar days. |
| PIP shows no measurable progress after 6 months. | PIP Lead escalates to QAPI Committee with analysis. | Committee reviews and either redesigns, provides additional resources, or replaces the PIP with a new project addressing the same area. Documented in minutes. | Decision within 30 calendar days of escalation. |
| PIP Lead vacancy or inability to continue. | QAPI Coordinator reassigns immediately. | New PIP Lead appointed; briefed within 7 calendar days. | Reassignment within 7 calendar days. |
| CMS survey finding requires PIP. | Administrator and QAPI Coordinator convene special QAPI meeting. | PIP chartered within 14 calendar days of survey exit per Plan of Correction requirements. | Charter within 14 days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| PIP selection rationale | QAPI Committee meeting minutes documenting data-driven selection and rationale. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At time of selection. |
| PIP Charter | Formal charter per Section 6.2.1. | PIP Lead | QAPI records. | Within 30 days of selection; approved at next committee meeting. |
| Monthly PIP status reports | Written or dashboard reports per Section 6.3.3. | PIP Lead | QAPI records. | Monthly at each committee meeting. |
| PIP data tracking | Ongoing data collection documentation per charter. | PIP Lead | QAPI records. | Per charter frequency. |
| PIP Completion Report | Final report per Section 6.4.1. | PIP Lead | QAPI records. | Within 30 days of goal achievement. |
| Sustainment monitoring data | Data collected during 3-month sustainment period. | PIP Lead | QAPI records. | Monthly during sustainment. |
| Archived PIP file | Complete project file per Section 6.4.4. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Within 14 days of closure; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator (collection). | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minimum 2 active PIPs at all times. | Review of PIP inventory and QAPI Committee minutes. | 2+ PIPs active continuously; at least 1 clinical/safety-focused. |
| PIPs are data-driven with documented selection rationale. | Review of committee minutes and charter problem statements. | 100% of PIPs have data-supported problem statement. |
| PIP Charters are complete and approved. | Audit of charter documents against Section 6.2.1 requirements. | 100% of PIPs have approved charter with all required elements. |
| Monthly progress reports submitted. | Review of committee minutes and PIP reports. | Reports submitted at each monthly committee meeting. |
| PIPs achieve measurable improvement. | Review of baseline vs. final outcome data. | Measurable improvement documented in at least 1 PIP annually. |
| Completed PIPs have sustainment monitoring. | Review of completion reports and sustainment data. | 3-month sustainment period documented for each completed PIP. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) request documentation of all active and recently completed PIPs; (2) verify each PIP has measurable goals and data-driven selection; (3) verify PIPs are reviewed at committee meetings; (4) look for evidence of actual improvement, not just activity; (5) verify the Governing Body receives PIP reports; (6) assess whether PIPs are proportionate to the agency's scope and complexity.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PIPs selected without data support ("We just picked this topic"). | Surveyor cites failure to use data-driven approach. | Require documented data analysis for every PIP selection per Section 6.1. |
| PIP goals are vague ("Improve wound care"). | Surveyor cannot assess measurable improvement. | Require specific numeric targets in every charter. |
| PIPs exist on paper but no data collected or interventions implemented. | Surveyor determines PIPs are not operationalized. | Monthly status reports with data required per Section 6.3. |
| PIP completed but no sustainment monitoring. | Surveyor questions sustainability of improvement. | Mandate 3-month sustainment period per Section 6.4. |
| Only 1 PIP active or no PIPs active. | Direct citation under 42 CFR § 484.65(d). | Monitor PIP inventory continuously; escalate per Section 6.5. |

10.2 All QAPI Committee members shall receive annual training on PIP review and evaluation expectations.
