# QA-PI-002 — Quality Indicator Monitoring & Reporting
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-002 |
| Title | Quality Indicator Monitoring & Reporting |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's quality indicator dashboard, monitoring frequency, data analysis methodology, performance thresholds, reporting requirements, and escalation triggers for adverse performance trends at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Quality indicators are the quantifiable measures through which the QAPI program tracks agency performance, identifies improvement opportunities, and evaluates the impact of interventions. This policy ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.65(b), which requires the agency to use quality indicator data, including measures derived from OASIS, to monitor the effectiveness of care and identify opportunities for improvement.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
All QAPI Committee members
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
All department heads and supervisors responsible for data collection or indicator performance
All clinical staff whose work generates indicator data
The Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a defined set of quality indicators organized into a Quality Indicator Dashboard that is reviewed at each QAPI Committee meeting and reported to the Governing Body quarterly.
4.2 Quality indicators shall span the following domains at minimum: (a) clinical outcomes; (b) patient safety; (c) process compliance; (d) patient experience; (e) utilization and efficiency; (f) workforce competency.
4.3 Each quality indicator shall have a defined performance threshold. Thresholds shall be established using: (a) national benchmarks (CMS Home Health Compare, HHCAHPS national averages); (b) state comparative data; (c) agency historical performance; or (d) regulatory standards. Thresholds shall be reviewed and updated annually during QAPI plan development.
4.4 Any quality indicator that falls below its defined threshold for 2 consecutive reporting periods shall trigger mandatory corrective action analysis by the QAPI Committee, documented in committee minutes.
4.5 Any quality indicator that falls below its defined threshold for 3 consecutive reporting periods without documented improvement shall be escalated to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.5.
4.6 Quality indicator data shall be trended over time (minimum 12-month rolling trend) to identify patterns, seasonal variations, and the impact of improvement interventions.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Quality Indicator Dashboard | A consolidated visual report presenting all monitored quality indicators with current performance, threshold, trend, and status (on target / caution / below threshold). |
| Performance Threshold | The minimum acceptable level of performance, below which corrective action is triggered. |
| Reporting Period | The time interval over which indicator data is collected and analyzed. Default: monthly for operational indicators; quarterly for outcome-based indicators. |
| Rolling Trend | The display of indicator performance over consecutive reporting periods (minimum 12 months) to identify directional patterns. |
| Caution Zone | Performance within 5% above the threshold — indicating risk of falling below threshold if trends continue. |
| Corrective Action Trigger | The point at which a quality indicator's performance mandates formal corrective analysis — specifically, 2 consecutive periods below threshold. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Quality Indicator Selection and Dashboard Design

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish the Quality Indicator Dashboard with indicators covering the following mandatory categories. For each indicator, define: (a) indicator name; (b) category; (c) data source; (d) calculation methodology; (e) reporting frequency; (f) performance threshold; (g) national benchmark (if available); (h) responsible data collector. | Established within 60 days of program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Minimum required indicator categories and examples (agency shall define specific indicators within each): Clinical Outcomes — acute care hospitalization rate, emergency department use without hospitalization, improvement in ambulation, improvement in bathing, improvement in pain management, wound healing rate. Patient Safety — fall rate, medication error rate, infection rate, adverse drug event rate. Process Compliance — timely SOC visit rate, OASIS completion timeliness, physician order signature turnaround, documentation completion rate, face-to-face encounter compliance. Patient Experience — HHCAHPS overall rating, likelihood to recommend, communication scores. Utilization & Efficiency — LUPA rate, visit utilization vs. ordered frequency, denial rate, average length of stay. Workforce — staff competency evaluation completion rate, training compliance rate, staff turnover rate. | At dashboard establishment; additional indicators may be added at any time. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Set performance thresholds for each indicator using the following hierarchy: (1) CMS or regulatory standard (if applicable); (2) national benchmark (Home Health Compare, HHCAHPS national average); (3) state comparative data; (4) agency historical performance (prior 12-month average); (5) QAPI Committee clinical judgment (documented). Document threshold rationale. | At dashboard establishment; reviewed annually during QAPI plan development. |
| 6.1.4 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the complete Quality Indicator Dashboard including all indicators and thresholds. Document approval in committee minutes. | At establishment; annually during QAPI plan review. |

### 6.2 Data Collection and Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Designated data collectors (per indicator) | Collect indicator data per the defined methodology and frequency. Sources include: (a) EHR/EMR clinical data; (b) OASIS outcome reports; (c) billing/claims data; (d) incident reports; (e) patient satisfaction surveys; (f) HR/training records; (g) Home Health Compare; (h) CASPER/iQIES reports. | Per indicator frequency (monthly or quarterly). |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Compile all indicator data into the Quality Indicator Dashboard. For each indicator, calculate: (a) current period performance; (b) comparison to threshold; (c) comparison to prior period; (d) comparison to national benchmark; (e) 12-month rolling trend; (f) status classification (On Target / Caution / Below Threshold). | Monthly; presented at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Perform trend analysis on all indicators. Identify: (a) indicators trending downward for 3+ consecutive periods even if still above threshold; (b) indicators in caution zone; (c) indicators below threshold; (d) indicators showing significant improvement following PIP intervention; (e) seasonal patterns requiring adjusted interpretation. Document analysis findings. | Monthly as part of dashboard preparation. |
| 6.2.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Flag any indicator meeting escalation criteria: (a) below threshold for 1 period — flagged as "Below Threshold" on dashboard; (b) below threshold for 2 consecutive periods — mandatory corrective action trigger; (c) below threshold for 3 consecutive periods — Governing Body escalation trigger. | At each dashboard update. |

### 6.3 Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Present the complete Quality Indicator Dashboard to the QAPI Committee at each monthly meeting. Presentation must include: current data, trend analysis, flagged indicators, recommended actions, and status of previously triggered corrective actions. | Monthly at each committee meeting. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Committee | Review dashboard data and take action: (a) for indicators at or above threshold — acknowledge and document; (b) for indicators in caution zone — identify contributing factors and preventive measures; (c) for indicators below threshold — initiate corrective action analysis per Section 6.4; (d) for indicators requiring escalation — direct escalation per Section 6.5. Document all discussion and decisions in committee minutes. | At each monthly meeting. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Include dashboard summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. Highlight: (a) indicators below threshold; (b) indicators showing improvement; (c) indicators requiring Governing Body attention. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |

### 6.4 Corrective Action Triggers

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Committee | When an indicator falls below threshold for 2 consecutive reporting periods, the committee shall: (a) assign a committee member to conduct a contributing factor analysis; (b) review relevant data sources, incident reports, and process documentation; (c) determine whether a formal root cause analysis (QA-AE-002) or corrective action plan (QA-AE-003) is warranted; (d) determine whether a new or existing PIP should address the issue; (e) assign corrective action with responsible party, specific interventions, and measurable resolution target; (f) define monitoring frequency (minimum monthly); (g) document all analysis and decisions in committee minutes. | At the meeting where 2nd consecutive below-threshold period is identified. |
| 6.4.2 | Assigned responsible party | Implement corrective interventions and report progress to the QAPI Committee monthly. | Monthly until indicator returns to threshold for 2 consecutive periods. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Committee | Close the corrective action when the indicator returns to or above threshold for 2 consecutive periods. Document closure in committee minutes. If the indicator does not return to threshold within 3 additional periods, escalate per Section 6.5. | At committee meeting when criteria met. |

### 6.5 Escalation

| Condition | Escalation Path | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Indicator below threshold for 3 consecutive periods. | QAPI Coordinator includes in quarterly Governing Body report with recommended action. | Governing Body directs corrective action per GV-GB-001 Section 6.2.4.3. | Next quarterly Governing Body meeting or sooner if patient safety risk. |
| Indicator below threshold for 3 consecutive periods with no improvement despite corrective action. | QAPI Coordinator issues special written report to Governing Body. | Governing Body directs enhanced intervention, resource reallocation, or PIP assignment with monthly Governing Body updates until resolved. | Special report within 7 days of identification. |
| Acute deterioration (≥ 20% decline in single period). | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and convenes special committee meeting. | Committee conducts emergency analysis and implements immediate interventions. Governing Body notified within 72 hours. | Special meeting within 48 hours; Governing Body notified within 72 hours. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Quality Indicator Dashboard | Complete dashboard with all indicators, thresholds, data, and trends. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated monthly; retained 7 years. |
| Indicator definitions and thresholds | Documentation per Section 6.1.1 for each indicator. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At establishment; updated annually. |
| Dashboard review and committee actions | QAPI Committee meeting minutes documenting review and decisions. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At each monthly meeting; retained 7 years. |
| Corrective action documentation | Analysis, interventions, responsible parties, and outcomes per Section 6.4. | Assigned responsible party; QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At trigger; tracked until closure. |
| Quarterly Governing Body reports | Dashboard summary per Section 6.3.3. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dashboard exists with defined indicators and thresholds. | Review of dashboard document. | Dashboard current; all required categories represented. |
| Dashboard reviewed at each committee meeting. | Review of committee minutes. | Dashboard review documented at 100% of meetings. |
| Corrective actions triggered for below-threshold indicators. | Review of dashboard flags and committee minutes. | 100% of indicators below threshold for 2+ periods have documented corrective action. |
| Dashboard reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of Governing Body minutes. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| Indicators trended over 12 months. | Review of dashboard trend data. | Rolling 12-month trend visible for all indicators. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) request the quality indicator dashboard; (2) verify indicators span multiple domains (not nursing-only); (3) verify thresholds exist and are based on data; (4) verify adverse trends trigger documented action; (5) verify the Governing Body receives and acts upon quality data.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dashboard tracks data but no thresholds defined. | Cannot determine when action is required. | Require threshold for every indicator per Section 6.1.3. |
| Data collected but never analyzed or acted upon. | Passive monitoring without improvement. | Mandate corrective action for 2 consecutive below-threshold periods. |
| Dashboard reviewed by committee but minutes show no analysis or decisions. | Surveyor cites perfunctory review. | Require documented discussion, findings, and actions for each flagged indicator. |
| Only OASIS-derived indicators monitored; operational and patient experience excluded. | Failure of agency-wide monitoring. | Require indicators across all 6 categories per Section 6.1.2. |

Training: QAPI Committee members trained on dashboard interpretation; all staff aware of quality indicators relevant to their role.

# QA-PI-003 — Clinical Outcome Benchmarking
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-003 |
| Title | Clinical Outcome Benchmarking |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy requires Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to systematically compare its clinical outcomes against national benchmarks, CMS quality measures, and state comparative data, and to document a structured response to identified underperformance. External benchmarking provides the objective context necessary to evaluate whether agency performance reflects acceptable quality of care, regardless of internal trend direction. This policy supports the QAPI program's data-driven mandate under 42 CFR § 484.65(b) and (c) by ensuring the agency does not operate in isolation from industry performance standards.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
All QAPI Committee members
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
All clinical supervisors responsible for outcome-driving care areas
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall compare its clinical outcomes against national and state benchmarks at least quarterly, using data from CMS Home Health Compare, CASPER/iQIES reports, HHCAHPS, and other recognized comparative data sources.
4.2 Benchmarking shall encompass, at minimum: (a) CMS-publicly reported quality measures; (b) patient outcome measures derived from OASIS; (c) patient experience measures from HHCAHPS; (d) Star Ratings and Home Health Compare composite scores.
4.3 When agency performance falls below national benchmarks on any publicly reported measure, the QAPI Committee shall conduct a documented analysis and develop a response plan within 30 calendar days.
4.4 Benchmarking results shall be reported to the Governing Body quarterly as part of the QAPI Performance Report.
4.5 Benchmark targets shall be incorporated into the annual QAPI plan per QA-PG-002.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Benchmark | An external performance standard derived from national, state, or peer group data against which the agency's performance is compared. |
| Home Health Compare | The CMS public reporting website that displays quality measures, patient survey results, and Star Ratings for Medicare-certified home health agencies. |
| CASPER/iQIES | CMS reporting systems providing agency-specific quality measure data, OASIS-based outcome reports, and potentially avoidable event reports. |
| HHCAHPS | Home Health Consumer Assessment of Healthcare Providers and Systems — the standardized patient experience survey. |
| Star Rating | The CMS composite quality score (1–5 stars) based on publicly reported measures. |
| Underperformance | Agency performance that falls below the national average or the 50th percentile on a benchmarked measure. |
| Significant Underperformance | Agency performance that falls below the 25th percentile on a benchmarked measure or is more than 2 standard deviations below the national average. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Benchmark Data Sources and Collection

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Identify and maintain access to the following benchmark data sources: (a) CMS Home Health Compare — quarterly quality measure data, Star Ratings; (b) CASPER/iQIES — OASIS-based outcome and process reports, potentially avoidable event reports; (c) HHCAHPS national and state comparative data; (d) state health department comparative reports (if available in California); (e) accreditation body comparative data (if applicable). Document all data sources in the QAPI Data Source Inventory per QA-PG-001 Section 6.4.4. | At program establishment; sources reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Retrieve and compile benchmark comparison data at least quarterly. For each benchmarked measure, document: (a) agency performance (current quarter and rolling 12-month); (b) national average; (c) state average (if available); (d) national percentile ranking (if available); (e) prior quarter comparison; (f) trend direction (improving / stable / declining). | Quarterly; aligned with CMS data release schedule. |

### 6.2 Benchmark Analysis and Response

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Present benchmark comparison data to the QAPI Committee. Classify each measure: (a) At or Above Benchmark — agency at or above national average; (b) Below Benchmark — agency below national average but above 25th percentile; (c) Significantly Below Benchmark — agency at or below 25th percentile. | At each quarterly QAPI Committee meeting following data compilation. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Committee | For measures classified as "Below Benchmark": (a) analyze potential contributing factors; (b) determine whether the measure is already addressed by an active PIP or corrective action; (c) if not already addressed, determine whether a new PIP, corrective action, or targeted intervention is warranted; (d) assign responsibility and timeline; (e) document analysis and decision in committee minutes. | At the committee meeting where data is presented. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Committee | For measures classified as "Significantly Below Benchmark": (a) conduct or commission a detailed root cause analysis per QA-AE-002 if not already conducted; (b) develop a formal response plan with measurable improvement targets and defined interventions; (c) assign a responsible lead; (d) establish monitoring frequency (minimum monthly); (e) escalate to the Governing Body in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report with the response plan and timeline. | Response plan within 30 calendar days; reported to Governing Body at next quarterly meeting. |
| 6.2.4 | QAPI Committee | For measures classified as "At or Above Benchmark": acknowledge strong performance and identify practices contributing to success for potential spread to other areas. Document in committee minutes. | At the committee meeting where data is presented. |

### 6.3 Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Include benchmarking summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body. The summary must include: (a) agency Star Rating and trend; (b) list of measures below benchmark with analysis status; (c) list of measures significantly below benchmark with response plan status; (d) measures showing improvement relative to benchmark; (e) recommendations for Governing Body action if needed. | Quarterly per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1 schedule. |
| 6.3.2 | Governing Body | Review benchmarking data and direct corrective action for any measure that has been significantly below benchmark for 2 consecutive quarters without demonstrated improvement. | At each quarterly meeting per GV-GB-001 Section 6.2.4. |

### 6.4 Annual Benchmark Integration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Incorporate benchmarking results into the annual QAPI plan development process per QA-PG-002. Specifically: (a) use benchmark data to identify quality priority areas for the upcoming plan year; (b) set QAPI plan indicator thresholds informed by national benchmarks; (c) use benchmark gaps as PIP selection criteria. | During annual planning cycle (October – December). |

### 6.5 Escalation

| Condition | Escalation Path | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Agency Star Rating declines to 3 stars or below. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and includes in Governing Body report. | Governing Body directs comprehensive improvement plan targeting lowest-performing measures. QAPI Committee implements with monthly reporting to Governing Body until rating improves. | Reported at next Governing Body meeting; improvement plan within 30 days. |
| Any publicly reported measure at or below 10th percentile. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator immediately. | Emergency QAPI Committee review within 5 business days. Root cause analysis and intensive response plan required. | RCA within 14 days; response plan within 30 days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Benchmark data compilation | Quarterly benchmark comparison report with all required data points per Section 6.1.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Committee analysis documentation | Meeting minutes documenting analysis and decisions per Section 6.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At each quarterly benchmarking review. |
| Response plans for significantly below benchmark measures | Written response plans per Section 6.2.3. | Assigned lead | QAPI records. | Within 30 days; tracked to resolution. |
| Governing Body benchmarking reports | Section of quarterly QAPI Performance Report per Section 6.3.1. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Benchmark comparison conducted quarterly. | Review of quarterly benchmark reports. | Reports completed within 30 days of CMS data release each quarter. |
| All measures below benchmark have documented analysis. | Review of committee minutes. | 100% of below-benchmark measures have documented analysis. |
| Response plans exist for significantly below benchmark measures. | Review of response plan documents. | Written plan within 30 days for 100% of significantly-below measures. |
| Benchmarking reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report and Governing Body minutes. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| Benchmark data informs annual QAPI plan. | Review of QAPI plan rationale and priority selection. | Benchmark references documented in plan. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask the agency to describe how it compares its performance to national benchmarks; (2) request documentation of benchmark analysis; (3) verify the agency acts on identified underperformance rather than simply tracking data; (4) verify the Governing Body is informed of benchmark standing; (5) verify publicly reported measures in poor standing are addressed.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Agency monitors only internal trends without external comparison. | Cannot assess absolute quality level; may be improving but still below industry standard. | Mandate quarterly external benchmarking per this policy. |
| Benchmark data available but never analyzed by committee. | Data unused; no quality improvement value. | Require committee presentation and documented analysis per Section 6.2. |
| Response to significant underperformance is delayed or absent. | Continued poor quality; potential adverse survey finding. | Enforce 30-day response plan requirement per Section 6.2.3. |

## 9–12. References, Training, Version Control, Appendices
References: 42 CFR § 484.65(b), (c); cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002, QA-PI-001, QA-PI-002, QA-AE-002, QA-SM-004, GV-GB-001.
Training: QAPI Coordinator trained on CMS data platforms; committee members trained on benchmark interpretation.
Version Control: Per EN-LC-001.
Appendices:
Appendix A: Quarterly Benchmark Comparison Report Template
Appendix B: Benchmark Response Plan Template
Appendix C: Benchmark Data Source Inventory

# QA-PI-004 — Data-Driven Decision Making
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-004 |
| Title | Data-Driven Decision Making |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy requires that all quality improvement initiatives, operational changes, and resource allocation decisions at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. be supported by documented data analysis rather than anecdotal observation, assumption, or convenience. Data-driven decision making is the foundational principle of the QAPI program and ensures that the agency's limited resources are directed toward interventions with the greatest evidence of impact. This policy operationalizes 42 CFR § 484.65(b), which requires the use of quality indicator data to monitor and improve care effectiveness.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Governing Body
The Administrator
The QAPI Coordinator and QAPI Committee
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All department heads and supervisors who propose or approve operational, clinical, or quality improvement changes
The Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 All quality improvement decisions — including PIP selection, corrective action development, policy modifications based on quality concerns, resource reallocation, process changes, and staff education priorities — shall be supported by documented data analysis.
4.2 Decisions presented to the QAPI Committee or Governing Body for approval must include: (a) the data source(s); (b) the analysis performed; (c) the findings; (d) the recommended action with rationale linked to findings.
4.3 Anecdotal observations and individual experiences are valued as input for generating hypotheses and identifying potential areas of concern. However, they shall not serve as the sole basis for resource allocation, process changes, or corrective actions without supporting data analysis.
4.4 The agency shall maintain data literacy among all QAPI Committee members and department heads through training on data interpretation, basic statistical concepts, and evidence-based decision-making practices.
4.5 Data integrity is a prerequisite for data-driven decisions. The agency shall ensure data accuracy, completeness, and timeliness through defined quality controls per CO-DC-001 and CL-OA-005.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Data-Driven Decision | A decision made based on the systematic collection, analysis, and interpretation of relevant quantitative and/or qualitative data, rather than assumption, anecdote, or convenience. |
| Data Analysis | The process of examining data to identify patterns, trends, correlations, and root causes that inform decision-making. Methods include trending, comparison to benchmarks, root cause analysis, statistical analysis, and stratification. |
| Anecdotal Observation | A report of an individual event or experience that has not been validated by systematic data collection or analysis. |
| Data Integrity | The accuracy, completeness, consistency, and reliability of data throughout its lifecycle. |
| Stratification | The process of separating data into subgroups (by clinician, discipline, diagnosis, payer, timeframe, etc.) to identify patterns not visible in aggregate data. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Decision Criteria and Documentation Requirements

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | All decision-makers within scope | When proposing a quality improvement action, process change, or resource allocation, prepare a Decision Support Summary that includes: (a) Issue Statement — concise description of the problem or opportunity; (b) Data Presented — specific data sources, sample size, timeframe, and methodology; (c) Analysis — description of how the data was analyzed (trending, stratification, comparison, RCA); (d) Findings — key data findings that support the proposed action; (e) Recommended Action — specific action proposed with rationale directly linked to data findings; (f) Expected Outcome — measurable outcome expected from the action; (g) Monitoring Plan — how the impact of the action will be measured. | At the time of proposal to QAPI Committee, Governing Body, or administrative leadership. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Committee / Governing Body | Evaluate proposals against the Decision Support Summary. Reject or table proposals that lack sufficient data support and request additional analysis before proceeding. Document the evaluation and decision in meeting minutes. | At the meeting where proposal is presented. |

### 6.2 Data Analysis Standards

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish minimum data analysis standards for QAPI decisions: (a) Trending — minimum 3 data points over time before identifying a trend; (b) Comparison — always compare to threshold, benchmark, or baseline; (c) Stratification — when aggregate data shows adverse trend, stratify by clinician, discipline, diagnosis, or other relevant variable to identify root cause; (d) Sample size — document sample size and acknowledge limitations when sample is small; (e) Timeliness — data must be current (within 60 days for clinical data, within 90 days for outcome data) to support current decisions. | At program establishment; standards reviewed annually. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | When anecdotal observations are reported as quality concerns, validate them with data before initiating corrective action. Validation process: (a) define the measurable dimension of the concern; (b) identify the data source; (c) collect data for a defined period (minimum 30 days); (d) analyze data; (e) present findings to QAPI Committee with recommendation. Exception: immediate patient safety threats may require immediate action with concurrent data validation. | Validation initiated within 7 business days of anecdotal report; data analysis completed within 45 days. |

### 6.3 Data Literacy and Training

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Provide data literacy training to all QAPI Committee members and department heads. Training must cover: (a) understanding data sources and their limitations; (b) basic trend interpretation; (c) benchmark comparison; (d) stratification concepts; (e) common data interpretation errors (e.g., small sample fallacy, correlation vs. causation); (f) how to prepare a Decision Support Summary. | Within 30 days of committee appointment; annual refresher. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Conduct annual assessment of data analysis quality by reviewing Decision Support Summaries submitted during the year. Identify common deficiencies and address through targeted education. | Annually during QAPI plan evaluation cycle. |

### 6.4 Escalation

| Condition | Escalation Path | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A quality improvement action is implemented without documented data support. | QAPI Coordinator identifies during review and notifies decision-maker and their supervisor. | Decision-maker required to retroactively document data support or reverse the action if unsupported. Documented in QAPI Committee minutes. Repeated occurrences reported to Administrator. | Notification within 7 business days; resolution within 30 days. |
| Immediate patient safety threat requires action before data analysis is complete. | Acting leader implements immediate safety intervention. | Immediate action is permitted and documented with notation "data validation pending." QAPI Coordinator ensures data validation is completed within 30 days and results reported to committee. | Immediate action with 30-day data validation. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Decision Support Summaries | Written summaries per Section 6.1.1 for all quality/operational proposals. | Proposing individual | QAPI records. | At time of proposal; retained 7 years. |
| Committee/Governing Body evaluation | Meeting minutes documenting evaluation of proposals against data standards. | QAPI Coordinator / GB Secretary | QAPI records; governance file. | At each meeting; retained 7 years. |
| Data analysis standards | Written standards document per Section 6.2.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At establishment; reviewed annually. |
| Anecdotal-to-data validation records | Documentation of validation process and findings per Section 6.2.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Per event. |
| Data literacy training records | Training attendance, content, and completion documentation. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; personnel files. | Per training event. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Quality improvement decisions supported by data. | Annual audit of Decision Support Summaries for a sample of QAPI decisions. | ≥ 90% of audited decisions have documented data support. |
| QAPI Committee evaluates data quality of proposals. | Review of committee minutes for documented evaluation. | Evidence of data evaluation documented at ≥ 80% of meetings where proposals are reviewed. |
| Data literacy training completed by all required staff. | Review of training records. | 100% of QAPI Committee members and department heads trained within required timeframes. |
| Anecdotal concerns validated with data before action. | Review of validation records. | 100% of anecdotal concerns validated before non-emergency corrective action. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask how the agency uses data to drive quality improvement; (2) review QAPI Committee minutes for evidence that data analysis precedes decisions; (3) look for documented data analysis in PIP charters and corrective action plans; (4) assess whether decisions are data-driven or anecdote-driven.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PIPs selected because "it seemed like a good idea" without data analysis. | Surveyor cites non-data-driven QAPI program. | Require Decision Support Summary for all PIP proposals. |
| Committee discusses data but minutes do not reflect how data informed decisions. | Surveyor cannot verify data-driven culture. | Train recorder to link specific data findings to specific decisions in minutes. |
| Anecdotal reports trigger immediate process changes without validation. | Non-evidence-based changes may harm quality or waste resources. | Enforce anecdotal validation process per Section 6.2.2 except for immediate safety threats. |

## 9–12. References, Training, Version Control, Appendices
References: 42 CFR § 484.65(b); cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002, QA-PI-001, QA-PI-002, QA-AE-002, CO-DC-001, CL-OA-005.
Training: Per Section 6.3.
Version Control: Per EN-LC-001.

Appendix A: Decision Support Summary Template
Appendix B: Data Analysis Standards Reference Guide
Appendix C: Anecdotal-to-Data Validation Worksheet
Appendix D: Policy Acknowledgment Form — QA-PI-004
# QA-PI-005 — Staff Competency Integration with QAPI
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-005 |
| Title | Staff Competency Integration with QAPI |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy links staff competency evaluation results to the QAPI program at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to ensure that individual and aggregate competency data are systematically used to identify training needs, inform performance improvement priorities, and drive system-level improvements. Competency gaps that remain unaddressed become quality gaps. This policy ensures that clinical competency is not evaluated in isolation (within HR) but is actively integrated into the agency's quality monitoring and improvement framework, consistent with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program scope and data use) and 42 CFR § 484.80 (home health aide competency requirements).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The HR Director
All QAPI Committee members
All department heads and clinical supervisors responsible for competency evaluation
All clinical staff subject to competency evaluation (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Staff competency evaluation results shall be reported to the QAPI Committee at least quarterly as a standing data input to the QAPI program.
4.2 The QAPI Coordinator and the Director of Nursing / HR Director shall collaborate to identify competency patterns — both strengths and deficiencies — that have implications for patient care quality, safety, and outcomes.
4.3 When competency evaluation data reveals a systemic pattern (defined as the same competency deficiency identified in 3 or more staff members within a 12-month period, or a single deficiency that correlates with an adverse event), the QAPI Committee shall determine whether a PIP, targeted education, process redesign, or other system-level intervention is warranted.
4.4 Competency evaluation focus areas shall be informed by QAPI data, including adverse event types, quality indicators below threshold, PIP focus areas, and CMS survey findings. The QAPI Coordinator shall provide input to the DON/HR annually on recommended competency evaluation focus areas for the upcoming year.
4.5 Individual competency deficiencies shall be addressed through the performance management and remediation process per HR-TD-003 and HR-ER-001. This policy governs only the aggregate analysis and system-level response within the QAPI framework.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Competency Evaluation | The systematic assessment of a staff member's knowledge, skills, and abilities to perform their job functions safely and effectively, per HR-TD-003. |
| Systemic Competency Pattern | A recurring competency deficiency identified in 3 or more staff members within a 12-month period, or a single deficiency correlated with an adverse event or quality indicator decline. |
| Competency-Quality Correlation | An identified relationship between a staff competency area and a quality indicator, adverse event type, or patient outcome measure. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Competency Data Reporting to QAPI

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Director of Nursing / HR Director | Compile and submit to the QAPI Coordinator a quarterly Competency Summary Report that includes: (a) total evaluations completed vs. required (compliance rate); (b) aggregate results by competency area (% passing/failing each area); (c) identified deficiency patterns; (d) remediation completion rates; (e) new hire competency evaluation results; (f) HHA competency evaluation results and supervisory visit findings; (g) correlation of deficiencies with clinical disciplines, service lines, or patient populations. | Quarterly; submitted at least 5 business days before the QAPI Committee meeting where it will be presented. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include the Competency Summary Report as a standing data input on the QAPI Committee agenda. Present alongside quality indicator data to identify correlations between competency and quality. | Quarterly at QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.2 Competency-Quality Correlation Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Analyze competency data alongside quality indicators, adverse event data, and patient outcome data to identify potential competency-quality correlations. Examples: (a) wound care competency deficiencies correlating with declining wound healing outcome rates; (b) OASIS competency deficiencies correlating with coding accuracy issues; (c) medication management competency deficiencies correlating with medication error incident reports; (d) fall prevention competency deficiencies correlating with increased fall rates. | Quarterly following receipt of Competency Summary Report. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Committee | Review the competency-quality correlation analysis. For each identified correlation: (a) validate the correlation with additional data if needed; (b) determine root cause (individual skill gap, training gap, process gap, resource gap); (c) determine the appropriate system-level response (targeted education, process redesign, PIP, supervision enhancement, tool/template modification). Document analysis and decision in committee minutes. | At the quarterly QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.3 System-Level Response

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Committee | When a systemic competency pattern (per Section 4.3) is identified, assign one of the following responses: (a) Targeted Education — develop and deliver focused training on the identified competency area, with post-training reassessment; (b) Process Redesign — modify the work process, template, or tool to reduce reliance on the deficient competency area; (c) PIP — initiate a formal PIP per QA-PI-001 if the competency pattern correlates with a significant quality gap; (d) Enhanced Supervision — increase supervisory oversight for the affected competency area. Assign a responsible lead and timeline. | At the QAPI Committee meeting where pattern is identified; response initiated within 30 calendar days. |
| 6.3.2 | Assigned lead | Implement the assigned response and report results to the QAPI Committee. Measure effectiveness by tracking: (a) competency reassessment pass rates; (b) quality indicator trend in the correlated area; (c) adverse event rate in the correlated area. | Per assigned timeline; progress reported monthly to committee. |

### 6.4 QAPI Input to Competency Focus Areas

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Annually provide written recommendations to the DON and HR Director regarding competency evaluation focus areas for the upcoming year, based on: (a) quality indicators that were below threshold; (b) PIP focus areas requiring staff skill development; (c) adverse event root cause analysis findings identifying competency-related contributing factors; (d) CMS survey findings; (e) new regulatory or clinical practice requirements. | Annually by November 15, to inform next year's competency evaluation plan. |
| 6.4.2 | Director of Nursing / HR Director | Incorporate QAPI-recommended focus areas into the annual competency evaluation plan per HR-TD-003. Document how QAPI input was used. | By December 31 for the upcoming year. |

### 6.5 Escalation

| Condition | Escalation Path | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Competency data not submitted to QAPI for 2 consecutive quarters. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator. | Administrator directs DON/HR Director to submit within 14 calendar days. Repeated failure reported to Governing Body. | Within 14 days. |
| Systemic competency pattern correlates with patient harm event. | QAPI Coordinator and DON escalate to Administrator and Governing Body. | Immediate targeted intervention; enhanced supervision pending remediation; reported to Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.4. | Immediate intervention; Governing Body notified within 72 hours. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Quarterly Competency Summary Report | Report per Section 6.1.1. | DON / HR Director | QAPI records. | Quarterly. |
| Competency-quality correlation analysis | Documented analysis per Section 6.2.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly. |
| Committee analysis and decisions | Meeting minutes documenting review and decisions per Section 6.2.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly. |
| System-level response plans | Documentation of assigned response per Section 6.3.1. | Assigned lead | QAPI records. | Per event; tracked to completion. |
| QAPI competency focus area recommendations | Written recommendations per Section 6.4.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Annually by November 15. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Competency data submitted to QAPI quarterly. | Review of Competency Summary Reports in QAPI records. | 4 reports per year; no gaps exceeding 1 quarter. |
| Correlation analysis performed. | Review of QAPI Coordinator analysis documentation. | Documented analysis for each quarterly report. |
| Systemic patterns addressed with system-level response. | Review of committee minutes and response plans. | 100% of identified systemic patterns have documented response within 30 days. |
| QAPI provides annual competency focus recommendations. | Review of written recommendations. | Submitted annually by November 15. |

### 8.2 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Competency evaluation is siloed in HR with no QAPI visibility. | Competency gaps not linked to quality outcomes; missed improvement opportunities. | Mandate quarterly reporting to QAPI per this policy. |
| Individual deficiencies addressed but systemic patterns ignored. | Underlying systems issues persist despite individual remediation. | Require systemic pattern analysis and system-level response per Section 6.3. |
| QAPI identifies training needs but competency plan does not incorporate them. | Disconnect between quality data and staff development. | Mandate annual QAPI input to competency focus areas per Section 6.4. |

# QA-PI-006 — Visit Utilization & LUPA Risk Management Program
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-006 |
| Title | Visit Utilization & LUPA Risk Management Program |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes a cross-functional QAPI program integrating clinical, scheduling, and financial data to monitor visit utilization patterns, identify Low Utilization Payment Adjustment (LUPA) risk episodes early, and implement coordinated interventions to ensure physician-ordered services are delivered as planned at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. LUPA episodes — those where the number of visits delivered falls below the CMS-defined threshold — represent both a clinical risk (patients not receiving ordered care) and a financial risk (reduced reimbursement). This policy ensures that visit utilization is managed as a quality of care issue, not merely a billing concern, consistent with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program scope), 42 CFR § 484.60 (care planning and coordination), and 42 CFR § 484.55 (comprehensive assessment).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All QAPI Committee members
All scheduling and intake staff
All clinical staff responsible for visit delivery (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs)
All clinical supervisors
Exclusion: This policy governs the QAPI monitoring and intervention framework for visit utilization. Individual billing procedures for LUPA episodes are governed by FN-BC-001 and FN-CM-005. Clinical visit frequency determination is governed by CL-CP-001 and CL-SD-025.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a structured, data-driven visit utilization monitoring program integrated within the QAPI framework that identifies episodes at risk of LUPA and coordinates clinical, scheduling, and financial interventions to ensure ordered services are delivered.
4.2 Visit utilization monitoring shall be treated as a patient care quality issue. Failure to deliver physician-ordered visits is a care delivery deficiency that affects clinical outcomes, not merely a revenue concern.
4.3 The agency shall maintain a real-time or near-real-time LUPA risk dashboard that identifies episodes approaching the LUPA visit threshold, enabling proactive intervention before the episode closes.
4.4 A cross-functional LUPA Prevention Team — comprising clinical, scheduling, and revenue cycle representatives — shall be designated to review LUPA risk episodes weekly and coordinate interventions.
4.5 All LUPA episodes that close shall be subject to a retrospective root cause analysis to determine whether the LUPA resulted from: (a) a clinically appropriate low-utilization case; (b) a scheduling failure; (c) a patient-driven factor (refusal, hospitalization, no-show); (d) a clinician-driven factor (missed visits, documentation gaps); or (e) a system failure (staffing shortage, communication breakdown).
4.6 LUPA rate shall be a standing quality indicator on the QAPI dashboard per QA-PI-002 with a defined performance threshold, and shall be reported to the Governing Body quarterly.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| LUPA (Low Utilization Payment Adjustment) | A CMS payment adjustment applied to a 30-day payment period when the number of visits delivered falls below the CMS-defined LUPA threshold for the applicable PDGM payment group. |
| LUPA Threshold | The CMS-defined minimum visit count for each PDGM payment group. Episodes with visits below this threshold receive per-visit payment rather than the full 30-day period payment. |
| LUPA Risk Episode | An active 30-day period where the number of visits delivered to date, projected against the remaining days in the period, indicates a likelihood of falling below the LUPA threshold. |
| Visit Utilization Rate | The percentage of physician-ordered visits actually delivered within each 30-day period. |
| Clinically Appropriate LUPA | A LUPA episode where the physician-ordered visit frequency was intentionally low due to clinical appropriateness (e.g., patient stabilized, limited skilled need), and the ordered visits were delivered as planned. |
| Preventable LUPA | A LUPA episode where the visit count fell below threshold due to scheduling failure, staffing shortage, clinician non-compliance, or system breakdown — not due to clinical appropriateness. |
| LUPA Prevention Team | The cross-functional team designated to monitor LUPA risk episodes and coordinate preventive interventions. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 LUPA Risk Monitoring System

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Establish and maintain a LUPA Risk Dashboard that identifies all active 30-day payment periods and calculates: (a) the applicable LUPA threshold for each period; (b) visits completed to date; (c) visits remaining to meet threshold; (d) days remaining in the period; (e) risk classification (Green = on track; Yellow = at risk if 1+ visits missed; Red = LUPA likely without immediate intervention). The dashboard must be updated no less frequently than every business day. | Dashboard operational within 60 days of policy effective date; updated daily. |
| 6.1.2 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Configure the LUPA Risk Dashboard to generate automated alerts when any episode enters Yellow or Red status. Alerts must be directed to: (a) the assigned clinician's supervisor; (b) the scheduling coordinator; (c) the LUPA Prevention Team lead. | Concurrent with dashboard establishment. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Include the LUPA rate as a standing quality indicator on the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002. Define the performance threshold: agency LUPA rate shall not exceed the national average LUPA rate as published by CMS, or an agency-specific threshold approved by the QAPI Committee, whichever is lower. | At policy effective date; threshold reviewed annually during QAPI plan development. |

### 6.2 LUPA Prevention Team Structure and Function

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Designate a LUPA Prevention Team with the following minimum membership: (a) LUPA Prevention Team Lead (clinical supervisor or QAPI Coordinator designee); (b) Scheduling Coordinator; (c) Revenue Cycle representative; (d) Director of Nursing or clinical designee. | Within 30 calendar days of policy effective date. |
| 6.2.2 | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | Convene a weekly LUPA Prevention Review meeting (maximum 30 minutes) to review: (a) all episodes currently in Yellow or Red status; (b) barriers to visit delivery for each at-risk episode; (c) specific interventions assigned for each at-risk episode; (d) LUPA episodes that closed since the prior meeting with root cause classification. | Weekly; minimum 48 meetings per calendar year. |
| 6.2.3 | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | Maintain a LUPA Prevention Action Log documenting for each at-risk episode: (a) patient identifier; (b) 30-day period dates; (c) LUPA threshold; (d) visits completed / remaining; (e) risk level; (f) identified barrier; (g) intervention assigned; (h) responsible party; (i) outcome (LUPA averted / LUPA occurred / episode transferred/discharged). | Updated at each weekly meeting; retained per CO-HP-007. |

### 6.3 Proactive Intervention for At-Risk Episodes

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Scheduling Coordinator | Upon receiving a Yellow or Red alert from the LUPA Risk Dashboard: (a) verify the visit schedule against physician orders; (b) identify any unscheduled ordered visits; (c) reschedule missed or cancelled visits within the current period; (d) communicate with the assigned clinician to confirm visit completion plan. | Within 1 business day of alert. |
| 6.3.2 | Clinical Supervisor | For Red status episodes: (a) personally review the case to confirm physician-ordered frequency is clinically appropriate; (b) if visits were missed due to patient factors (refusal, hospitalization, no-show), document the reason and determine whether a physician order change is appropriate; (c) if visits were missed due to clinician or scheduling factors, implement immediate corrective scheduling; (d) if the ordered frequency appears insufficient for the patient's clinical needs, consult with the physician regarding order modification. | Within 1 business day of Red classification. |
| 6.3.3 | Assigned Clinician | When notified of an at-risk episode in their caseload: (a) prioritize completion of remaining ordered visits; (b) document any patient-driven barriers (refusal documented in clinical record per CL-CD-001); (c) communicate scheduling conflicts to the scheduling coordinator immediately; (d) do not "front-load" visits solely to meet LUPA threshold if not clinically indicated — all visits must be clinically justified and documented. | Ongoing during the at-risk period. |

### 6.4 Retrospective LUPA Root Cause Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | For every closed LUPA episode, conduct a root cause classification. Classify each LUPA into one of the following categories: (a) Clinically Appropriate — ordered frequency was intentionally low; ordered visits were delivered; LUPA was expected and acceptable; (b) Patient-Driven — patient refused visits, was hospitalized, was unavailable, or was discharged early; agency made documented attempts to deliver ordered visits; (c) Scheduling/System Failure — visits were ordered but not scheduled, or scheduled but not delivered due to scheduling error, staffing shortage, or system breakdown; (d) Clinician-Driven — visits were scheduled but the clinician did not complete them (missed visits, late cancellations without rescheduling); (e) Documentation Gap — visits may have been delivered but documentation was incomplete or untimely, resulting in inability to confirm visit count. | Root cause classification completed within 7 calendar days of episode closure. |
| 6.4.2 | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | Document root cause classification on the LUPA Prevention Action Log and compile monthly summary data for QAPI Committee reporting. | Monthly compilation; presented to QAPI Committee. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Analyze LUPA root cause data for patterns. If any single root cause category accounts for more than 30% of preventable LUPAs in a quarter, recommend a targeted intervention or PIP to the QAPI Committee. | Quarterly analysis; presented at QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.5 QAPI Committee and Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Present LUPA data to the QAPI Committee monthly as part of the Quality Indicator Dashboard review. Report must include: (a) LUPA rate for the reporting period; (b) trend comparison to prior periods and threshold; (c) root cause distribution; (d) interventions implemented; (e) effectiveness of interventions. | Monthly at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.5.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include LUPA rate, trend, and root cause summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA rate exceeds threshold for 2 consecutive months. | LUPA Prevention Team Lead escalates to QAPI Committee. | QAPI Committee initiates formal corrective action analysis per QA-PI-002 Section 6.4. | At next QAPI Committee meeting. |
| LUPA rate exceeds threshold for 2 consecutive quarters. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to Governing Body in quarterly report. | Governing Body directs corrective action per GV-GB-001 Section 6.2.4.3. | At next Governing Body meeting. |
| Preventable LUPA rate exceeds 50% of total LUPAs in any month. | LUPA Prevention Team Lead notifies Administrator. | Administrator convenes review with DON, Scheduling, and Revenue Cycle within 5 business days. Action plan required within 14 calendar days. | Review within 5 business days; plan within 14 days. |
| Individual clinician responsible for 3+ preventable LUPAs in a rolling 6-month period. | Clinical Supervisor notifies DON. | DON reviews clinician's visit compliance pattern. Progressive performance management per HR-ER-002 if pattern reflects non-compliance rather than systemic barrier. | Within 14 calendar days of identification. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA Risk Dashboard | Daily dashboard with all active episodes, thresholds, and risk status. | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Revenue Cycle systems; accessible to LUPA Prevention Team. | Updated daily; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| LUPA Prevention Team roster | Current roster with member names, roles, and contact information. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated within 7 days of any change. |
| Weekly LUPA Prevention Review minutes | Summary of episodes reviewed, barriers identified, interventions assigned, and outcomes. | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | QAPI records. | Completed within 2 business days of each weekly meeting; retained 7 years. |
| LUPA Prevention Action Log | Detailed log per Section 6.2.3. | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | QAPI records. | Updated at each weekly meeting; retained 7 years. |
| LUPA root cause classifications | Classification per Section 6.4.1 for each closed LUPA episode. | LUPA Prevention Team Lead | QAPI records. | Within 7 calendar days of episode closure. |
| Monthly LUPA data report to QAPI Committee | LUPA rate, trend, root cause distribution, interventions. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Monthly at each committee meeting. |
| Quarterly LUPA report to Governing Body | LUPA summary within quarterly QAPI Performance Report. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed acknowledgment of this policy by all personnel within scope. | Each individual (completion); QAPI Coordinator (collection) | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new assignment. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA Risk Dashboard operational and updated daily. | Review of dashboard; spot-check update dates. | Dashboard current each business day. |
| LUPA Prevention Team meets weekly. | Review of meeting minutes. | 48+ meetings per calendar year. |
| Root cause classification completed for all closed LUPA episodes. | Review of LUPA Prevention Action Log. | 100% of closed LUPAs classified within 7 calendar days. |
| LUPA rate monitored as QAPI quality indicator. | Review of QAPI dashboard. | LUPA rate on dashboard; threshold defined; reported monthly. |
| LUPA data reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report and Governing Body minutes. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| Preventable LUPA patterns trigger corrective action. | Review of committee minutes for root cause pattern response. | Corrective action documented for any root cause exceeding 30% of preventable LUPAs per quarter. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors evaluating care coordination and quality will verify: (1) the agency monitors whether ordered visits are delivered; (2) missed visits are documented with reasons; (3) the agency has a process for ensuring patients receive planned care; (4) patterns of under-delivery are identified and addressed through the QAPI program; (5) LUPA management is framed as a quality issue, not solely a billing concern.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| LUPA monitoring is done only by billing/finance with no clinical involvement. | Missed clinical quality implications; surveyor concern about care delivery. | Require cross-functional team with clinical leadership per Section 6.2.1. |
| No retrospective analysis of why LUPAs occur. | Repeated preventable LUPAs; no system improvement. | Mandate root cause classification for every closed LUPA per Section 6.4. |
| Clinicians "front-load" visits to meet threshold without clinical justification. | Potential fraud concern; inflated visit counts without medical necessity. | Prohibit non-clinically-justified visits per Section 6.3.3; all visits must be documented as medically necessary. |
| LUPA rate tracked but never reported to QAPI Committee or Governing Body. | Data unused; no governance oversight. | Require monthly QAPI and quarterly Governing Body reporting per Section 6.5. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All LUPA Prevention Team members shall receive orientation to this policy, the LUPA Risk Dashboard, root cause classification methodology, and intervention protocols within 14 calendar days of appointment. Orientation shall be conducted by the QAPI Coordinator or CFO designee.
10.2 All clinical staff (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs) shall receive training during annual mandatory training (per HR-TD-001) on: (a) the relationship between visit delivery and patient outcomes; (b) their responsibility to deliver ordered visits and document barriers; (c) the prohibition against non-clinically-justified visit front-loading; (d) how to report scheduling conflicts or barriers to the scheduling coordinator.
10.3 All scheduling and intake staff shall receive training on LUPA risk identification, dashboard alert response procedures, and their role in proactive rescheduling within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter.
10.4 All personnel within scope of this policy shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
10.5 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a training tracking log documenting all LUPA-related training including attendee name, topic, date, trainer, and completion status.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope, within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting. Non-substantive revisions do not require re-acknowledgment.
