# QA-PI-007 — Staff Competency Impact on Patient Outcomes
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-PI-007__ |
| Title | Staff Competency Impact on Patient Outcomes |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | PI — Performance Improvement |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL__ |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted__ |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10__ |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy requires Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to establish a measurable linkage between staff competency evaluation results and patient outcome data. While QA-PI-005 establishes the integration of competency data into the QAPI program for pattern identification and system-level response, this policy goes further by mandating specific metrics that demonstrate training effectiveness, quantify the relationship between competency performance and clinical outcomes, and trigger targeted education and performance improvement initiatives when gaps are identified. This policy ensures the agency can demonstrate to CMS surveyors that its competency program does not merely "check the box" but actively drives measurable quality improvement, consistent with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI data-driven improvement) and 42 CFR § 484.80 (home health aide training and competency).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The HR Director
All QAPI Committee members
All clinical supervisors responsible for competency evaluation and outcome monitoring
All clinical staff subject to competency evaluation (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs)
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain defined metrics that link staff competency evaluation performance to patient outcome data, enabling the agency to demonstrate that its training and competency programs produce measurable quality improvement.
4.2 The agency shall track at least three (3) Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics annually, each pairing a competency evaluation area with a corresponding patient outcome indicator.
4.3 When competency-outcome linkage analysis reveals a statistically or clinically meaningful correlation between a competency gap and an adverse outcome trend, the QAPI Committee shall initiate a targeted intervention within 30 calendar days.
4.4 Training effectiveness shall be measured not only by competency evaluation pass rates but by subsequent improvement in the correlated patient outcome indicator following education or remediation.
4.5 Competency-outcome linkage data shall be reported to the Governing Body annually as part of the year-end QAPI evaluation per QA-PG-002.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Competency-Outcome Linkage Metric | A defined pairing of a staff competency evaluation area with a corresponding patient outcome indicator, enabling measurement of the relationship between staff skill and clinical results. |
| Training Effectiveness | The degree to which a training or education intervention produces measurable improvement in both staff competency assessment scores and the correlated patient outcome indicator. |
| Clinically Meaningful Correlation | A relationship between a competency area and a patient outcome where the magnitude and direction of the association suggest that competency performance influences patient results, as determined by the QAPI Committee based on data analysis. |
| Pre-/Post-Training Outcome Comparison | The comparison of patient outcome data for a defined period before a training intervention with the same indicator for a defined period following the intervention, to assess training effectiveness. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Competency-Outcome Linkage Metric Selection

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Director of Nursing | Select a minimum of 3 Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics for the current plan year. Each metric must pair a specific competency evaluation area with a measurable patient outcome indicator. Examples include but are not limited to: (a) Wound care competency evaluation scores ↔ Wound healing improvement rate; (b) Medication management competency scores ↔ Medication error rate / adverse drug event rate; (c) Fall prevention competency scores ↔ Patient fall rate; (d) OASIS accuracy competency scores ↔ OASIS error rate / coding accuracy rate; (e) Pain assessment competency scores ↔ Pain improvement outcome; (f) Infection control competency scores ↔ Infection rate; (g) HHA competency evaluation results ↔ HHA supervisory visit finding rate. | Annually during QAPI plan development cycle (by December 15); reviewed and updated annually.__ |
| 6.1.__ | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the selected Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics. For each metric, document: (a) the competency area and evaluation tool; (b) the patient outcome indicator and data source; (c) the baseline competency performance; (d) the baseline outcome performance; (e) the expected direction of correlation; (f) the analysis frequency. Document approval in committee minutes. | At QAPI Committee meeting during annual planning. |

### 6.2 Data Collection and Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Director of Nursing / HR Director | Provide competency evaluation results to the QAPI Coordinator per QA-PI-005 Section 6.1.1 (quarterly Competency Summary Report). Results must be disaggregated by competency area to enable metric-level analysis. | Quarterly. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | For each Competency-Outcome Linkage Metric, compile and analyze: (a) aggregate competency evaluation scores for the competency area (pass rate, average score, score distribution); (b) the corresponding patient outcome indicator data for the same period; (c) trend comparison over at least 4 quarters to identify directional correlation; (d) stratification by discipline, unit, or clinician group where sample size permits. | Quarterly following receipt of competency data and outcome data. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Determine whether a clinically meaningful correlation exists. The QAPI Coordinator shall present findings to the QAPI Committee with a recommendation: (a) No significant correlation identified — continue monitoring; (b) Positive correlation identified (higher competency → better outcomes) — document as evidence of training effectiveness; (c) Negative correlation identified (competency gap correlates with adverse outcome trend) — recommend targeted intervention per Section 6.3. | At quarterly QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.3 Targeted Intervention for Identified Gaps

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.__ | QAPI Committee | When a negative correlation is identified (competency gap correlating with adverse outcome trend), direct one or more of the following interventions: (a) Targeted Education — develop and deliver focused training on the specific competency area; (b) Competency Remediation — require re-evaluation with skills validation for affected staff per HR-TD-003; (c) Process Improvement — modify the clinical process, tool, or template related to the competency area to reduce reliance on the deficient skill; (d) PIP — initiate a formal PIP per QA-PI-001 if the gap is significant and persistent; (e) Enhanced Supervision — increase supervisory observation frequency for the affected competency area. Assign responsible lead, timeline, and measurable goal__ | Intervention initiated within 30 calendar days of identification. |
| 6.3.2 | Assigned Lead | Implement the intervention and establish a pre-/post-training outcome comparison: (a) define the pre-training period (minimum 3 months of outcome data before intervention); (b) implement the training or intervention; (c) define the post-training measurement period (minimum 3 months of outcome data after intervention); (d) compare pre-/post outcome data to assess training effectiveness. | Per assigned timeline; post-training measurement completed within 6 months of intervention. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Report pre-/post-training outcome comparison results to the QAPI Committee. If the outcome indicator improved following the intervention, document as evidence of training effectiveness. If no improvement, recommend escalated intervention or PIP. | At QAPI Committee meeting following completion of post-training period. |

### 6.4 Annual Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Prepare an annual Competency-Outcome Linkage Report that includes: (a) each metric tracked during the year with analysis findings; (b) correlations identified (positive and negative); (c) interventions implemented and pre-/post-training comparisons; (d) demonstrated training effectiveness for each intervention; (e) recommendations for the upcoming year's metrics. | Completed by November 30 as part of the QAPI year-end evaluation per QA-PG-002.__ |
| 6.4.__ | QAPI Coordinator | Include the annual Competency-Outcome Linkage Report in the year-end QAPI evaluation submitted to the Governing Body. | Per QA-PG-002 submission schedule. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Competency data insufficient to perform linkage analysis (data not provided or sample too small). | QAPI Coordinator notifies DON and HR Director. | DON/HR Director ensures data submission per QA-PI-005. If sample size is insufficient, QAPI Committee documents limitation and adjusts metric or extends measurement period. | Within 30 calendar days. |
| Negative correlation persists despite targeted intervention (outcome does not improve after 6 months). | QAPI Coordinator escalates to QAPI Committee as high-priority__ | QAPI Committee escalates to Governing Body with recommendation for enhanced intervention, resource allocation, or PIP. | At next Governing Body quarterly meeting. |
| No Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics defined for the plan year. | QAPI Coordinator identifies gap during planning. | Metrics must be defined before QAPI plan approval per Section 6.1. QAPI plan shall not be submitted to Governing Body without this component__ | Resolved before QAPI plan submission (December 15). |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Competency-Outcome Linkage Metric definitions | Documented metric pairings per Section 6.1.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; QAPI plan. | Annually during planning; updated as needed. |
| Quarterly analysis reports | Analysis findings per Section 6.2.2__ | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly. |
| QAPI Committee analysis and decisions | Meeting minutes documenting metric review and decisions. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly.__ |
| Pre-/post-training outcome comparisons | Data analysis per Section 6.3.2. | Assigned Lead | QAPI records. | Per intervention timeline.__ |
| Annual Competency-Outcome Linkage Report | Written report per Section 6.4.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; Governance file. | By November 30 annually. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator (collection). | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Minimum 3 Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics defined annually. | Review of QAPI plan and metric documentation. | 3+ metrics defined and approved each plan year. |
| Quarterly analysis performed. | Review of analysis reports and committee minutes. | Analysis documented quarterly for each metric. |
| Identified gaps trigger intervention within 30 days. | Review of committee minutes and intervention documentation. | 100% of identified negative correlations have intervention within 30 days. |
| Pre-/post-training comparisons completed. | Review of comparison data. | Comparison completed for each intervention. |
| Annual report submitted to Governing Body. | Review of year-end evaluation. | Included in annual evaluation per QA-PG-002. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask how the agency uses competency data to improve patient outcomes; (2) look for evidence that training leads to measurable improvement, not just completion; (3) verify the QAPI program integrates competency monitoring with outcome monitoring; (4) assess whether the agency can demonstrate training effectiveness with data.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Competency evaluations completed but never linked to outcomes. | Training viewed as "check-the-box" with no quality improvement value__ | Mandate Competency-Outcome Linkage Metrics per this policy. |
| Training delivered but no pre-/post-outcome comparison. | Cannot demonstrate training effectiveness. | Require pre-/post-training outcome comparison for every intervention per Section 6.3.2.__ |
| Metrics defined but data never analyzed. | Metrics exist on paper only__ | Require quarterly analysis and committee review per Sections 6.2 and 6.3. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 The QAPI Coordinator and Director of Nursing shall be trained on competency-outcome linkage methodology, pre-/post-training comparison design, and data interpretation within 30 calendar days of policy effective date.
10.2 All QAPI Committee members shall receive annual training on competency-outcome linkage metric review and interpretation.
10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version is valid. Superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body.

# QA-AE-001 — Adverse Event Identification & Reporting
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-AE-001 |
| Title | Adverse Event Identification & Reporting |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | AE — Adverse Events & Corrective Action |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines what constitutes an adverse event, near-miss, and sentinel event at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., establishes mandatory reporting requirements, defines investigation expectations, and ensures that adverse event data is systematically captured, analyzed, and used to drive quality improvement through the QAPI program. Adverse event identification and reporting is the foundation of patient safety. This policy ensures the agency satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program data use and patient safety), 42 CFR § 484.60 (quality of care), and applicable California state mandatory reporting requirements.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All agency staff — clinical, administrative, contracted, and per diem
All QAPI Committee members
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The Compliance Officer
The Risk Manager
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a non-punitive adverse event reporting culture that encourages all staff to report adverse events, near-misses, and safety concerns without fear of retaliation.
4.2 All adverse events, near-misses, and sentinel events occurring during or related to agency services shall be reported, documented, and investigated per the procedures in this policy.
4.3 Adverse event reporting is mandatory for all agency staff. Failure to report a known adverse event is a violation of this policy and may result in disciplinary action per HR-ER-002.
4.4 Adverse event data shall be a standing data input to the QAPI program. The QAPI Committee shall review adverse event trends monthly and the Governing Body shall receive a summary quarterly.
4.5 Sentinel events shall be reported to the Governing Body within 72 hours and shall trigger a mandatory root cause analysis per QA-AE-002.
4.6 The agency shall comply with all applicable California state mandatory reporting requirements for adverse events.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Adverse Event | An undesirable event that occurs during or as a result of care provided by the agency that results in harm to the patient. Includes events that were preventable and those that were not. |
| Near-Miss | An event that had the potential to cause harm but did not reach the patient or did not result in harm due to timely intervention or chance. |
| Sentinel Event | An adverse event that results in: (a) patient death; (b) permanent loss of function not related to the natural course of the patient's illness; (c) serious physical or psychological injury; or (d) any event the agency defines as sentinel based on severity. |
| Incident | A broader term encompassing adverse events, near-misses, safety hazards, property damage, staff injuries, and any unplanned occurrence that could affect patient or staff safety. |
| Reportable Event Categories | Categories of events requiring mandatory reporting per this policy (see Section 6.1.2). |
| Non-Punitive Reporting Culture | An organizational commitment to treating adverse event reports as opportunities for system improvement rather than as grounds for individual blame, except in cases of willful misconduct, criminal activity, or repeated negligence. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Adverse Event Classification and Reportable Events

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain a written Adverse Event Classification Guide that defines the agency's adverse event categories, severity levels, and reporting requirements. The guide must be accessible to all staff and included in orientation training. | At policy effective date; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | All Staff | Report the following categories of events using the Adverse Event Report Form (Appendix A): (a) Patient falls (with or without injury); (b) Medication errors (wrong drug, wrong dose, wrong route, wrong time, omission); (c) Adverse drug reactions; (d) Hospital admissions/emergency department visits during an active episode; (e) Infections acquired during the episode of care; (f) Skin breakdown / pressure injury development; (g) Patient elopement or wandering; (h) Complaints of abuse, neglect, or exploitation; (i) Unanticipated decline in patient condition; (j) Equipment malfunction resulting in potential or actual harm; (k) Suicide attempt or ideation; (l) Patient death during active episode; (m) Near-misses in any of the above categories; (n) Any other event the staff member reasonably believes could have or did result in patient harm. | See Section 6.2 for reporting timeframes. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Assign severity levels to each reported event using the following scale: Level 1 — Near-Miss (no harm reached patient); Level 2 — Minor Harm (temporary, resolved without intervention); Level 3 — Moderate Harm (required intervention but resolved without permanent effect); Level 4 — Serious Harm (permanent harm or required significant intervention); Level 5 — Sentinel Event (death, permanent loss of function, or agency-defined sentinel criteria). | Within 2 business days of receiving the report. |

### 6.2 Reporting Procedures

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Discovering Staff Member | Immediately ensure patient safety — provide or arrange for any necessary emergency care. | Immediately upon discovery. |
| 6.2.2 | Discovering Staff Member | Notify the patient's physician of any adverse event that has the potential to affect the patient's plan of care or clinical status. Document the notification in the clinical record. | Within 1 hour of discovery for Level 3–5 events; within 4 hours for Level 1–2 events or by end of the visit. |
| 6.2.3 | Discovering Staff Member | Notify the clinical supervisor / Director of Nursing verbally. | Within 1 hour for Level 3–5 events; within 4 hours for Level 1–2 events. |
| 6.2.4 | Discovering Staff Member | Complete the Adverse Event Report Form (Appendix A) with: (a) patient identification; (b) date, time, and location of event; (c) description of event (factual, objective, without opinion or blame); (d) patient status following event; (e) immediate actions taken; (f) physician notification (date, time, name of physician, response); (g) names of all persons present or involved; (h) reporting staff member name, title, and signature. Do NOT document the Adverse Event Report Form in the patient's clinical record — the form is maintained separately. Document the clinical facts of the event in the clinical record per standard documentation practice. | Adverse Event Report Form submitted within 24 hours of event discovery. |
| 6.2.5 | Director of Nursing | Review the Adverse Event Report Form for completeness. Forward to the QAPI Coordinator. For Level 4–5 events, immediately notify the Administrator, QAPI Coordinator, and Risk Manager. | DON review within 24 hours of receipt; immediate notification for Level 4–5. |
| 6.2.6 | QAPI Coordinator | Log the event in the Adverse Event Tracking Log (Appendix B) with severity classification. Initiate investigation per Section 6.3. | Within 2 business days of receipt. |

### 6.3 Investigation and Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | For Level 1–3 events: Conduct or assign an initial review to determine: (a) immediate cause; (b) contributing factors; (c) whether the event was preventable; (d) whether the event represents an isolated incident or potential pattern; (e) immediate corrective actions needed. Document findings on the Adverse Event Investigation Summary (Appendix C). | Investigation completed within 14 calendar days of event report. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator / Risk Manager | For Level 4–5 events (Sentinel Events): Initiate a formal Root Cause Analysis per QA-AE-002 within 48 hours. Notify the Governing Body within 72 hours per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.4. | RCA initiated within 48 hours; Governing Body notified within 72 hours. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator | For all events requiring corrective action: develop or direct development of a Corrective Action Plan per QA-AE-003. | CAP initiated within 14 calendar days of investigation completion. |

### 6.4 Trending and QAPI Integration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Compile and present an Adverse Event Summary to the QAPI Committee at each monthly meeting. The summary must include: (a) total events reported by category and severity; (b) comparison to prior month and 12-month trend; (c) open investigations and their status; (d) root cause analysis findings (for completed RCAs); (e) corrective action plan status; (f) patterns or trends identified; (g) recommendations for system-level improvement. | Monthly at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Committee | Review adverse event data and: (a) acknowledge isolated events with adequate corrective response; (b) identify patterns requiring system-level intervention; (c) direct PIPs for persistent patterns per QA-PI-001; (d) escalate to Governing Body per QA-PG-003 Section 6.5 when warranted. | At each monthly meeting. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Include adverse event summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. | Quarterly. |

### 6.5 External Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator / Compliance Officer | Comply with all applicable California state mandatory reporting requirements. Determine whether the event requires reporting to: (a) California Department of Public Health (CDPH); (b) Adult Protective Services / Child Protective Services (for suspected abuse/neglect per CL-PR-006); (c) CMS (if the event triggers a reporting obligation under CoP); (d) law enforcement (if criminal activity suspected); (e) the patient's physician (per Section 6.2.2). Document all external reports made. | Per applicable regulatory timeframes; typically within 24–72 hours depending on reporting requirement. |
| 6.5.2 | Compliance Officer | Maintain a log of all external adverse event reports submitted, including the reporting agency, date, and content of report. | At the time of each external report. |

### 6.6 Non-Punitive Reporting Culture

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | Administrator / QAPI Coordinator | Communicate to all staff — during orientation, annual training, and in policy acknowledgment — that adverse event reporting is non-punitive. Staff who report in good faith shall not be subject to retaliation or discipline for the act of reporting, per CO-CP-005. | Ongoing; reinforced annually. |
| 6.6.2 | Administrator | Ensure that disciplinary action related to an adverse event is directed at the underlying conduct (e.g., willful deviation from protocol, impairment, falsification), not at the act of reporting. Document this distinction in any disciplinary action. | Ongoing. |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sentinel event occurs. | DON notifies Administrator, QAPI Coordinator, and Risk Manager immediately. | RCA per QA-AE-002; Governing Body notified within 72 hours; external reporting per Section 6.5. | Immediate. |
| Staff member fails to report a known adverse event. | Supervisor notifies DON and QAPI Coordinator. | DON investigates and determines whether failure was intentional or due to knowledge gap. If intentional, disciplinary action per HR-ER-002. If knowledge gap, targeted training within 7 calendar days. | Within 14 calendar days of discovery. |
| Adverse event reporting rate drops significantly (>30% decline from prior quarter). | QAPI Coordinator flags at QAPI Committee meeting. | Committee investigates cause — possible under-reporting culture, fear of retaliation, or training gap. Address with communication campaign, training, or leadership intervention. | Within 30 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adverse event reports filed timely. | Audit of report submission dates vs. event dates. | ≥ 95% of reports submitted within 24 hours. |
| All reported events logged and classified. | Review of Tracking Log vs. submitted reports. | 100% of submitted reports appear in log within 2 business days. |
| Investigations completed timely. | Review of investigation completion dates. | Level 1–3 within 14 days; Level 4–5 RCA per QA-AE-002. |
| Adverse events reviewed by QAPI Committee monthly. | Review of committee minutes. | Adverse event summary presented at 100% of monthly meetings. |
| Sentinel events reported to Governing Body within 72 hours. | Review of Governing Body notification records. | 100% of sentinel events reported within 72 hours. |
| Non-punitive culture communicated. | Review of training records and staff acknowledgments. | Training documented during orientation and annually for 100% of staff. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) request the agency's adverse event policy and reporting forms; (2) review the Adverse Event Tracking Log for evidence of ongoing event capture; (3) verify events are investigated with documented findings; (4) verify events are integrated into the QAPI program; (5) look for trending and pattern analysis; (6) assess whether the agency responds to patterns with corrective action; (7) verify sentinel events are reported to the Governing Body.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adverse events documented in clinical record but not reported through the adverse event reporting system. | Events not captured for QAPI trending; surveyor sees events in records but no investigation evidence. | Train all staff to complete Adverse Event Report Form separately from clinical documentation. |
| Non-punitive culture not established; staff fear reporting. | Under-reporting; hidden safety risks. | Reinforce non-punitive culture at orientation and annually; monitor reporting rates. |
| Events reported but no investigation or corrective action. | Surveyor cites failure to act on identified safety issues. | Mandate investigation and CAP per Sections 6.3 and 6.4. |
| Near-misses not captured. | Lost opportunity for proactive prevention. | Include near-misses in reportable event categories per Section 6.1.2. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All agency staff (clinical, administrative, contracted, and per diem) shall receive adverse event reporting training during initial orientation per HR-TA-005. Training must cover: (a) the definition of adverse events, near-misses, and sentinel events; (b) the reportable event categories; (c) how to complete the Adverse Event Report Form; (d) reporting timeframes; (e) physician notification requirements; (f) the non-punitive reporting culture; (g) consequences of failure to report.
10.2 Annual refresher training on adverse event reporting shall be included in mandatory annual training per HR-TD-001.
10.3 All QAPI Committee members shall receive training on adverse event investigation, trending, and QAPI integration within 14 calendar days of committee appointment.
10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
10.5 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a training tracking log for all adverse event reporting training.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version is valid. Superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body.

# QA-AE-002 — Root Cause Analysis Process
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-AE-002 |
| Title | Root Cause Analysis Process |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | AE — Adverse Events & Corrective Action |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the methodology, team composition, documentation requirements, and completion standards for conducting Root Cause Analyses (RCAs) at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. An RCA is a structured, retrospective investigative process used to identify the fundamental systemic cause(s) of adverse events, sentinel events, or persistent quality failures — moving beyond the immediate or apparent cause to uncover the underlying process, system, or organizational factors that allowed the event to occur. RCA is mandatory for all Level 4 (Serious Harm) and Level 5 (Sentinel Event) adverse events per QA-AE-001, and may be directed by the QAPI Committee for any event or pattern deemed significant. This policy supports compliance with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program effectiveness and data use) and 42 CFR § 484.60 (quality of care).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
All individuals appointed to RCA teams
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The Risk Manager
The Compliance Officer
All QAPI Committee members
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall conduct a formal Root Cause Analysis for all Level 4 (Serious Harm) and Level 5 (Sentinel Event) adverse events as classified per QA-AE-001.
4.2 The QAPI Committee may direct an RCA for any event, pattern, or systemic failure it determines warrants formal root cause investigation, regardless of severity classification.
4.3 RCAs shall be conducted by a multidisciplinary team using a structured methodology that focuses on systems and processes, not individual blame.
4.4 Each RCA must produce identified root cause(s), contributing factors, and a Corrective Action Plan (per QA-AE-003) with measurable outcomes and defined accountability.
4.5 RCAs shall be completed within 45 calendar days of event identification. For sentinel events, the preliminary findings must be available to the Governing Body within 14 calendar days.
4.6 RCA findings and corrective actions shall be tracked to completion and monitored for effectiveness through the QAPI program.
4.7 RCA documents are quality improvement records and shall be maintained confidentially per applicable state peer review protections and agency policy.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Root Cause Analysis (RCA) | A structured, retrospective analysis that identifies the fundamental cause(s) of an adverse event or systemic failure by systematically examining the sequence of events, contributing factors, and underlying system conditions. |
| Root Cause | The most fundamental reason an event occurred — the factor that, if corrected, would prevent recurrence. Root causes are typically process, system, or organizational factors rather than individual human errors. |
| Contributing Factor | A condition or circumstance that, while not the primary cause, increased the likelihood of the event occurring or worsened its outcome. |
| RCA Team | The multidisciplinary group assigned to conduct the RCA, led by a designated RCA Lead. |
| 5 Whys | A questioning technique used in RCA to drill below surface causes by repeatedly asking "why?" (typically 5 iterations) to reach the root cause. |
| Fishbone Diagram (Ishikawa) | A visual tool used to categorize potential causes of an event across domains (e.g., people, process, equipment, environment, materials, management). |
| Corrective Action Plan (CAP) | The structured plan developed in response to RCA findings to address root cause(s) and prevent recurrence, per QA-AE-003. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 RCA Trigger and Initiation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Initiate an RCA when: (a) a Level 4 or Level 5 adverse event occurs (mandatory); (b) the QAPI Committee directs an RCA for a lower-severity event or pattern; (c) a quality indicator falls below threshold for 3 consecutive periods without improvement despite corrective action; (d) a CMS survey identifies a Condition-level deficiency. | Within 48 hours of event identification (Level 4–5) or QAPI Committee directive. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Designate an RCA Lead. The RCA Lead must: (a) have training in RCA methodology; (b) not have been directly involved in the event under investigation; (c) have authority to access all relevant records, data, and personnel. | At time of RCA initiation. |
| 6.1.3 | RCA Lead | Assemble the RCA Team. The team must include: (a) RCA Lead (chair); (b) at least one clinician from the discipline most closely related to the event; (c) at least one individual with process/systems knowledge relevant to the event; (d) a representative not involved in the event (outside perspective); (e) the Risk Manager or designee (for Level 4–5 events). Team may include additional subject matter experts as needed. Team members must not include the individual(s) primarily involved in the event, to preserve objectivity; however, involved individuals shall be interviewed. | Within 5 calendar days of initiation. |

### 6.2 RCA Investigation Methodology

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | RCA Team | Gather all relevant information including: (a) Adverse Event Report Form; (b) clinical record; (c) physician orders; (d) scheduling records; (e) staffing records; (f) equipment logs; (g) policy/procedure relevant to the event; (h) training records of involved staff; (i) prior adverse event history for the patient and/or involved staff; (j) any other data sources relevant to the event. | Within 10 calendar days of team assembly. |
| 6.2.2 | RCA Team | Conduct interviews with all individuals involved in or with knowledge of the event. Interviews shall be conducted individually, in a non-punitive manner, using open-ended questions. Document interview summaries. | Within 14 calendar days of team assembly. |
| 6.2.3 | RCA Team | Construct a detailed timeline of the event from the earliest relevant antecedent to the final outcome, including all actions taken, decisions made, and communications exchanged. | During the investigation phase. |
| 6.2.4 | RCA Team | Apply structured RCA methodology to identify root cause(s). Acceptable methodologies include: (a) 5 Whys Analysis — iteratively asking "why?" to move from apparent cause to root cause; (b) Fishbone (Ishikawa) Diagram — categorizing potential causes across domains (people, process, equipment, environment, materials, management); (c) Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA) — for process-related failures; (d) Barrier Analysis — identifying what barriers should have prevented the event and why they failed. At least one structured methodology must be documented. | During the investigation phase. |
| 6.2.5 | RCA Team | Identify: (a) Root Cause(s) — the fundamental systemic factor(s) that, if corrected, would prevent recurrence; (b) Contributing Factors — conditions that increased likelihood or severity; (c) System Strengths — factors that mitigated or limited the event's impact. Root causes must be stated in terms of systems and processes, not in terms of individual fault (e.g., "No standardized medication reconciliation process at SOC" rather than "Nurse failed to reconcile medications"). | During the investigation phase. |

### 6.3 RCA Report

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | RCA Lead | Prepare the RCA Report (Appendix A) documenting: (a) event summary; (b) RCA team composition; (c) data sources reviewed; (d) interviews conducted; (e) event timeline; (f) methodology used; (g) analysis findings; (h) identified root cause(s) with supporting evidence; (i) contributing factors; (j) system strengths identified; (k) recommended corrective actions with rationale, responsible parties, and measurable outcomes; (l) recommended monitoring plan to verify effectiveness. | Draft report completed within 30 calendar days of RCA initiation. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the RCA Report at the next QAPI Committee meeting. Committee may request additional investigation, modify recommendations, or approve as submitted. Document review in committee minutes. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following report completion; within 45 calendar days of event identification. |
| 6.3.3 | RCA Lead | For sentinel events: prepare a preliminary findings summary for the Governing Body within 14 calendar days of event identification. The summary must include: (a) event description; (b) initial findings; (c) anticipated corrective actions; (d) expected RCA completion date. | Preliminary summary to Governing Body within 14 calendar days. |
| 6.3.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Submit the final approved RCA Report to the Governing Body as part of the quarterly QAPI Performance Report. For sentinel events, provide the full report at the next Governing Body meeting following committee approval. | Per quarterly report schedule; sentinel event reports at next Governing Body meeting. |

### 6.4 Corrective Action Development and Tracking

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Initiate development of a Corrective Action Plan per QA-AE-003 based on the RCA Report's recommended corrective actions. | Within 14 calendar days of RCA Report approval. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Track corrective action implementation and effectiveness through the QAPI Action Item Tracker and report status to the QAPI Committee monthly. | Monthly until CAP closure. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Verify effectiveness of corrective actions by monitoring the relevant quality indicator(s) or outcome data for a minimum of 3 months following implementation. If the root cause condition recurs, reopen the investigation or initiate a new RCA. | 3-month effectiveness monitoring period. |

### 6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RCA not completed within 45 calendar days. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator. | Administrator reviews cause of delay and allocates additional resources. Extension may be granted for complex investigations but must not exceed 60 calendar days total. | Extension approved within 5 business days. |
| RCA team unable to identify root cause. | RCA Lead reports to QAPI Committee. | Committee may: (a) expand team to include external expertise; (b) direct additional data collection; (c) document the limitation and implement best available corrective actions. | Within 14 calendar days of team report. |
| Corrective action fails to prevent recurrence. | QAPI Coordinator reports recurrence to QAPI Committee. | Committee directs new or enhanced investigation. If recurrence involves patient harm, escalate to Governing Body. | At next QAPI Committee meeting; Governing Body if harm occurred. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RCA initiation record | Documentation of trigger, date, and RCA Lead assignment. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At initiation. |
| RCA Team roster | Team composition with names, titles, and roles. | RCA Lead | QAPI records (RCA file). | Within 5 days of initiation. |
| Interview summaries | Documented summaries of all interviews conducted. | RCA Lead | QAPI records (RCA file — confidential). | During investigation. |
| RCA Report | Complete report per Section 6.3.1 (Appendix A). | RCA Lead | QAPI records (RCA file — confidential). | Draft within 30 days; approved within 45 days. |
| Sentinel event preliminary summary | Summary per Section 6.3.3 for Governing Body. | RCA Lead | Governance file; QAPI records. | Within 14 days of sentinel event. |
| QAPI Committee review documentation | Meeting minutes documenting RCA review and approval. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At committee meeting. |
| Corrective Action Plan | Per QA-AE-003. | QAPI Coordinator / Assigned Lead | QAPI records. | Within 14 days of RCA approval. |
| Effectiveness monitoring data | Outcome data for 3-month post-implementation period. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | 3 months post-implementation. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RCA conducted for all Level 4–5 events. | Review of Adverse Event Tracking Log vs. RCA records. | 100% of Level 4–5 events have documented RCA. |
| RCA completed within 45 calendar days. | Review of RCA initiation and completion dates. | 100% of RCAs completed within 45 days (60 days with documented extension). |
| RCA Reports contain all required elements. | Audit of reports against Section 6.3.1 requirements. | All elements present in 100% of reports. |
| Corrective actions developed for all RCA findings. | Review of CAPs linked to RCA reports. | CAP initiated within 14 days of RCA approval for 100% of RCAs. |
| Sentinel event preliminary summaries to Governing Body within 14 days. | Review of Governing Body notification records. | 100% compliance. |
| Effectiveness monitoring completed. | Review of 3-month monitoring data. | 100% of CAPs have effectiveness monitoring documented. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) request documentation of RCAs conducted during the survey look-back period; (2) verify RCAs use structured methodology, not informal review; (3) verify RCAs identify systemic causes, not just individual blame; (4) verify RCAs lead to corrective action plans with measurable outcomes; (5) verify the Governing Body is informed of sentinel events and RCA findings; (6) assess whether corrective actions were effective in preventing recurrence.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RCA conducted but findings are superficial ("human error"). | Surveyor cites failure to identify systemic root causes. | Train RCA teams to use structured methodology; require root causes stated in system terms per Section 6.2.5. |
| RCA report completed but no corrective action plan developed. | Investigation without improvement. | Mandate CAP per QA-AE-003 for every RCA. |
| RCA completed but effectiveness of corrective actions never verified. | Unknown whether root cause was actually addressed. | Mandate 3-month effectiveness monitoring per Section 6.4.3. |
| Only physician-involved events receive RCA; system failures excluded. | Incomplete quality improvement. | Define RCA triggers broadly per Section 6.1.1 including patterns and persistent quality failures. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All individuals who may serve as RCA Leads or RCA Team members shall receive formal training in RCA methodology within 30 calendar days of their first assignment. Training must cover: (a) RCA purpose and principles; (b) 5 Whys technique; (c) Fishbone diagram construction; (d) interview techniques; (e) distinguishing root causes from contributing factors; (f) writing root causes in system terms; (g) developing measurable corrective recommendations.
10.2 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a roster of trained RCA facilitators and ensure at least 3 agency staff are trained at all times.
10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form within 14 calendar days.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001).

# QA-AE-003 — Corrective Action Plan Development & Tracking
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-AE-003 |
| Title | Corrective Action Plan Development & Tracking |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | AE — Adverse Events & Corrective Action |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy defines the requirements for developing, implementing, tracking, verifying effectiveness, and closing Corrective Action Plans (CAPs) at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. A CAP is the structured, documented response to identified deficiencies — whether arising from adverse events, root cause analyses, quality indicator thresholds, CMS survey findings, compliance audits, or QAPI Committee directives. This policy ensures that identified problems are addressed with specific, measurable, accountable, and time-bound corrective actions, and that the agency can demonstrate to CMS surveyors and the Governing Body that quality improvement is sustained. This policy supports compliance with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program improvement activities) and 42 CFR § 484.100 (compliance with federal, state, and local laws).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
All individuals assigned as CAP Leads or responsible parties
All QAPI Committee members
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The Compliance Officer
The Risk Manager
All department heads whose areas are subject to a CAP
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 A Corrective Action Plan shall be developed for every: (a) Root Cause Analysis completed per QA-AE-002; (b) quality indicator below threshold for 2 consecutive periods with QAPI Committee directive; (c) CMS survey finding requiring a Plan of Correction; (d) compliance audit finding classified as high-risk; (e) Governing Body directive for corrective action; (f) any other deficiency the QAPI Committee determines warrants formal corrective action.
4.2 Each CAP must include: (a) a clear problem statement; (b) the identified root cause or contributing factors; (c) specific corrective actions; (d) responsible party for each action; (e) measurable outcome targets; (f) implementation timeline; (g) monitoring plan; (h) effectiveness verification criteria.
4.3 CAPs shall be tracked centrally by the QAPI Coordinator through the QAPI Corrective Action Tracker and reported to the QAPI Committee monthly and to the Governing Body quarterly.
4.4 No CAP shall be closed until effectiveness has been verified through documented evidence that the corrective action achieved the intended outcome and the deficiency has not recurred for a minimum of 3 consecutive monitoring periods.
4.5 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Corrective Action Plan (CAP) | A formal, documented plan that specifies the actions to be taken to address the root cause(s) of an identified deficiency, prevent recurrence, and achieve a measurable improvement outcome. |
| CAP Lead | The individual assigned primary accountability for the development, implementation, and monitoring of a specific CAP. |
| Effectiveness Verification | The documented confirmation that corrective actions have achieved the intended outcome and the deficiency has not recurred, based on defined monitoring data. |
| Closure Criteria | The specific, measurable conditions that must be met before a CAP can be formally closed, including effectiveness verification. |
| QAPI Corrective Action Tracker | The central log maintained by the QAPI Coordinator documenting all open and closed CAPs, their status, and responsible parties. |
| Plan of Correction (POC) | A specific type of CAP developed in response to CMS survey findings, submitted to CMS within required timeframes. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 CAP Initiation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Initiate a CAP when any trigger condition in Section 4.1 is met. Document the trigger in the QAPI Corrective Action Tracker (Appendix B). | Within 7 calendar days of trigger event. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Assign a CAP Lead. The CAP Lead must: (a) have operational authority in the area affected by the deficiency; (b) have access to relevant data and personnel; (c) be accountable for implementation and reporting. | At time of initiation. |

### 6.2 CAP Development

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | CAP Lead | Develop the Corrective Action Plan using the CAP Template (Appendix A). The plan must include all of the following elements: (a) CAP Reference Number; (b) Trigger Source (RCA, quality indicator, survey finding, etc.); (c) Problem Statement — concise, specific description of the deficiency; (d) Root Cause / Contributing Factors — from RCA findings or contributing factor analysis; (e) Corrective Actions — specific, actionable steps to address each root cause; each action must be individually numbered; (f) Responsible Party — named individual for each corrective action; (g) Measurable Outcome Target — the specific, quantifiable standard the corrective action must achieve (e.g., "Medication reconciliation completion rate ≥ 95% for 3 consecutive months"); (h) Implementation Timeline — start date and completion date for each action; (i) Monitoring Plan — data source, monitoring frequency, and responsible monitor; (j) Closure Criteria — the specific conditions that must be met to close the CAP; (k) Effectiveness Verification Method — how the agency will confirm the corrective action prevented recurrence. | Draft CAP completed within 14 calendar days of CAP initiation (or per CMS timeline for survey-related POCs). |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve the CAP. Committee may require modifications. Document approval in committee minutes. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following draft submission. |

### 6.3 CAP Implementation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | CAP Lead | Implement each corrective action per the approved timeline. Document implementation activities including: date, action taken, staff involved, and any barriers encountered. | Per approved timeline. |
| 6.3.2 | CAP Lead | Provide a written progress report to the QAPI Coordinator at least monthly. Report must include: (a) status of each corrective action (Not Started / In Progress / Completed); (b) data collected per monitoring plan; (c) progress toward measurable outcome target; (d) barriers encountered and mitigation; (e) any requested timeline modifications with rationale. | Monthly until CAP closure. |

### 6.4 Monitoring and Effectiveness Verification

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | CAP Lead / QAPI Coordinator | Monitor the effectiveness of corrective actions by collecting and analyzing data per the CAP monitoring plan. Compare actual performance to the measurable outcome target at each monitoring interval. | Per monitoring plan frequency (minimum monthly). |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Present CAP status to the QAPI Committee monthly as part of the QAPI Action Item Tracker review. For each open CAP: report implementation status, current performance vs. target, and recommendation (continue monitoring / ready for closure / escalate). | Monthly at QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.4.3 | CAP Lead | When the measurable outcome target has been achieved for 3 consecutive monitoring periods (minimum), prepare a CAP Closure Recommendation (Appendix C) documenting: (a) evidence that each corrective action was implemented; (b) data demonstrating the outcome target was met for 3 consecutive periods; (c) evidence that the original deficiency has not recurred; (d) recommendation for closure or ongoing monitoring. | Upon meeting closure criteria. |
| 6.4.4 | QAPI Committee | Review and approve CAP closure. If approved, the CAP is formally closed and documented in the QAPI Corrective Action Tracker. If not approved, the committee specifies additional monitoring or actions required. | At QAPI Committee meeting following closure recommendation. |

### 6.5 CMS Survey Plan of Correction

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | Administrator / QAPI Coordinator | For CMS survey findings requiring a Plan of Correction: develop the POC per CMS format requirements using this policy's CAP development process (Section 6.2) as the foundation. The POC must include: (a) corrective actions for each cited deficiency; (b) how the agency will ensure the deficiency is corrected for the cited patient(s); (c) how the agency will identify other patients potentially affected; (d) what systemic changes will prevent recurrence; (e) how the agency will monitor effectiveness; (f) completion date for each action. | Per CMS-required timeframe (typically 10 calendar days from receipt of survey findings). |
| 6.5.2 | Governing Body | Receive notification of CMS survey findings and POC per GV-GB-001. Provide directives and oversight of corrective action. | Per GV-GB-001 escalation procedures. |
| 6.5.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Track the POC using the same QAPI Corrective Action Tracker and monitoring process as all other CAPs. Report status to the Governing Body weekly until CMS confirms resolution. | Weekly until CMS confirmation. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CAP implementation falls behind approved timeline by > 30 days. | CAP Lead reports to QAPI Coordinator with explanation. | QAPI Coordinator presents to QAPI Committee for review. Committee may approve revised timeline, assign additional resources, or reassign lead. | At next QAPI Committee meeting. |
| CAP fails to achieve measurable outcome target after full implementation period. | CAP Lead reports failure to QAPI Coordinator. | QAPI Committee reviews and may: (a) extend monitoring; (b) modify corrective actions; (c) initiate a new RCA per QA-AE-002; (d) escalate to Governing Body. | Within 30 calendar days of failure identification. |
| CAP closure criteria met but deficiency recurs within 6 months of closure. | QAPI Coordinator reopens the CAP or initiates new investigation. | QAPI Committee reviews recurrence and determines whether original corrective actions were adequate or whether systemic issue requires enhanced response. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following recurrence identification. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CAP document | Completed CAP per Section 6.2.1 (Appendix A). | CAP Lead | QAPI records. | Draft within 14 days of initiation; approved at next committee meeting. |
| QAPI Corrective Action Tracker | Central log per Section 4.3 (Appendix B). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated continuously; presented monthly; retained 7 years. |
| Monthly progress reports | Written reports per Section 6.3.2. | CAP Lead | QAPI records. | Monthly until closure. |
| CAP Closure Recommendation | Recommendation per Section 6.4.3 (Appendix C). | CAP Lead | QAPI records. | Upon meeting closure criteria. |
| QAPI Committee review documentation | Meeting minutes documenting CAP review and decisions. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At each meeting where CAP is discussed. |
| CMS Plan of Correction | POC per Section 6.5.1. | Administrator / QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records; submitted to CMS. | Per CMS timeline. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator (collection). | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CAP developed for all trigger events per Section 4.1. | Review of Corrective Action Tracker vs. adverse event log, quality indicator triggers, and survey findings. | 100% of trigger events have documented CAP. |
| CAPs contain all required elements. | Audit of CAP documents against Section 6.2.1. | 100% of CAPs contain all elements. |
| CAPs approved by QAPI Committee. | Review of committee minutes. | 100% of CAPs approved by committee. |
| Monthly progress reports submitted. | Review of CAP Lead reports. | Reports submitted for 100% of open CAPs monthly. |
| Effectiveness verified before closure. | Review of closure recommendations and supporting data. | 100% of closed CAPs have documented effectiveness verification. |
| CAP tracker reported to QAPI Committee monthly. | Review of committee minutes. | Tracker presented at 100% of monthly meetings. |
| CAPs reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report. | CAP summary in all quarterly reports. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) request documentation of all corrective action plans during the survey look-back period; (2) verify each CAP has specific, measurable corrective actions with responsible parties and deadlines; (3) verify CAPs were actually implemented, not just documented; (4) verify effectiveness was measured; (5) verify the Governing Body was informed and involved when appropriate; (6) for repeat survey findings, determine whether prior CAPs were effective.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CAP actions are vague ("improve documentation"). | Unmeasurable; surveyor cannot assess effectiveness. | Require specific measurable outcome targets per Section 6.2.1. |
| CAP developed but never implemented. | Paper compliance without actual improvement. | Require monthly progress reports per Section 6.3.2. |
| CAP closed without effectiveness verification. | Unknown whether deficiency was actually corrected. | Mandate 3-period effectiveness verification before closure per Section 6.4.3. |
| Same deficiency recurs after CAP closure. | Prior corrective action was inadequate. | Enforce recurrence monitoring per Section 6.6 and reopen investigation. |

10.2 All QAPI Committee members shall receive annual training on CAP review, approval criteria, and closure evaluation.
10.3 Policy acknowledgment within 14 calendar days per standard.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001).

# QA-AE-004 — Patient Safety Program
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-AE-004 |
| Title | Patient Safety Program |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | AE — Adverse Events & Corrective Action |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 3 — Confidential |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes a formal, agency-wide Patient Safety Program at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., integrated with the QAPI framework, to proactively identify, assess, and mitigate risks to patient safety across all service delivery processes. A patient safety program moves beyond reactive adverse event management to include proactive hazard identification, safety culture promotion, and system-level risk mitigation. This policy ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program including patient safety), 42 CFR § 484.60 (quality of care), and 42 CFR § 484.70 (infection prevention and control as a safety concern).
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
All agency staff — clinical, administrative, contracted, and per diem
All QAPI Committee members
The QAPI Coordinator (Patient Safety Program Lead)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The Risk Manager
The Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formal Patient Safety Program that is integrated within the QAPI program and led by the QAPI Coordinator (or a designated Patient Safety Officer).
4.2 The Patient Safety Program shall include both proactive and reactive components: (a) Proactive — hazard identification, safety risk assessments, safety culture monitoring, and preventive interventions; (b) Reactive — adverse event identification, investigation, corrective action, and trending per QA-AE-001 through QA-AE-003.
4.3 The Patient Safety Program shall maintain standing focus areas that include at minimum: (a) fall prevention; (b) medication safety; (c) infection prevention; (d) communication safety (transitions, handoffs, orders); (e) environmental safety in the patient home.
4.4 A culture of safety shall be promoted through non-punitive reporting, staff empowerment to report concerns, leadership engagement, and visible organizational commitment to patient safety.
4.5 Patient safety data shall be a standing item on the QAPI Committee agenda and included in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Patient Safety Program | The formal, agency-wide program that integrates proactive hazard identification, risk mitigation, adverse event management, and safety culture promotion to prevent harm to patients receiving home health services. |
| Patient Safety Culture | An organizational environment where all staff feel empowered and obligated to report safety concerns, where leadership visibly prioritizes safety, and where the focus is on system improvement rather than individual blame. |
| Proactive Safety Assessment | A systematic evaluation of a process, system, or environment to identify potential risks before an adverse event occurs. |
| Safety Huddle | A brief, focused team communication (typically 5–15 minutes) addressing immediate patient safety concerns, emerging risks, or follow-up on recent safety events. |
| High-Reliability Principle | A principle from high-reliability organizations (HROs) applied to healthcare: preoccupation with failure, reluctance to simplify, sensitivity to operations, commitment to resilience, and deference to expertise. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Patient Safety Program Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Formally establish and maintain the Patient Safety Program as a defined component of the QAPI program. Document the program in the QAPI Program Description per QA-PG-001 Section 6.1.3. The program must include: (a) defined leadership (QAPI Coordinator or designated Patient Safety Officer); (b) standing patient safety focus areas; (c) proactive safety assessment schedule; (d) adverse event management integration; (e) safety culture promotion activities; (f) reporting structure to QAPI Committee and Governing Body. | At program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Designate a Patient Safety Officer if the QAPI Coordinator does not serve in this role. The Patient Safety Officer must: (a) be a licensed healthcare professional or individual with patient safety certification/experience; (b) have authority to conduct safety assessments and recommend system changes; (c) report directly to the QAPI Committee. | At program establishment; within 30 days of any vacancy. |

### 6.2 Standing Patient Safety Focus Areas

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain the following standing patient safety focus areas with defined monitoring, interventions, and quality indicators: (a) Fall Prevention — per CL-SD-015; monitoring fall rates, risk assessment compliance, intervention compliance; (b) Medication Safety — per CL-SD-012, CL-SD-013; monitoring medication error rates, reconciliation compliance, adverse drug event rates; (c) Infection Prevention — per CL-SD-016, QA-SM-002; monitoring infection rates, standard precautions compliance, hand hygiene compliance; (d) Communication Safety — monitoring physician order accuracy, verbal order compliance, transition-of-care documentation completeness, handoff communication failures; (e) Environmental Safety — per RM-PS-001; monitoring home safety assessment completion, identified hazard mitigation, equipment safety. | Ongoing; reviewed annually during QAPI plan development. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | For each standing focus area, define and track at least one patient safety indicator on the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002. Patient safety indicators must be reviewed at each monthly QAPI Committee meeting. | At program establishment; ongoing. |

### 6.3 Proactive Safety Assessments

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Patient Safety Officer | Conduct at least 2 proactive safety assessments per year, targeting high-risk processes identified through adverse event trending, near-miss data, or QAPI Committee direction. Proactive assessment methods include: (a) Failure Mode and Effects Analysis (FMEA) — systematic analysis of a process to identify potential failure points before they cause harm; (b) Safety walk-throughs — observation of clinical processes in the field; (c) Process mapping with risk overlay — mapping a clinical workflow and identifying safety risk points; (d) Patient safety rounding — structured conversations with clinical staff about safety concerns. | Minimum 2 per calendar year; schedule established during QAPI plan development. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Document proactive safety assessment findings and present to the QAPI Committee. For each identified risk: (a) describe the risk; (b) assess severity and likelihood; (c) recommend preventive intervention; (d) assign responsible party and timeline. | Assessment report presented at next QAPI Committee meeting following completion. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Committee | Review proactive assessment findings and direct implementation of preventive interventions. Track interventions through the QAPI Action Item Tracker. | At QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.4 Safety Culture Promotion

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Administrator | Promote a culture of safety through: (a) Leadership commitment — Administrator and DON actively communicate that patient safety is the agency's highest priority; (b) Non-punitive reporting — reinforced through training, policy acknowledgment, and visible leadership support per QA-AE-001 Section 6.6; (c) Safety communication — regular safety reminders, bulletins, or alerts distributed to all clinical staff; (d) Safety recognition — acknowledge staff who identify and report safety concerns; (e) Just Culture principles — distinguish between human error (system fix), at-risk behavior (coaching), and reckless behavior (discipline). | Ongoing. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Conduct a patient safety culture assessment at least every 2 years using a validated tool (e.g., AHRQ SOPS, or equivalent). Present results to the QAPI Committee and develop an action plan to address identified weaknesses. | Every 2 years; results presented to committee. |
| 6.4.3 | Clinical Supervisors | Conduct monthly Safety Huddles with clinical staff (may be integrated into team meetings). Safety Huddles must address: (a) recent adverse events or near-misses (without patient identifying information); (b) current safety concerns; (c) reminders on high-risk safety topics; (d) feedback from staff on safety barriers. Document Safety Huddle topics and attendance. | Monthly. |

### 6.5 Integration with QAPI and Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Include patient safety data as a standing section in the monthly QAPI Committee agenda: (a) patient safety indicator review; (b) adverse event and near-miss summary (per QA-AE-001); (c) proactive assessment findings; (d) safety culture activities; (e) open safety-related corrective actions. | Monthly at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.5.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include patient safety summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. | Quarterly. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Patient safety event classified as sentinel. | Immediate RCA per QA-AE-002; Governing Body notified within 72 hours. | Full RCA, CAP, and enhanced monitoring. | Per QA-AE-001 and QA-AE-002 timeframes. |
| Proactive assessment identifies imminent patient safety risk. | QAPI Coordinator notifies DON and Administrator immediately. | Immediate preventive action implemented; formal corrective plan follows within 7 calendar days. | Immediate action; formal plan within 7 days. |
| Safety culture assessment reveals significant deficiency (< 50% positive response in any domain). | QAPI Coordinator presents to QAPI Committee and Administrator. | Culture improvement action plan within 30 calendar days; re-assessment within 12 months. | Plan within 30 days; re-assessment within 12 months. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Patient Safety Program description | Documented within QAPI Program Description per Section 6.1.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | At establishment; updated annually. |
| Patient safety indicators | Defined indicators on QAPI dashboard per Section 6.2.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Ongoing. |
| Proactive safety assessment reports | Written reports per Section 6.3.2 (Appendix A). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Minimum 2 per year. |
| Safety culture assessment results | Assessment results and action plan per Section 6.4.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Every 2 years. |
| Safety Huddle documentation | Topics and attendance per Section 6.4.3. | Clinical Supervisors | QAPI records. | Monthly. |
| QAPI Committee patient safety review | Meeting minutes documenting patient safety review. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Monthly. |
| Governing Body patient safety reports | Quarterly QAPI Performance Report section. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator (collection). | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Patient Safety Program formally established and documented. | Review of QAPI Program Description. | Program documented and current. |
| Patient safety indicators monitored monthly. | Review of QAPI dashboard. | All standing focus area indicators on dashboard; reviewed monthly. |
| Proactive safety assessments conducted. | Review of assessment reports. | Minimum 2 per year. |
| Safety culture assessed. | Review of assessment records. | Assessment completed at least every 2 years. |
| Safety Huddles conducted monthly. | Review of Huddle documentation. | Monthly documentation with topics and attendance. |
| Patient safety reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report. | Included in all quarterly reports. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask the agency to describe its patient safety program; (2) look for evidence of proactive safety activities, not just reactive event management; (3) verify safety is integrated with QAPI; (4) assess whether a non-punitive reporting culture exists; (5) verify the Governing Body is informed of patient safety data; (6) look for evidence that safety concerns lead to measurable improvement.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| "Patient safety program" is just the adverse event reporting policy. | Surveyor cites lack of proactive program. | Establish proactive components per Section 6.3. |
| No evidence of safety culture activities. | Surveyor questions organizational commitment to safety. | Conduct safety culture assessment and huddles per Section 6.4. |
| Safety indicators exist but are identical to general quality indicators with no safety-specific focus. | Insufficient patient safety monitoring. | Define safety-specific indicators for each standing focus area per Section 6.2. |

## 9–12. References, Training, Version Control, Appendices
References: 42 CFR § 484.65, § 484.60, § 484.70; cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-AE-001, QA-AE-002, QA-AE-003, QA-PI-002, CL-SD-012, CL-SD-015, CL-SD-016, RM-PS-001, RM-ER-002, GV-GB-001, EN-TG-001.
Training: 10.1 All staff receive patient safety awareness training during orientation and annually. 10.2 QAPI Committee members trained on safety program components. 10.3 Staff conducting proactive assessments trained in FMEA or equivalent methodology. 10.4 Policy acknowledgment within 14 days.
Version Control: Per EN-LC-001.
