# QA-SM-001 — Utilization Review & Management
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-SM-001 |
| Title | Utilization Review & Management |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | SM — Surveillance & Monitoring |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes a structured, data-driven Utilization Review (UR) program at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. that monitors the appropriateness, efficiency, and clinical necessity of all home health services delivered to patients. The UR program ensures that services are ordered and delivered based on documented clinical need, that visit frequency and duration align with patient goals and physician orders, and that patterns of over-utilization or under-utilization are identified and corrected. This policy satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI — utilization data as a quality input), 42 CFR § 484.60 (care planning and quality of care), and 42 CFR § 484.55 (comprehensive assessment of patients), while also addressing the financial integrity requirements implicit in CMS payment integrity programs. Utilization review is treated as a quality of care program, not solely a cost-containment function.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
The Administrator
The CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
All QAPI Committee members
All clinical supervisors responsible for caseload oversight
All scheduling and intake staff
All clinical staff (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs) providing billable services
The Compliance Officer
Exclusion: Individual billing procedures for specific payers are governed by FN-BC-001. LUPA-specific monitoring procedures are governed by QA-PI-006 and FN-CM-005. This policy governs the overarching UR program framework.
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formal, agency-wide Utilization Review program that monitors the appropriateness, frequency, duration, and clinical justification of all home health services at the episode, discipline, visit, and clinician level.
4.2 The UR program shall be integrated within the QAPI framework and shall serve as a standing data input to the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002.
4.3 All services delivered by the agency must be: (a) ordered by a licensed physician or allowed practitioner; (b) supported by documented clinical need in the patient's clinical record; (c) consistent with the patient's plan of care; (d) reasonable and necessary as defined by CMS coverage criteria.
4.4 The UR program shall identify and trigger review for both over-utilization (visits in excess of ordered or clinically justified frequency) and under-utilization (failure to deliver ordered visits), recognizing both as quality of care deficiencies.
4.5 A designated Utilization Review Committee (or function integrated within the QAPI Committee) shall conduct structured reviews at defined intervals and report findings to the QAPI Committee and Governing Body.
4.6 Utilization data shall be used to identify patterns by clinician, discipline, diagnosis group, referral source, and payer that may indicate quality concerns, training needs, or compliance risks.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Utilization Review (UR) | A systematic process for evaluating the appropriateness, medical necessity, and efficiency of health services provided to patients, using defined criteria and data analysis. |
| Over-Utilization | A pattern in which visits are delivered in excess of physician-ordered frequency, clinical necessity, or coverage criteria — potentially indicating waste or inappropriate billing. |
| Under-Utilization | A pattern in which ordered visits are not delivered, or visit frequency is insufficient relative to the patient's documented clinical needs — a patient care quality failure. |
| Medical Necessity | The standard by which Medicare and other payers determine coverage: services must be reasonable and necessary for the diagnosis or treatment of illness or injury and must align with accepted standards of medical practice. |
| Episode | A 30-day home health payment period under the Patient-Driven Groupings Model (PDGM). |
| Utilization Rate | The ratio of visits delivered to visits ordered within a defined period, episode, or discipline. |
| UR Committee | The designated group (or integrated QAPI Committee function) responsible for conducting structured utilization reviews, analyzing patterns, and directing corrective action. |
| Outlier Case | An episode in which visit utilization deviates significantly (above or below) from expected norms for similar diagnoses, as defined by agency thresholds. |
| Frequency Variance | The difference between ordered visit frequency and actual visit delivery for a given episode and discipline. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 UR Program Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Formally establish the UR program as a defined component of the QAPI program. Document in the QAPI Program Description per QA-PG-001 Section 6.1.3. The program must include: (a) designated UR function (integrated within QAPI Committee or separate UR Committee); (b) defined UR indicators and thresholds; (c) case review schedule; (d) reporting structure; (e) escalation pathways. | At program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Designate the UR function. Options: (a) integrate UR as a standing QAPI Committee agenda item with monthly review; or (b) establish a separate UR Committee meeting biweekly or monthly. If a separate UR Committee is established, membership must include at minimum: DON/Clinical Manager, QAPI Coordinator, CFO/Revenue Cycle Representative, and at least one clinical discipline representative. | At program establishment; roster updated within 7 days of any change. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Develop and maintain a written UR Criteria Document defining: (a) visit frequency benchmarks by diagnosis group and discipline (based on CMS PDGM data and internal clinical standards); (b) thresholds for outlier case identification; (c) criteria for medical necessity documentation adequacy; (d) triggers for case-level review. | Within 60 days of program establishment; reviewed annually. |

### 6.2 Prospective Utilization Review (Pre-Admission and Start of Care)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Intake Staff / Clinical Manager | At the time of intake screening, verify that: (a) the referral source has provided or will obtain a physician order for home health services; (b) the patient's diagnosis and clinical presentation are consistent with home health eligibility criteria; (c) the proposed services are within the agency's licensed scope. | At intake; prior to SOC. |
| 6.2.2 | Assessing Clinician (RN or Therapist) | At Start of Care (SOC), ensure the plan of care visit frequency and discipline mix are: (a) clinically justified by the OASIS and comprehensive assessment findings; (b) consistent with the physician's order; (c) reasonable and necessary per CMS coverage criteria; (d) achievable within the patient's 30-day period given scheduling and geographic factors. | At SOC assessment. |
| 6.2.3 | Clinical Supervisor / DON | Review all SOC plans of care within 5 calendar days of SOC for: (a) appropriateness of ordered visit frequency; (b) consistency of disciplines ordered with documented skilled need; (c) flags for unusually high or low visit frequencies relative to diagnosis benchmarks. Flag outlier plans of care for discussion at the next UR review. | Within 5 calendar days of SOC. |

### 6.3 Concurrent Utilization Review (During Active Episodes)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | CFO / Revenue Cycle Director | Generate and distribute a weekly Active Episode Utilization Report to the QAPI Coordinator and DON. The report must include for each active episode: (a) patient identifier; (b) episode start/end dates; (c) diagnosis group; (d) ordered visit frequency by discipline; (e) actual visits delivered to date by discipline; (f) frequency variance (ordered vs. delivered); (g) projected visits at current rate; (h) LUPA risk status (cross-reference QA-PI-006 dashboard). | Weekly, every Monday. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator / DON | Review the Active Episode Utilization Report and flag for case-level review any episode where: (a) frequency variance exceeds ± 25% for any discipline for more than 2 consecutive weeks; (b) total visit count is on track to exceed ordered frequency by more than 20%; (c) LUPA risk is Red (per QA-PI-006); (d) discharge criteria appear to have been met but the episode continues; (e) episode length exceeds the 60th percentile for the diagnosis group. | Weekly, within 2 business days of report receipt. |
| 6.3.3 | Clinical Supervisor | For each flagged episode, conduct a case-level review including: (a) clinical record review for documentation of medical necessity; (b) conversation with the assigned clinician; (c) confirmation that goals are still achievable and discharge is not overdue. Document findings on the Case-Level UR Review Form (Appendix C). | Within 3 business days of flagging. |
| 6.3.4 | Clinical Supervisor | Based on case-level review, take one of the following actions: (a) No action required — document clinical justification supporting current utilization pattern; (b) Order modification — consult with the physician to modify visit frequency if clinically appropriate; (c) Discharge planning — initiate discharge planning if goals have been met; (d) Education — provide clinical guidance to the assigned clinician; (e) Corrective action — escalate to DON if documentation deficiency or inappropriate utilization is confirmed. Document action taken on the Case-Level UR Review Form. | Within 5 business days of case-level review completion. |

### 6.4 Retrospective Utilization Review (Post-Episode Analysis)

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Monthly, generate the Retrospective Utilization Analysis Report for all episodes closed in the prior month. The report must include: (a) total episodes closed; (b) episodes by PDGM clinical grouping; (c) average visits per episode by discipline and diagnosis group; (d) frequency variance distribution; (e) LUPA rate (cross-reference QA-PI-006); (f) episodes exceeding high-utilization threshold; (g) average episode length by clinical group; (h) visit counts by clinician. | Monthly; within 10 business days of month end. |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Analyze retrospective data for patterns. Flag for UR Committee review: (a) any clinician whose average visits per episode for a diagnosis group exceeds agency average by more than 30%; (b) any clinician whose average visits per episode falls below agency average by more than 30% (potential under-serving); (c) any diagnosis group showing increasing average visits without corresponding clinical acuity change; (d) any referral source whose episodes consistently show outlier utilization patterns. | Monthly as part of report generation. |
| 6.4.3 | UR Committee / QAPI Committee | Review the Retrospective Utilization Analysis Report at the monthly QAPI Committee meeting. For each pattern identified: (a) review supporting clinical documentation for a sample of episodes (minimum 10% or 5 records, whichever is greater); (b) determine root cause (clinical, documentation, training, systemic); (c) assign corrective action with responsible party and timeline. Document review and decisions in QAPI Committee minutes. | Monthly at QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.5 Clinician-Level Utilization Profiling

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Generate a Clinician Utilization Profile quarterly for each active clinical staff member. The profile must include: (a) total episodes managed in the quarter; (b) average visits per episode by discipline; (c) LUPA rate for the clinician's episodes; (d) frequency variance rate; (e) high-utilization outlier rate; (f) documentation timeliness rate (from CL-CD-004 monitoring). | Quarterly; distributed to DON within 10 business days of quarter end. |
| 6.5.2 | DON / Clinical Supervisor | Review each Clinician Utilization Profile. For any clinician whose profile shows a pattern of outlier utilization (high or low) in 2 or more consecutive quarters: (a) review a sample of the clinician's clinical records for medical necessity documentation adequacy; (b) provide individual education and coaching; (c) if pattern persists for a third consecutive quarter, initiate corrective action per HR-ER-002. Document all reviews and actions. | Within 14 calendar days of profile receipt. |

### 6.6 QAPI Committee and Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Present the monthly utilization data summary to the QAPI Committee as a standing agenda item. Summary must include: (a) active episode utilization overview; (b) flagged cases and disposition; (c) retrospective analysis findings; (d) patterns identified; (e) corrective actions in progress. | Monthly at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.6.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include utilization review summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. Summary must include: (a) utilization rate trends; (b) LUPA rate; (c) high-utilization outlier rate; (d) corrective actions taken; (e) any compliance concerns escalated to Compliance Officer. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Case-level review identifies medical necessity documentation that is inadequate to support billed visits. | Clinical Supervisor escalates to DON and Compliance Officer immediately. | DON directs clinician to supplement documentation where clinically accurate; Compliance Officer assesses whether claim correction or overpayment disclosure is required per FN-BC-004 and CO-FA-002. | Notification within 1 business day; Compliance assessment within 7 calendar days. |
| Clinician utilization profile shows high-utilization pattern for 3 consecutive quarters. | DON escalates to Administrator. | Administrator convenes review with DON and Compliance Officer. If inappropriate billing is suspected, Compliance Officer initiates investigation per CO-CP-007. | Review within 14 calendar days. |
| Retrospective analysis shows agency-wide utilization trend deviating significantly from CMS norms. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to QAPI Committee and Administrator. | QAPI Committee initiates a PIP per QA-PI-001. Compliance Officer conducts a focused audit per CO-RA-002. | PIP initiated within 30 calendar days. |
| UR data not generated or reviewed for 2 consecutive months. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator. | Administrator directs immediate resumption. Root cause of gap documented per QA-AE-003. Reported to Governing Body at next quarterly meeting. | Resumption within 5 business days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UR program formally established and documented. | Review of QAPI Program Description. | Program documented; UR Criteria Document on file. |
| Weekly Active Episode Utilization Report generated. | Review of report files. | Reports generated every business week; no gaps exceeding 1 week. |
| Flagged cases receive case-level review. | Review of Case-Level UR Review Forms. | 100% of flagged cases have documented review within 3 business days. |
| Monthly Retrospective Analysis Report completed. | Review of report files and QAPI Committee minutes. | 12 reports per year; presented at each monthly QAPI Committee meeting. |
| Clinician Utilization Profiles generated quarterly. | Review of profile files and DON review documentation. | 4 profiles per clinician per year; DON review documented. |
| UR data reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report and Governing Body minutes. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| Corrective actions triggered for identified outliers. | Review of QAPI Committee minutes and corrective action records. | 100% of identified patterns have documented corrective action. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors evaluating care planning, quality, and payment integrity will verify: (1) the agency has a process for monitoring whether services are appropriate and medically necessary; (2) visit delivery matches physician orders; (3) over-utilization patterns are identified and addressed; (4) the UR process is integrated with QAPI; (5) the Governing Body is informed of utilization trends; (6) documentation supports all billed services.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| UR program exists only for billing purposes with no clinical quality focus. | Surveyor cites failure of agency-wide QAPI scope. | Frame UR as quality program; integrate with QAPI Committee. |
| No concurrent monitoring — only retrospective review. | High-risk episodes not intercepted before episode closes. | Mandate weekly Active Episode Utilization Report per Section 6.3. |
| Clinician outlier patterns identified but no corrective action documented. | Patterns persist; potential billing integrity concern. | Enforce clinician profile review and corrective action per Sections 6.5 and 6.7. |
| UR data not included in QAPI Committee meetings. | Disconnect between utilization monitoring and quality improvement. | Add UR data as standing QAPI agenda item per Section 6.6.1. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All clinical supervisors and the DON shall receive training on utilization review methodology, case-level review standards, and documentation of medical necessity within 14 calendar days of assignment. Training must cover: (a) UR criteria and outlier thresholds; (b) how to conduct a case-level review; (c) medical necessity documentation standards; (d) escalation pathways.
10.2 All clinical staff (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs) shall receive training during initial orientation and annually on: (a) medical necessity documentation requirements; (b) visit frequency compliance; (c) how utilization monitoring protects patients and the agency.
10.3 The QAPI Coordinator and CFO shall receive training on utilization data analysis and report generation within 30 calendar days of policy effective date.
10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix F) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new assignment.
10.5 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a training tracking log documenting all UR-related training.
## 11. Version Control
11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.
11.2 Only the most current approved version, as reflected in the policy header, is valid. Superseded versions must be archived and marked "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."
11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.
11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body.

Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Utilization Review Criteria Document Policy Reference: QA-SM-001 | Version: 6.0 | Date: 2025-07-10
Established By: _________________________ Date: _____________ Approved by QAPI Committee: ☐ Yes — Date: _____________ Minutes Ref: _____________
SECTION 1 — VISIT FREQUENCY BENCHMARKS BY DIAGNOSIS GROUP
The following benchmarks represent expected visit frequency ranges per 30-day episode. Visits outside these ranges trigger case-level review.

| PDGM Clinical Grouping | Discipline | Expected Low (visits/30-day) | Expected High (visits/30-day) | Outlier Threshold (High) | Outlier Threshold (Low) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Musculoskeletal Rehabilitation | PT | 8 | 14 | > 18 | < 5 |
| Musculoskeletal Rehabilitation | OT | 4 | 10 | > 14 | < 3 |
| Neuro Rehabilitation | PT | 10 | 16 | > 20 | < 6 |
| Neuro Rehabilitation | ST | 6 | 12 | > 16 | < 4 |
| Wounds | RN | 8 | 18 | > 24 | < 5 |
| Medication Management | RN | 4 | 10 | > 14 | < 3 |
| Behavioral Health | MSW | 2 | 6 | > 8 | < 2 |
| Cardiac/Circulatory | RN | 6 | 14 | > 18 | < 4 |
| Endocrine/Metabolic | RN | 4 | 12 | > 16 | < 3 |
| Complex Nursing | RN | 10 | 20 | > 26 | < 6 |
| HHA | HHA | 8 | 20 | > 26 | < 5 |
| [Add rows for agency-specific groupings] |  |  |  |  |  |

Benchmarks reviewed and updated annually based on CMS PDGM data and agency historical performance.
SECTION 2 — CONCURRENT REVIEW TRIGGERS
The following conditions trigger mandatory case-level review during an active episode:

| Trigger | Threshold | Action Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Frequency variance (any discipline) | ± 25% for 2+ consecutive weeks | Case-Level UR Review Form |
| Visit count tracking above ordered frequency | > 20% over ordered visits | Case-Level UR Review Form |
| LUPA Risk Status | Red (per QA-PI-006 dashboard) | Case-Level UR Review Form + LUPA Team notification |
| Episode approaching planned end date — goals unmet | Within 5 days of episode end | Recertification review |
| Episode length exceeds 60th percentile for diagnosis group | [Agency-specific percentile] | Case-Level UR Review Form |
| Physician order not received/signed within 30 days of verbal | Per CL-CP-004 | Order management escalation |

SECTION 3 — MEDICAL NECESSITY DOCUMENTATION ADEQUACY CRITERIA
For any case-level review, the reviewer shall assess documentation adequacy using the following checklist. All items marked "No" require corrective action.

| Documentation Element | Present in Record? |
| --- | --- |
| OASIS assessment supports identified skilled need | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Physician order specifies visit frequency and disciplines ordered | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Visit notes document skilled nursing/therapy interventions (not routine aide tasks) | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Homebound status documented at each skilled visit | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Patient progress or lack of progress documented with objective measures | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Goals are measurable and time-bound | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Plan of care reflects active skilled need (not maintenance-only) | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Medication administration documented with medication names/doses/routes | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Wound care measurements/staging documented at each wound visit | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |
| Therapy notes include functional assessment scores | ☐ Yes ☐ No ☐ N/A |

Overall Documentation Adequacy Rating: ☐ Adequate ☐ Requires Supplementation ☐ Inadequate — Compliance Review Required
SECTION 4 — CLINICIAN OUTLIER THRESHOLDS (Quarterly Profile)

| Profile Metric | Green (Acceptable) | Yellow (Watch) | Red (Review Required) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Avg visits/episode vs. agency average | Within ± 15% | ± 16–29% | ± 30% or more |
| LUPA rate for clinician's episodes | ≤ Agency threshold | 1–5% above threshold | > 5% above threshold |
| Documentation timeliness rate | ≥ 95% | 85–94% | < 85% |
| High-utilization outlier rate | ≤ 5% of episodes | 6–10% | > 10% |

# QA-SM-002 — Infection Surveillance & Trending
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-SM-002 |
| Title | Infection Surveillance & Trending |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | SM — Surveillance & Monitoring |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's infection surveillance program at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. as a formal, data-driven component of the QAPI framework and the infection prevention and control program (CL-SD-016). Infection surveillance is the systematic collection, analysis, trending, and reporting of data on infectious diseases and infection-related events among patients receiving home health services and among agency staff, enabling the agency to identify patterns, investigate clusters, implement targeted interventions, and demonstrate continuous improvement in infection prevention outcomes. This policy satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.70 (Condition of Participation: Infection Prevention and Control), which requires an ongoing infection surveillance program, and 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program), which requires infection data as a standing QAPI input.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Infection Prevention and Control Designee (as designated per CL-SD-016)
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All clinical staff (RNs, LVNs, therapists, MSWs, HHAs)
All QAPI Committee members
The Administrator
The Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formal, ongoing infection surveillance program that systematically collects, analyzes, and trends infection data for both patient infections and staff occupational exposures.
4.2 The infection surveillance program shall be integrated within the QAPI framework and shall serve as a standing data input to both the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard (QA-PI-002) and the monthly QAPI Committee agenda (QA-PG-003).
4.3 All clinical staff are responsible for identifying and reporting suspected or confirmed infections in patients and in themselves to the Infection Prevention and Control Designee and/or the Director of Nursing within 24 hours of identification.
4.4 Infection data shall be trended over time (minimum 12-month rolling trend) by infection type, patient population characteristics, geographic area, and clinician, to identify patterns and clusters requiring investigation.
4.5 When an infection cluster (defined in Section 5) is identified, the QAPI Committee shall conduct a documented investigation within 14 calendar days and implement a corrective action plan per QA-AE-003.
4.6 The infection surveillance program shall include monitoring of: (a) patient infections acquired during the episode of care; (b) staff occupational exposures and work-related infections; (c) process compliance indicators (hand hygiene, PPE use, wound care technique); (d) antibiotic use patterns (where data is available); (e) hospitalizations related to infection.
4.7 Infection surveillance findings and trends shall be reported to the Governing Body quarterly as part of the QAPI Performance Report.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Infection Surveillance | The ongoing, systematic collection, analysis, and interpretation of health data for use in the planning, implementation, and evaluation of infection prevention and control practices. |
| Healthcare-Associated Infection (HAI) | An infection occurring in a patient during or as a result of home health services that was not present or incubating at the time of the start of care. |
| Infection Cluster | Two or more cases of the same type of infection among the agency's patients within a 30-day period that share a common exposure factor (e.g., clinician, diagnosis, wound type, geographic area). |
| Occupational Exposure | Contact with blood, body fluids, or infectious materials that occurs in the course of performing job duties. |
| Infection Rate | The number of infections of a specific type per 100 episodes (or per 1,000 patient-visit days), calculated for a defined time period. |
| Process Compliance Indicator | A measurable behavior or practice (e.g., hand hygiene compliance, PPE use rate) that reflects adherence to evidence-based infection prevention standards. |
| Infection Prevention and Control Designee (IPC Designee) | The individual designated by the agency per CL-SD-016 to administer the infection prevention and control program and serve as the primary liaison for infection surveillance activities. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Infection Surveillance System Establishment

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator / IPC Designee | Establish and maintain the Infection Surveillance System components: (a) Patient Infection Report Form (Appendix A); (b) Staff Occupational Exposure Report Form (clinical record); (c) Infection Tracking Log (Appendix B); (d) Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary Report (Appendix C); (e) defined infection categories for surveillance; (f) infection rate calculation methodology; (g) defined performance thresholds for each infection category. | At program establishment; components reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator / IPC Designee | Define the following mandatory infection surveillance categories. For each category, establish: (a) case definition; (b) data source; (c) numerator and denominator for rate calculation; (d) performance threshold; (e) national benchmark (if available): (i) Urinary tract infections (UTI); (ii) Respiratory tract infections (RTI/pneumonia); (iii) Wound/surgical site infections; (iv) Skin and soft tissue infections; (v) Bloodstream infections (central line-associated, where applicable); (vi) Gastrointestinal infections; (vii) Influenza and influenza-like illness (ILI); (viii) COVID-19 and other emerging pathogens; (ix) Methicillin-resistant Staphylococcus aureus (MRSA) — for colonized or infected patients; (x) Staff occupational exposures (needle stick, blood/body fluid contact). | At establishment; case definitions reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.3 | IPC Designee | Maintain access to current infection surveillance resources including: (a) CDC NHSN definitions for home health settings (where applicable); (b) APIC (Association for Professionals in Infection Control) home care guidelines; (c) CMS infection prevention guidance; (d) California Department of Public Health (CDPH) reportable disease requirements. | Ongoing. |

### 6.2 Patient Infection Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Discovering Clinician | When a patient exhibits signs and symptoms consistent with an infection (new onset of fever, new or worsening wound drainage, urinary symptoms, respiratory symptoms, skin changes, or any other infection indicators), report to the clinical supervisor and IPC Designee within 24 hours and complete the Patient Infection Report Form (Appendix A). | Within 24 hours of identification. |
| 6.2.2 | Discovering Clinician | Notify the patient's physician of the identified infection per clinical protocol. Document physician notification, response, and any orders in the patient's clinical record. | Within 1 hour for moderate-to-severe infections; within 4 hours for mild infections or by end of the visit day. |
| 6.2.3 | IPC Designee | Review the Patient Infection Report Form and determine: (a) infection type and case definition met (confirmed vs. suspected); (b) likely acquisition timing (HAI vs. community-acquired); (c) whether the infection represents a potential cluster; (d) whether the infection requires external reporting per California mandatory reporting requirements (CDPH). | Within 2 business days of form receipt. |
| 6.2.4 | IPC Designee | Log the confirmed and suspected infection in the Infection Tracking Log (Appendix B) with all required data elements within 2 business days. | Within 2 business days of case determination. |

### 6.3 Staff Occupational Exposure Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | Exposed Staff Member | Report any occupational exposure (needle stick, blood/body fluid contact, mucous membrane exposure) to the clinical supervisor and DON immediately. Complete the Staff Occupational Exposure Report per RM-SS-001 and HR-WM-004. | Immediately upon exposure; report within 1 hour. |
| 6.3.2 | DON / Supervisor | Ensure the exposed staff member receives immediate medical evaluation per agency protocol. Report the exposure to the IPC Designee for tracking. | Within 2 hours of exposure notification. |
| 6.3.3 | IPC Designee | Log the occupational exposure in the Infection Tracking Log (Appendix B) and include in the monthly surveillance summary. | Within 2 business days. |

### 6.4 Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | IPC Designee | Compile the Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary Report (Appendix C) for all infections and exposures reported in the prior month. The report must include: (a) total patient infections by category; (b) infection rates calculated per 100 episodes; (c) 12-month rolling trend for each infection category; (d) comparison to performance thresholds; (e) comparison to prior month; (f) staff occupational exposures; (g) potential clusters identified; (h) process compliance indicator results; (i) corrective actions in progress; (j) external reports filed. | Monthly; completed within 10 business days of month end. |
| 6.4.2 | IPC Designee / QAPI Coordinator | Present the Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary to the QAPI Committee as a standing agenda item. The presentation must include trend graphs for each infection category, identification of any below-threshold indicators, and recommendations for action. | Monthly at each QAPI Committee meeting. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Committee | Review the infection surveillance summary and: (a) acknowledge categories at or below threshold; (b) for any infection rate exceeding threshold for 2 consecutive months, direct a root cause analysis or contributing factor analysis and corrective action; (c) for any identified cluster, direct a formal investigation per Section 6.5; (d) document all analysis and decisions in committee minutes. | At each monthly QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.5 Infection Cluster Investigation

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | IPC Designee | Declare a cluster when 2 or more cases of the same infection type occur within 30 days that share a potential common exposure factor. Notify the DON, QAPI Coordinator, and Administrator immediately. | Within 1 business day of cluster identification. |
| 6.5.2 | IPC Designee / QAPI Coordinator | Convene a special cluster investigation meeting within 3 business days. The investigation must: (a) review all cases for commonalities (clinician, visit dates, geographic area, wound type, equipment used); (b) collect additional information from involved clinicians; (c) review process compliance for infection prevention protocols; (d) determine likely transmission mode; (e) implement immediate containment measures. | Within 3 business days of cluster declaration. |
| 6.5.3 | IPC Designee | Develop and implement a Cluster Response Plan including: (a) immediate corrective actions (re-education, equipment inspection, PPE audit); (b) enhanced monitoring of the affected patient population; (c) any required external reporting (CDPH if disease is reportable); (d) follow-up surveillance plan to verify resolution. Document the plan and submit to QAPI Coordinator. | Within 7 calendar days of cluster declaration. |
| 6.5.4 | QAPI Committee | Review cluster investigation findings and response plan at the next QAPI Committee meeting (or at a special meeting if urgency requires). Direct development of a formal Corrective Action Plan per QA-AE-003. | At next committee meeting; within 14 calendar days of cluster declaration. |

### 6.6 Process Compliance Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | IPC Designee / Clinical Supervisors | Monitor infection prevention process compliance indicators through quarterly direct observation audits and documentation review. Minimum indicators to monitor: (a) hand hygiene compliance rate (direct observation during supervisory visits); (b) PPE use compliance rate (gloves, mask as indicated — direct observation); (c) wound care sterile technique compliance rate (documentation audit); (d) sharps disposal compliance rate (direct observation); (e) infection-related patient education completion rate (documentation audit). | Quarterly; minimum one observation per clinician per quarter for indicators a–d. |
| 6.6.2 | IPC Designee | Report process compliance indicator results in the Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary. If any indicator falls below 90%, direct immediate targeted re-education and document in the Infection Tracking Log. | Monthly in surveillance summary; re-education within 7 calendar days of identification. |

### 6.7 External Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.7.1 | IPC Designee / Administrator | Comply with all California Department of Public Health (CDPH) mandatory reportable disease reporting requirements. The following conditions are reportable under California law and must be reported within the required timeframe (typically within 1–7 working days depending on the condition): (a) COVID-19; (b) Influenza A (novel); (c) Hepatitis A (acute); (d) Tuberculosis (active); (e) Legionellosis; (f) any other disease on the California CDPH reportable disease list when confirmed or suspected. | Per CDPH reporting timeframes by disease; see California Code of Regulations, Title 17. |
| 6.7.2 | IPC Designee | Maintain the External Infection Reporting Log (incorporated in Appendix B) documenting all external reports including disease type, date reported, reporting agency, and reporter's name. | At each external report; retained per CO-HP-007. |

### 6.8 Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.8.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Include infection surveillance summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. The summary must include: (a) quarterly infection rate by category with trend; (b) comparison to thresholds; (c) clusters identified and status; (d) corrective actions in progress; (e) staff occupational exposures; (f) external reports filed. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |
| 6.8.2 | Governing Body | Review infection data and direct corrective action if any infection rate exceeds threshold for 2 consecutive quarters without documented improvement. | At each quarterly meeting per GV-GB-001 Section 6.2.4. |

### 6.9 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cluster declared involving 3 or more patients. | IPC Designee notifies Administrator and Compliance Officer immediately. | Emergency QAPI Committee meeting within 48 hours. External reporting assessed. Governing Body notified within 72 hours. | Emergency meeting within 48 hours. |
| Patient hospitalized due to infection acquired during episode of care. | IPC Designee reports to QAPI Coordinator; triggers adverse event report per QA-AE-001. | Level 3 or 4 adverse event investigation per QA-AE-001. QAPI Committee reviews at next meeting. | Adverse event report within 24 hours. |
| Infection reporting rate drops significantly (>30% decline from prior quarter). | IPC Designee flags at QAPI Committee meeting. | Committee investigates potential under-reporting. Targeted re-education within 14 calendar days. | Within 14 days of identification. |
| Staff member develops work-related infection. | DON notifies Administrator and IPC Designee. | Workers' compensation initiated per HR-WM-004. Root cause analysis of occupational exposure. | Immediate; workers' compensation initiated within 24 hours. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Patient Infection Report Forms | Completed form per Section 6.2.1 (Appendix A). | Discovering Clinician | QAPI/IPC records (NOT in clinical record). | Within 24 hours of identification. |
| Infection Tracking Log | Comprehensive log per Section 6.1.1 (Appendix B). | IPC Designee | QAPI/IPC records. | Updated within 2 business days; retained 7 years. |
| Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary | Report per Section 6.4.1 (Appendix C). | IPC Designee | QAPI records. | Monthly; retained 7 years. |
| Cluster Declaration and Investigation | Documentation per Sections 6.5.1–6.5.4 (Appendix D). | IPC Designee / QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Within 7 days of declaration; retained 7 years. |
| Process Compliance Audit Results | Quarterly observation and documentation audit results (Appendix E). | IPC Designee / Clinical Supervisors | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| External Infection Reporting Log | Log per Section 6.7.2 (incorporated in Appendix B). | IPC Designee | Compliance records. | At each report; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| QAPI Committee infection review | Meeting minutes per Section 6.4.3. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Monthly; retained 7 years. |
| Quarterly Governing Body infection report | Section of quarterly QAPI Performance Report. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix F forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days of effective date, revision, or new assignment. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Infection surveillance system established and operational. | Review of Infection Tracking Log; Infection Report Forms on file. | System operational; no gaps in data collection. |
| Patient infections reported within 24 hours. | Audit of Report Form dates vs. event dates. | ≥ 95% of infections reported within 24 hours. |
| Monthly Infection Surveillance Summary completed. | Review of report files. | 12 reports per year; no gaps. |
| Summary presented at QAPI Committee monthly. | Review of committee minutes. | Infection review documented at 100% of monthly meetings. |
| Clusters investigated within 14 calendar days. | Review of cluster investigation files. | 100% of declared clusters have documented investigation within 14 days. |
| Process compliance audited quarterly. | Review of audit documentation. | 4 audits per year; all indicators reported. |
| Infection data reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| External reports filed per CDPH requirements. | Review of External Infection Reporting Log. | 100% of reportable conditions reported within required timeframes. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
CMS surveyors evaluating 42 CFR § 484.70 compliance will verify: (1) an ongoing infection surveillance program exists with documented data collection; (2) infection data is trended over time with comparison to thresholds; (3) the agency acts when infection rates are elevated; (4) clusters are investigated with documented findings and corrective actions; (5) infection prevention process compliance is monitored; (6) the QAPI Committee reviews infection data; (7) the Governing Body is informed of infection trends.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Infection surveillance is only documentation of individual events with no trending or analysis. | Surveyor cites lack of ongoing surveillance program. | Mandate monthly summaries with rolling trend data per Appendix C. |
| Clusters not identified because data is not analyzed. | Patterns missed; ongoing patient harm potential. | Enforce monthly trend analysis per Section 6.4.1; define cluster threshold. |
| Process compliance not monitored — only outcome rates. | Surveyor cites incomplete surveillance program. | Mandate quarterly process compliance audits per Section 6.6. |
| CDPH reportable conditions not reported. | State law violation; potential licensure action. | IPC Designee must know current CDPH reportable disease list; maintain External Reporting Log. |

## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All clinical staff shall receive infection surveillance training during initial orientation per HR-TA-005, covering: (a) how to identify and report a patient infection; (b) the Patient Infection Report Form and submission process; (c) the 24-hour reporting requirement; (d) how to report an occupational exposure; (e) the non-punitive reporting culture for infection reporting.
10.2 Annual refresher training on infection surveillance shall be included in mandatory annual training per HR-TD-001.
10.3 The IPC Designee shall receive specific training on infection surveillance methodology, cluster identification, rate calculation, and CDPH reporting requirements within 30 days of designation and annually thereafter.
10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix F) within 14 calendar days.
10.5 The QAPI Coordinator shall maintain a training tracking log for all infection surveillance training.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001). Only the most current approved version is valid.

# QA-SM-003 — Patient Satisfaction Survey & Analysis
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-SM-003 |
| Title | Patient Satisfaction Survey & Analysis |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | SM — Surveillance & Monitoring |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes the agency's patient satisfaction survey and analysis program at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., integrating the federally mandated Home Health Consumer Assessment of Healthcare Providers and Systems (HHCAHPS) survey with any supplemental satisfaction monitoring the agency conducts. Patient experience data reflects the patient's perspective on care quality and is a critical, non-clinical dimension of quality that complements clinical outcome monitoring. This policy ensures that HHCAHPS and supplemental data are systematically collected, analyzed, trended, and used to drive improvements in patient experience, communication, care delivery, and agency reputation. This policy satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program — patient experience data as a quality input) and CMS HHCAHPS program requirements.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All QAPI Committee members
All clinical staff whose conduct and communication affect patient experience
The CFO / Revenue Cycle Director (for Star Rating implications)
The Compliance Officer
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall comply with all CMS HHCAHPS program requirements including patient eligibility criteria, vendor contracting standards, and data submission timelines.
4.2 HHCAHPS results — including all reported measures, composite scores, and Star Ratings — shall be systematically monitored, trended, and reported through the QAPI program.
4.3 The agency shall supplement HHCAHPS data with agency-administered satisfaction monitoring (e.g., discharge satisfaction calls, post-discharge surveys) to obtain more timely, actionable feedback than the HHCAHPS quarterly release cycle provides.
4.4 Patient satisfaction data shall be a standing item on the monthly QAPI Committee agenda and shall be reported to the Governing Body quarterly.
4.5 When any HHCAHPS composite measure falls below the national average for 2 consecutive quarters, the QAPI Committee shall direct a documented root cause analysis and corrective action plan per QA-AE-003.
4.6 Patient satisfaction data shall be used to identify staff education needs, communication improvement opportunities, and service delivery changes.
4.7 All patient satisfaction data collection, storage, and analysis shall comply with HIPAA privacy and security requirements per CO-HP-001 and CO-HP-002.
4.8 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| HHCAHPS | Home Health Consumer Assessment of Healthcare Providers and Systems — the standardized, CMS-mandated survey administered by an approved CMS HHCAHPS vendor to a random sample of eligible home health patients to measure their experience with care. |
| HHCAHPS Vendor | A CMS-approved third-party organization contracted to administer HHCAHPS surveys on behalf of the agency in compliance with CMS HHCAHPS protocols. |
| HHCAHPS Composite Measure | A summary score derived from related HHCAHPS survey items, representing patient experience in a specific domain (e.g., Care of Patients, Communications Between Providers and Patients). |
| HHCAHPS Star Rating | A 1–5 star summary rating for patient experience published on Home Health Compare, derived from HHCAHPS composite scores. |
| Patient Satisfaction Survey (PSS) | An agency-administered supplemental survey instrument designed to collect timely feedback from patients and caregivers beyond the HHCAHPS sample. |
| Net Promoter Score (NPS) | A supplemental metric measuring the likelihood of patients to recommend the agency (optional; may be used as a supplemental indicator). |
| Case Mix Adjustment | The statistical adjustment applied by CMS to HHCAHPS scores to account for differences in patient populations across agencies, enabling fair comparison. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 HHCAHPS Program Compliance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | Administrator / QAPI Coordinator | Contract with a CMS-approved HHCAHPS vendor. The contract must specify: (a) survey administration methodology (telephone, mail, mixed); (b) sample size and frequency; (c) data reporting format and schedule; (d) compliance with CMS HHCAHPS protocols. Maintain the current vendor contract in agency records. | Prior to HHCAHPS program participation; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain a current HHCAHPS Patient Eligibility Tracking Log documenting all patients who meet CMS eligibility criteria for HHCAHPS sampling (Medicare or Medicaid patients discharged from a Medicare-certified episode during the calendar quarter). Submit the eligible patient list to the HHCAHPS vendor per the vendor's required submission schedule. | Per vendor-required submission schedule (typically quarterly). |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Monitor HHCAHPS vendor performance including: (a) survey response rate (target ≥ 30%); (b) timeliness of data reporting; (c) compliance with CMS HHCAHPS protocols. Report any vendor performance concerns to the Administrator. | Quarterly following each data release. |
| 6.1.4 | QAPI Coordinator | Upon receipt of HHCAHPS data from the vendor, download and store the data in the agency's secure QAPI records repository per CO-HP-002. | Within 5 business days of data receipt. |

### 6.2 HHCAHPS Data Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Upon receipt of each quarterly HHCAHPS data release, prepare the HHCAHPS Quarterly Analysis Report (Appendix B) documenting: (a) scores for all HHCAHPS composite measures and individual items; (b) comparison to prior quarter; (c) 12-month rolling trend for each composite; (d) comparison to national average (case mix adjusted); (e) Star Rating and trend; (f) items and composites below national average; (g) items showing improvement; (h) recommended actions. | Within 10 business days of data receipt. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Present the HHCAHPS Quarterly Analysis Report to the QAPI Committee at the next monthly meeting following data receipt. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following report completion. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Committee | Review HHCAHPS data and: (a) for composites at or above national average — acknowledge and identify sustaining practices; (b) for composites below national average — analyze root causes; (c) for composites below national average for 2 consecutive quarters — direct corrective action per Section 6.4; (d) document all analysis and decisions in committee minutes. | At QAPI Committee meeting where data is presented. |

### 6.3 Supplemental Patient Satisfaction Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | DON / Clinical Supervisors | Conduct Post-Discharge Satisfaction Calls for all discharged patients within 7 calendar days of discharge using the standardized call script (Appendix C). The call must address: (a) overall satisfaction with care; (b) communication experience with clinical staff; (c) whether care instructions were clear and understandable; (d) whether the patient would recommend the agency; (e) any complaints or unresolved concerns; (f) any quality or safety issues not previously reported. Document call results on the Post-Discharge Call Log (Appendix D). | Within 7 calendar days of discharge; goal 100% of discharges. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Compile monthly Post-Discharge Call Summary data including: (a) calls completed vs. discharges (completion rate); (b) distribution of overall satisfaction ratings; (c) "Would recommend" rate; (d) themes identified from open-ended responses; (e) complaints or concerns referred for follow-up. | Monthly; within 5 business days of month end. |
| 6.3.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Analyze supplemental satisfaction data for patterns and integrate with HHCAHPS findings to provide a more complete picture of patient experience. Present combined summary at each monthly QAPI Committee meeting. | Monthly at QAPI Committee meeting. |

### 6.4 Corrective Action for Below-Benchmark Performance

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Committee | When any HHCAHPS composite measure falls below the national average for 2 consecutive quarters, the committee shall: (a) identify the specific survey items driving the low composite score; (b) conduct a root cause analysis of the low-scoring items (possible causes: communication skills, timeliness, responsiveness, care quality, education effectiveness); (c) develop a targeted corrective action plan per QA-AE-003 with specific interventions, responsible parties, measurable targets, and timeline; (d) document analysis and plan in committee minutes. | At the committee meeting where the second consecutive below-average quarter is confirmed; CAP within 14 calendar days. |
| 6.4.2 | Assigned CAP Lead | Implement corrective interventions which may include: (a) staff communication skills training; (b) enhanced patient education protocols; (c) care coordination process improvements; (d) after-hours responsiveness improvements; (e) specific clinical discipline-targeted education. Report progress to QAPI Committee monthly. | Per CAP timeline; monthly progress reports. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Monitor HHCAHPS scores following intervention implementation to assess effectiveness. If no improvement is observed within 2 subsequent quarters, escalate to Governing Body for enhanced intervention or resource allocation. | Monitored each quarterly data release; escalation if no improvement in 6 months. |

### 6.5 Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Include patient satisfaction summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. Report must include: (a) most recent HHCAHPS composite scores with comparison to national averages; (b) Star Rating and trend; (c) below-average composites with corrective action status; (d) supplemental satisfaction data summary; (e) improvement recommendations. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |
| 6.5.2 | Governing Body | Review satisfaction data and direct corrective action if HHCAHPS Star Rating declines to 3 stars or below. | At each quarterly meeting per GV-GB-001 Section 6.2.4. |

### 6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HHCAHPS overall Star Rating falls to 3 stars or below. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator immediately; escalated to Governing Body. | Comprehensive improvement plan per QA-SM-004 Section 6.5; monthly Governing Body updates. | Report at next Governing Body meeting; plan within 30 days. |
| Post-discharge call completion rate falls below 70%. | QAPI Coordinator notifies DON. | DON assigns dedicated staff and establishes daily call tracking. | Corrective action within 7 calendar days. |
| Patient complaint escalated through post-discharge call requires immediate follow-up (safety, abuse, quality of care). | DON and Compliance Officer notified immediately. | Adverse event investigation per QA-AE-001 initiated if patient harm involved. Patient contacted by Administrator or DON within 24 hours. | Immediate notification; follow-up within 24 hours. |
| HHCAHPS vendor fails to submit data to CMS on schedule. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and Compliance Officer. | Administrator contacts vendor immediately. If vendor cannot resolve, alternative vendor contracting initiated. | Within 48 hours of identification. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HHCAHPS vendor contract | Current contract. | Administrator | Agency contracts file. | Maintained continuously. |
| HHCAHPS Patient Eligibility Tracking Log | Log per Section 6.1.2 (Appendix A). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| HHCAHPS raw data files | Quarterly data files from vendor. | QAPI Coordinator | Secure QAPI records repository. | Per quarterly release; retained 7 years. |
| HHCAHPS Quarterly Analysis Report | Report per Section 6.2.1 (Appendix B). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Post-Discharge Call Log | Completed log per Section 6.3.1 (Appendix D). | DON / Clinical Supervisors | QAPI records. | Monthly; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| Monthly Supplemental Satisfaction Summary | Summary per Section 6.3.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Monthly; retained 7 years. |
| QAPI Committee satisfaction review | Meeting minutes per Section 6.2.3. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Monthly; retained 7 years. |
| Corrective Action Plans for below-average composites | CAPs per Section 6.4.1 and QA-AE-003. | QAPI Coordinator / CAP Lead | QAPI records. | Per CAP trigger; retained 7 years. |
| Quarterly Governing Body satisfaction report | Section of QAPI Performance Report. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix E forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HHCAHPS vendor contracted. | Review of current vendor contract. | Active contract in force at all times. |
| HHCAHPS eligibility list submitted per vendor schedule. | Review of submission records. | 100% timely submissions per vendor contract. |
| HHCAHPS Quarterly Analysis Report prepared. | Review of report files. | 4 reports per year; completed within 10 business days of data receipt. |
| HHCAHPS data reviewed at QAPI Committee. | Review of committee minutes. | Data reviewed at each QAPI Committee meeting following quarterly release. |
| Post-discharge calls completed. | Review of Post-Discharge Call Log. | ≥ 80% of discharged patients contacted within 7 days. |
| Below-average composites have CAPs. | Review of CAP tracker and committee minutes. | 100% of composites below average for 2 consecutive quarters have documented CAP. |
| Patient satisfaction reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report. | Included in all quarterly reports. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask whether the agency participates in HHCAHPS; (2) verify an approved vendor is contracted; (3) verify HHCAHPS data is analyzed and used to drive improvement; (4) assess whether below-average scores have documented corrective action; (5) verify the Governing Body is informed; (6) look for evidence that patient experience data is integrated with QAPI.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No approved HHCAHPS vendor contracted. | CMS enforcement action; potential Medicare certification impact. | Contract vendor prior to HHCAHPS participation; review annually. |
| HHCAHPS data received but not analyzed or acted upon. | Scores decline without correction; surveyor cites data not used. | Mandate quarterly analysis report per Section 6.2.1. |
| Agency has no supplemental satisfaction monitoring between HHCAHPS releases. | Patient complaints not captured timely; missed improvement opportunities. | Mandate post-discharge calls per Section 6.3. |
| Below-average HHCAHPS scores have no corrective action. | Continued decline; negative impact on Star Rating and referrals. | Enforce CAP requirement per Section 6.4.1. |

## 9. References
References: CMS HHCAHPS program requirements; 42 CFR § 484.65; cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002, QA-PI-001, QA-PI-002, QA-PI-003, QA-AE-003, QA-SM-004, CO-HP-001, CO-HP-002, GV-GB-001, EN-TG-001.
## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 All clinical staff shall receive training on patient experience and communication skills during orientation and annually, including HHCAHPS survey domains most affected by clinician behavior (care of patients, communication, responsiveness).
10.2 Staff conducting post-discharge calls shall receive training on the standardized call script, active listening, complaint escalation, and documentation standards within 7 calendar days of assignment.
10.3 Policy acknowledgment within 14 calendar days per standard.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001).
