# QA-SM-004 — Home Health Compare & Star Rating Monitoring
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-SM-004 |
| Title | Home Health Compare & Star Rating Monitoring |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | SM — Surveillance & Monitoring |
| Classification Tier | ESSENTIAL |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy establishes systematic monitoring, analysis, and response procedures for the agency's Home Health Compare publicly reported quality measures and Star Ratings at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Home Health Compare data represents the agency's public quality profile — the information that patients, caregivers, referral sources, payers, and surveyors use to evaluate and compare the agency. Declining Star Ratings and below-average publicly reported quality measures have direct impacts on referral volume, competitive positioning, Governing Body accountability, and regulatory scrutiny. This policy ensures that the agency continuously monitors its public quality profile, understands the drivers of its ratings, and implements targeted improvement initiatives when ratings decline or fall below acceptable levels. This policy satisfies the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program data use including publicly reported measures) and supports the benchmarking requirements of QA-PI-003.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The QAPI Coordinator
The Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All QAPI Committee members
The CFO / Revenue Cycle Director
The Governing Body
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall actively and continuously monitor all publicly reported Home Health Compare quality measures and Star Ratings, with formal analysis conducted at each quarterly release of updated data.
4.2 Home Health Compare data shall be a standing component of the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002, the quarterly QAPI Performance Report per QA-PG-001, and the quarterly Governing Body report per GV-GB-001.
4.3 The agency shall maintain a Star Rating target of 4 stars or higher on both the Quality of Patient Care Star Rating and the Patient Survey Star Rating, and shall develop a documented improvement plan any time either rating falls below 4 stars.
4.4 For every publicly reported quality measure on which the agency performs below the national average for 2 consecutive reporting periods, the QAPI Committee shall direct a documented corrective action plan per QA-AE-003.
4.5 The Administrator and QAPI Coordinator shall verify the accuracy of the agency's Home Health Compare data within each quarterly release window and initiate the CMS data correction process if inaccuracies are identified.
4.6 Home Health Compare performance shall be reported publicly within the agency's marketing and referral source materials only as accurately as permitted by CMS guidelines, and shall not be misrepresented.
4.7 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Home Health Compare | The CMS public reporting website (now part of Care Compare at Medicare.gov) that displays quality measures, patient survey results, and Star Ratings for Medicare-certified home health agencies. |
| Quality of Patient Care Star Rating | A CMS-calculated composite Star Rating (1–5 stars) based on OASIS-derived outcome and process quality measures submitted to CMS. |
| Patient Survey Star Rating | A CMS-calculated composite Star Rating (1–5 stars) based on HHCAHPS results. |
| Home Health Quality Reporting Program (HH QRP) | The CMS program requiring home health agencies to submit quality measure data; non-participation results in a 2% payment penalty. |
| OASIS-Based Quality Measures | Quality measures derived from OASIS data submitted by the agency, reflecting patient outcomes and process compliance (e.g., improvement in ambulation, timely initiation of care). |
| Process Measures | Quality measures reflecting whether specific care processes were followed (e.g., influenza vaccination screening, depression screening). |
| Outcome Measures | Quality measures reflecting patient health status changes over the course of the episode (e.g., improvement in bathing, reduction in hospitalizations). |
| Star Rating Target | The agency's defined minimum acceptable Star Rating per Section 4.3 of this policy (4 stars or higher). |
| CMS Data Correction Window | The defined period following each data release during which agencies may submit corrections to reported data through the CMS iQIES system. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 Monitoring Infrastructure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Establish and maintain access to the following data systems used for Home Health Compare monitoring: (a) CMS Care Compare website (public-facing); (b) CMS iQIES (internal quality improvement reports, agency-specific data previews, data correction submissions); (c) CASPER (Certification and Survey Provider Enhanced Reports); (d) HHCAHPS vendor reports (per QA-SM-003). Document all access credentials and system URLs in the QAPI Data Source Inventory per QA-PG-001 Section 6.4.4. | At program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Monitor the CMS Home Health Quality Reporting Program (HH QRP) participation and submission requirements calendar. Ensure the agency submits all required OASIS data and public reporting data within required timeframes to avoid the 2% payment penalty. | Ongoing per CMS HH QRP calendar. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Subscribe to CMS mailing lists, CASPER notifications, and the agency's HHCAHPS vendor alerts to receive notification of each new data release and preview window. | At program establishment; maintain subscriptions continuously. |

### 6.2 Quarterly Data Review and Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Upon each quarterly CMS data release, download the agency's current publicly reported quality measure data from iQIES and Home Health Compare. Prepare the Home Health Compare Quarterly Analysis Report (Appendix A) within 10 business days of data release. The report must include: (a) all reported quality measures with agency score, national average, and state average; (b) Quality of Patient Care Star Rating — current and trend; (c) Patient Survey Star Rating — current and trend; (d) measures below national average; (e) measures below state average; (f) measures trending in the wrong direction (declining over 3+ periods, even if still above average); (g) comparison to agency Star Rating target (4 stars); (h) prior 4 quarters of data for trend analysis; (i) recommended actions. | Within 10 business days of each quarterly CMS data release. |
| 6.2.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Verify the accuracy of the agency's reported data during the CMS data preview window (prior to public release). If inaccurate data is identified, submit a correction request through iQIES within the correction window. Document all data verification activities and any corrections submitted. | During each CMS data preview window (per CMS calendar). |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Present the Home Health Compare Quarterly Analysis Report to the QAPI Committee at the next monthly meeting following data release. | At next QAPI Committee meeting following report completion. |

### 6.3 QAPI Committee Review and Action

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Committee | Review the Quarterly Analysis Report and take the following actions: (a) For measures at or above national average — acknowledge; identify and document sustaining practices; (b) For measures below national average but in improving trend — acknowledge improvement; continue monitoring; (c) For measures below national average with flat or declining trend — assign contributing factor analysis; determine if existing PIP or CAP addresses the issue; (d) For measures below national average for 2 consecutive periods — mandate new or enhanced CAP per QA-AE-003; (e) For either Star Rating below target (4 stars) — escalate to Governing Body and initiate comprehensive improvement plan per Section 6.4. Document all decisions and assigned actions in committee minutes. | At QAPI Committee meeting where report is presented. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Maintain a Home Health Compare Action Tracker (Appendix B) documenting all measures below threshold, associated corrective actions, assigned leads, deadlines, and current status. Present the tracker at each QAPI Committee meeting. | Updated quarterly following each data release; presented monthly. |

### 6.4 Comprehensive Star Rating Improvement Plan

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | When either Star Rating falls below 4 stars, prepare a Star Rating Improvement Plan (Appendix C) within 30 calendar days. The plan must: (a) identify each contributing quality measure that is below national average or declining; (b) analyze the root cause of underperformance for each measure (OASIS accuracy issue, care delivery issue, documentation issue, or systemic care quality issue); (c) assign specific, measurable improvement interventions for each measure; (d) define measurable targets (e.g., "improve improvement in ambulation rate from 64% to 72% within 2 quarters"); (e) identify responsible leads for each intervention; (f) establish a monitoring schedule; (g) define the timeline for achieving target Star Rating. | Within 30 calendar days of Star Rating falling below 4 stars. |
| 6.4.2 | Governing Body | Review and approve the Star Rating Improvement Plan at the next Governing Body meeting or at a special meeting if the rating has fallen to 3 stars or below. | At next Governing Body meeting; special meeting if ≤ 3 stars. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Report Star Rating Improvement Plan progress to the Governing Body at each quarterly meeting until the target rating is achieved and sustained for 2 consecutive quarters. | Quarterly until resolution. |

### 6.5 Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Include Home Health Compare summary in the quarterly QAPI Performance Report to the Governing Body per QA-PG-001 Section 6.3.1. Report must include: (a) current Quality of Patient Care Star Rating and trend; (b) current Patient Survey Star Rating and trend; (c) top 3 and bottom 3 publicly reported measures by relative agency performance; (d) measures with active corrective action plans and their status; (e) any data corrections submitted; (f) comparison to agency Star Rating target; (g) recommendations for Governing Body action. | Quarterly per GV-GB-001 schedule. |
| 6.5.2 | Governing Body | Review Home Health Compare data and direct corrective action. If either Star Rating falls to 3 stars or below and does not improve within 2 consecutive quarters with documented corrective action, the Governing Body shall convene a special session to evaluate whether additional resources, leadership action, or structural changes are required. | At each quarterly meeting. |

### 6.6 Benchmarking Integration

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Integrate Home Health Compare quarterly analysis into the agency's clinical outcome benchmarking process per QA-PI-003. Ensure that all publicly reported measures are included in the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002 with thresholds set at or above national averages. | At each quarterly release; integrated in annual QAPI plan development. |
| 6.6.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Annually, during QAPI plan development (per QA-PG-002), use the most recent Home Health Compare data to identify quality priority areas for the upcoming plan year and to set QAPI plan goals aligned with publicly reported measure improvement. | During annual planning cycle (October–December). |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Either Star Rating falls to 3 stars or below. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator and Governing Body immediately. | Star Rating Improvement Plan per Section 6.4.1 within 30 days; Governing Body oversight until 4 stars restored. | Plan within 30 days; monthly Governing Body updates. |
| Agency falls below national average on 5 or more publicly reported measures simultaneously. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to Administrator and Governing Body. | Comprehensive QAPI plan review and possible special Governing Body session to reassess resources and priorities. | Within 30 days of identification. |
| CMS data correction not submitted within the preview window for identified inaccurate data. | QAPI Coordinator notifies Administrator. | Administrator and Compliance Officer assess corrective options; document the gap and root cause. | Within 5 business days of window closing. |
| HH QRP participation penalty applied (2% payment reduction). | QAPI Coordinator notifies CFO and Administrator. | Immediate assessment of submission compliance; corrective action to restore full participation per CO-RA-001. | Within 5 business days of penalty notification. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Home Health Compare Quarterly Analysis Report | Report per Section 6.2.1 (Appendix A). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Home Health Compare Action Tracker | Tracker per Section 6.3.2 (Appendix B). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Updated quarterly; presented monthly. |
| Star Rating Improvement Plan | Plan per Section 6.4.1 (Appendix C). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; Governance file. | Within 30 days of trigger; retained 7 years. |
| Data verification records | Documentation of iQIES data review and any correction submissions per Section 6.2.2. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Per quarter; retained 7 years. |
| QAPI Committee HHC review | Meeting minutes per Section 6.3.1. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| Quarterly Governing Body HHC report | Section of quarterly QAPI Performance Report per Section 6.5.1. | QAPI Coordinator | Governance file; QAPI records. | Quarterly; retained 7 years. |
| HH QRP submission confirmation | Submission confirmation records from iQIES. | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Per submission; retained per CO-HP-007. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix D forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Quarterly analysis report prepared. | Review of report files. | 4 reports per year; completed within 10 business days of each release. |
| Data accuracy verified each quarter. | Review of data verification records and any iQIES correction submissions. | Verification completed each quarter; corrections submitted within the preview window. |
| HHC data reviewed at QAPI Committee. | Review of committee minutes. | HHC data reviewed at each quarterly QAPI Committee meeting following release. |
| Action Tracker maintained and current. | Review of tracker. | All below-average measures tracked; status current. |
| Star Rating Improvement Plan initiated when rating < 4 stars. | Review of plan documents. | Plan initiated within 30 days of rating falling below 4 stars. |
| HHC data reported to Governing Body quarterly. | Review of QAPI Performance Report. | Included in all quarterly reports. |
| HH QRP submissions completed per CMS calendar. | Review of iQIES submission confirmations. | 100% of required submissions completed; no payment penalty incurred. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask whether the agency monitors its publicly reported quality measures; (2) request evidence of quarterly analysis and QAPI Committee review; (3) verify the agency acts on below-average measures; (4) assess whether the Governing Body is aware of the agency's public quality profile; (5) cross-reference survey findings with publicly reported measures to assess consistency.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Agency does not monitor Home Health Compare between CMS surveys. | Declining measures not addressed until surveyor finds them. | Mandate quarterly analysis per Section 6.2.1. |
| Star Ratings declining but no QAPI-directed corrective action. | Continued decline; reputational and referral impact; survey risk. | Enforce corrective action triggers per Section 6.3.1. |
| Data inaccuracies not identified or corrected during preview window. | Incorrect publicly reported data; payment and reputation impact. | Mandate data verification per Section 6.2.2 each quarter. |
| Governing Body not aware of agency's Star Rating. | Governance failure; surveyor concern. | Include Star Ratings in every quarterly Governing Body report per Section 6.5.1. |

## 9. References
References: 42 CFR § 484.65; CMS Home Health Quality Reporting Program regulations and guidance; CMS Care Compare methodology documents; cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002, QA-PI-002, QA-PI-003, QA-AE-003, QA-SM-003, CL-OA-001–CL-OA-019, GV-GB-001, FN-BC-001, EN-TG-001.
## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 The QAPI Coordinator shall receive training on iQIES system access, CASPER report interpretation, HH QRP submission requirements, and Home Health Compare data methodology within 30 days of appointment and annually thereafter.
10.2 All QAPI Committee members shall receive annual training on Home Health Compare measure definitions, Star Rating calculation methodology, and quality improvement strategies for commonly below-average measures.
10.3 The DON and clinical supervisors shall receive annual training on the relationship between OASIS accuracy, clinical documentation quality, and publicly reported outcome measures.
10.4 Policy acknowledgment within 14 calendar days per standard.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001).

# QA-SM-005 — Policy Effectiveness Monitoring and Outcome Validation
## 1. Policy Header

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Policy ID | QA-SM-005 |
| Title | Policy Effectiveness Monitoring and Outcome Validation |
| Domain | QA — Quality Assurance & Performance Improvement |
| Subdomain | SM — Surveillance & Monitoring |
| Classification Tier | REQUIRED |
| Access Tier | Tier 2 — Restricted |
| Version | 6.0 |
| Effective Date | 2025-07-10 |
| Approved By | Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. |
| Policy Owner/Steward | QAPI Coordinator |
| Last Reviewed | 2025-07-10 |
| Next Review Date | 2026-07-10 |
| Review Cycle | Annual |
| Status | ACTIVE |
| Supersedes | N/A (Initial Version) |

## 2. Purpose
This policy requires Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. to systematically measure whether its policies are achieving their intended outcomes through defined compliance metrics, outcome indicators, and incident correlation analysis. Policies are the foundational mechanism through which the agency operationalizes regulatory requirements, best practices, and quality standards. A policy that exists in writing but does not change behavior or achieve measurable outcomes provides no quality assurance value and may create false compliance assurance. This policy ensures that the agency's policy framework is evaluated not merely for existence and acknowledgment, but for actual effectiveness — measured through outcomes data, compliance audit results, incident trends, and staff behavior indicators. This policy supports compliance with 42 CFR § 484.65 (QAPI program data-driven improvement) and the agency's enterprise policy governance framework per EN-TG-001.
## 3. Scope
This policy applies to:
The Compliance Officer
The QAPI Coordinator
The Administrator
The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager
All QAPI Committee members
All department heads and supervisors responsible for policy implementation in their domains
## 4. Policy Statements
4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a formal Policy Effectiveness Monitoring (PEM) program that evaluates whether REQUIRED and ESSENTIAL-tier policies are achieving their intended compliance and quality outcomes.
4.2 For each REQUIRED-tier policy, at least one defined outcome indicator shall be established that, when measured, provides evidence of whether the policy is being implemented as intended and achieving its stated purpose.
4.3 Policy effectiveness shall be evaluated through three complementary mechanisms: (a) compliance audit results per CO-RA-002 and CO-DC-002; (b) defined outcome indicators monitored through the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002; (c) incident and adverse event correlation analysis — identifying whether adverse events are linked to policy non-compliance.
4.4 Any REQUIRED-tier policy that demonstrates persistent non-compliance (below defined threshold for 2 or more consecutive audit cycles) or persistent adverse outcome correlation shall be reviewed for revision, enhanced communication, training improvement, or structural redesign.
4.5 The Policy Effectiveness Monitoring program shall be reported to the QAPI Committee semiannually and to the Governing Body annually as part of the year-end QAPI evaluation per QA-PG-002.
4.6 Only the most current approved version of this policy shall be considered valid.
## 5. Definitions

| Term | Definition |
| --- | --- |
| Policy Effectiveness Monitoring (PEM) | The systematic process of measuring whether a policy achieves its stated purpose through defined compliance metrics, outcome indicators, and incident correlation analysis. |
| Policy Outcome Indicator | A quantifiable measure that provides evidence of whether a policy is being implemented as intended and is achieving its stated quality or compliance goal. |
| Compliance Metric | A measurable indicator of adherence to a policy's specific requirements, typically derived from documentation audits, observation, or process tracking. |
| Incident Correlation Analysis | The process of examining adverse event data to determine whether incidents are linked to policy non-compliance, policy gaps, or policy design failures. |
| Policy Non-Compliance Pattern | A recurring failure to meet a policy's compliance metric across 2 or more consecutive audit cycles, indicating a systemic implementation problem rather than an isolated deviation. |
| Policy Revision Trigger | A defined condition — persistent non-compliance, adverse outcome correlation, regulatory change, or QAPI Committee directive — that initiates a formal policy review and potential revision process. |

## 6. Procedures
### 6.1 PEM Program Structure

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.1.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | Formally establish the Policy Effectiveness Monitoring program as a defined component of the QAPI and compliance programs. Document in the QAPI Program Description per QA-PG-001 Section 6.1.3. The program must define: (a) scope (minimum: all REQUIRED-tier policies); (b) evaluation mechanisms (audit, outcome indicator, incident correlation); (c) reporting frequency and format; (d) governance reporting structure. | At program establishment; reviewed annually. |
| 6.1.2 | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | For each REQUIRED-tier policy, complete the Policy Effectiveness Profile (Appendix A) documenting: (a) the policy's stated purpose; (b) the primary compliance metric (what should be measurably different because of this policy); (c) the defined outcome indicator (the quality or safety outcome the policy is intended to influence); (d) the data source for measurement; (e) the performance threshold; (f) the measurement frequency; (g) the incident correlation variable (what type of adverse event or incident would suggest this policy is not being followed). | Within 90 days of program establishment; updated annually or when a policy is revised. |
| 6.1.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Develop and maintain the Policy Effectiveness Master Registry (Appendix B) — a comprehensive log of all REQUIRED-tier policies with their associated compliance metrics, outcome indicators, and current effectiveness status. | Within 90 days of establishment; updated semiannually. |

### 6.2 Compliance Metric Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.2.1 | Compliance Officer | Ensure that the annual internal audit schedule per CO-RA-002 includes compliance audits for all REQUIRED-tier policies within the audit cycle. Each audit must generate a documented compliance rate for the policy's primary compliance metric. | Per annual audit schedule; all REQUIRED-tier policies audited at least annually. |
| 6.2.2 | Compliance Officer | Report compliance audit results to the QAPI Coordinator within 14 calendar days of audit completion. The report must include: (a) policy ID and title; (b) compliance metric measured; (c) sample size; (d) compliance rate; (e) prior cycle comparison; (f) items of non-compliance with root cause; (g) recommended corrective action. | Within 14 calendar days of each audit. |
| 6.2.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Integrate audit compliance data into the Policy Effectiveness Master Registry. Flag any policy with a compliance rate below the defined threshold for 2 consecutive audit cycles as "Non-Compliant Pattern — Policy Review Required." | Within 5 business days of audit report receipt. |

### 6.3 Outcome Indicator Monitoring

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.3.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Ensure that the policy outcome indicator for each REQUIRED-tier policy is included in the QAPI Quality Indicator Dashboard per QA-PI-002 or monitored through an equivalent tracking mechanism. Outcome indicator data must be reviewed at least quarterly. | Ongoing per QA-PI-002 monitoring schedule. |
| 6.3.2 | QAPI Coordinator | For each policy outcome indicator, assess quarterly whether the indicator trend is consistent with the policy being effectively implemented. If the indicator is declining or below threshold while the compliance metric appears adequate, investigate whether: (a) the compliance metric is measuring the right behavior; (b) the policy design itself is flawed; (c) the training approach is insufficient; (d) external factors are confounding the result. | Quarterly as part of QA-PI-002 dashboard review. |

### 6.4 Incident Correlation Analysis

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.4.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Semiannually, conduct an Incident Correlation Analysis reviewing all adverse events and near-misses reported in the prior 6 months per QA-AE-001. For each incident category, determine: (a) whether the incident could have been prevented if the related policy had been followed; (b) whether the related policy was in fact followed (review clinical documentation and staff accounts); (c) whether the policy exists, is current, and addresses the incident type; (d) whether staff were trained on the policy. | Semiannually (January and July). |
| 6.4.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Categorize each analyzed incident into one of the following: (a) Policy Followed — no policy-related contributing factor; (b) Policy Not Followed — the policy existed but was not adhered to; (c) Policy Gap — no policy addresses this incident type; (d) Policy Design Failure — the policy exists and was followed, but the policy itself did not prevent the outcome; (e) Training Gap — staff were unaware of or did not understand the applicable policy. | At time of semiannual analysis. |
| 6.4.3 | QAPI Coordinator | For incidents categorized as "Policy Not Followed," "Policy Gap," or "Policy Design Failure," recommend to the QAPI Committee: (a) targeted re-education (for "Not Followed" with isolated occurrence); (b) policy development (for "Policy Gap"); (c) policy revision (for "Design Failure" or "Not Followed" with pattern). | At next QAPI Committee meeting following semiannual analysis. |

### 6.5 Policy Revision Triggers and Process

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.5.1 | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | Initiate a formal policy review and potential revision process when any of the following Policy Revision Triggers are met: (a) Compliance rate below threshold for 2 consecutive audit cycles; (b) Outcome indicator below threshold for 3 consecutive monitoring periods despite adequate compliance audit rate; (c) Incident correlation analysis identifies 3 or more incidents in a 12-month period categorized as "Policy Not Followed" for the same policy; (d) Incident categorized as "Policy Design Failure" with patient harm at Level 3 or above (per QA-AE-001); (e) Regulatory change requiring policy update; (f) QAPI Committee or Governing Body directive for revision; (g) CMS survey finding related to the policy's subject matter. | Per trigger; initiation documented in Policy Effectiveness Master Registry within 7 calendar days. |
| 6.5.2 | Compliance Officer | Manage the policy revision process per EN-LC-001 (policy lifecycle management) and GV-PM-001 (policy development and approval process). Ensure the revised policy is: (a) reviewed against current regulatory requirements; (b) reviewed by subject matter experts; (c) approved by the Governing Body for REQUIRED-tier policies; (d) re-acknowledged by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date. | Per policy development timeline; Governing Body approval at next regular meeting or special meeting if urgent. |
| 6.5.3 | QAPI Coordinator | Following policy revision, re-establish baseline measurements for the revised policy's compliance metric and outcome indicator. Monitor for improvement within 2 audit cycles following the revision. If no improvement is observed, conduct deeper analysis of barriers to effective implementation. | Baseline re-established within 60 days of revised effective date; monitored for 2 audit cycles. |

### 6.6 QAPI Committee and Governing Body Reporting

| Step | Responsible Party | Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 6.6.1 | QAPI Coordinator | Present the Policy Effectiveness Monitoring Summary (Appendix C) to the QAPI Committee semiannually (at the first meeting following the January and July incident correlation analyses). The summary must include: (a) overall compliance metric status for all REQUIRED-tier policies; (b) policies with non-compliant patterns; (c) incident correlation findings; (d) policies with active revision triggers; (e) policies revised in the prior 6 months and effectiveness follow-up data; (f) recommended actions. | Semiannually at QAPI Committee; first meeting in February and August. |
| 6.6.2 | QAPI Coordinator | Include the annual Policy Effectiveness Monitoring Report (Appendix D) in the year-end QAPI evaluation submitted to the Governing Body per QA-PG-002 Section 6.4.1. | Annually as part of year-end QAPI evaluation. |
| 6.6.3 | Governing Body | Review the annual PEM report and direct: (a) additional resources for policies with persistent non-compliance; (b) structural or leadership changes if systemic policy adherence failures are identified; (c) revision priorities for the upcoming plan year. | At Q1 Governing Body meeting during year-end QAPI presentation. |

### 6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

| Condition | Escalation Path | Corrective Action | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| More than 25% of REQUIRED-tier policies in a single domain show non-compliant patterns simultaneously. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to Administrator and Compliance Officer. | Domain-level review; enhanced training; possible external audit. Reported to Governing Body at next meeting. | Domain review within 30 days. |
| Incident correlation identifies Policy Design Failure with sentinel event. | QAPI Coordinator escalates to Administrator and Governing Body immediately. | Immediate policy suspension if risk of further harm exists; urgent revision per Governing Body directive; special Governing Body meeting if needed. | Within 48 hours of identification. |
| PEM program reports not generated for a semiannual cycle. | Compliance Officer notifies Administrator. | Reports generated and presented retroactively. Root cause of gap addressed per QA-AE-003. Reported to Governing Body. | Retroactive reports within 30 days; root cause addressed within 14 days. |

## 7. Documentation Requirements

| Requirement | Document / Record | Responsible Party | Location | Timeframe |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Effectiveness Profiles | Completed profile per Section 6.1.2 (Appendix A) for each REQUIRED-tier policy. | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | QAPI records; Compliance records. | Within 90 days of establishment; updated annually. |
| Policy Effectiveness Master Registry | Comprehensive registry per Section 6.1.3 (Appendix B). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Within 90 days; updated semiannually; retained 7 years. |
| Compliance audit reports | Audit reports per Section 6.2.2 (per CO-RA-002). | Compliance Officer | Compliance records. | Within 14 days of each audit; retained 7 years. |
| Semiannual Incident Correlation Analysis | Analysis per Section 6.4.1 (incorporated in Appendix C). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Semiannually; retained 7 years. |
| Policy Effectiveness Monitoring Summary | Semiannual summary per Section 6.6.1 (Appendix C). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records. | Semiannually; retained 7 years. |
| Annual Policy Effectiveness Monitoring Report | Annual report per Section 6.6.2 (Appendix D). | QAPI Coordinator | QAPI records; Governance file. | Annually with year-end QAPI evaluation. |
| Policy revision trigger documentation | Documentation of trigger, date, and initiation per Section 6.5.1. | QAPI Coordinator / Compliance Officer | QAPI records; Policy lifecycle file. | Within 7 days of trigger. |
| Policy acknowledgments | Signed Appendix E forms. | Each individual; QAPI Coordinator. | Policy acknowledgment file. | Within 14 calendar days. |

## 8. Compliance Monitoring
### 8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

| Compliance Indicator | Measurement Method | Acceptable Standard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Policy Effectiveness Profile completed for all REQUIRED-tier policies. | Review of profiles in QAPI records. | 100% of REQUIRED-tier policies have current profile. |
| Policy Effectiveness Master Registry maintained. | Review of registry currency. | Updated within 30 days of each semiannual PEM cycle. |
| Compliance audits completed for all REQUIRED-tier policies annually. | Review of audit schedule vs. audit reports. | 100% of REQUIRED-tier policies audited within the annual cycle. |
| Semiannual incident correlation analysis completed. | Review of analysis reports. | 2 analyses per year; completed in February and August. |
| Policies with non-compliant patterns have documented revision triggers. | Review of Master Registry and QAPI Committee minutes. | 100% of policies with 2+ consecutive non-compliant audit cycles have documented revision trigger. |
| Semiannual PEM summary presented to QAPI Committee. | Review of committee minutes. | 2 presentations per year; documented in minutes. |
| Annual PEM report included in Governing Body year-end review. | Review of year-end QAPI evaluation and Governing Body minutes. | Included in annual Q1 Governing Body presentation. |

### 8.2 Surveyor Expectations
Surveyors will: (1) ask whether the agency monitors whether its policies are actually followed and effective; (2) look for evidence that the agency uses audit and outcome data to evaluate policies, not just to document them; (3) assess whether policy non-compliance is identified and addressed systematically; (4) verify that the QAPI program integrates policy compliance as a data input; (5) look for evidence that the Governing Body is aware of policy effectiveness.
### 8.3 Common Failure Points

| Failure Point | Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Agency has comprehensive policies but does not measure whether they are followed. | False compliance assurance; surveyor finds practice diverging from policy. | Mandate compliance metrics per Section 6.2. |
| Adverse events occur but are not analyzed for policy non-compliance contributing factors. | Root causes not identified; policy gaps not closed. | Mandate incident correlation analysis per Section 6.4. |
| Policy non-compliance identified in audit but policy is not revised or training not enhanced. | Pattern of non-compliance persists indefinitely. | Enforce policy revision triggers per Section 6.5.1. |
| PEM program exists but is never reported to the Governing Body. | Governance failure; systemic policy problems unseen at the top. | Mandate annual Governing Body reporting per Section 6.6.2. |

## 9. References
References: 42 CFR § 484.65; cross-references QA-PG-001, QA-PG-002, QA-PI-002, QA-PI-004, QA-AE-001, QA-AE-003, CO-RA-002, CO-DC-002, GV-PM-001, GV-PM-002, EN-LC-001, EN-LC-004, EN-TG-001, GV-GB-001.
## 10. Training Requirements
10.1 The QAPI Coordinator and Compliance Officer shall receive training on policy effectiveness measurement methodology, compliance metric design, and incident correlation analysis within 30 days of assignment.
10.2 All department heads and supervisors shall receive training on their responsibility for policy implementation in their domain and how compliance metrics are measured within 14 calendar days of policy effective date.
10.3 Policy acknowledgment within 14 calendar days per standard.
## 11. Version Control
Per standard enterprise version control (EN-LC-001).
